## DG-16. Singular Cochains, Mayer–Vietoris, and Smooth Singular Comparison

**A page:** `singular-cochains-mayer-vietoris-and-smooth-singular-comparison`
**B page:** `singular-and-smooth-singular-cochain-examples`
**Requires:** DG-7, DG-13, DG-15; AT-2 `singular-chains-and-singular-homology`,
AT-3 `relative-homology-excision-and-mayer-vietoris`, and AT-6
`singular-cohomology-and-coefficient-theorems`; published `free-modules-and-exact-sequences`
and `homotopy-and-homotopy-equivalence`;
HA pages `chain-complexes-and-homology`, `chain-homotopy-and-the-homotopy-category`,
and `long-exact-sequences-in-homology`.

AT-2/AT-3/AT-6 supply the ordinary singular simplex, chain, boundary, prism,
subdivision, cover-small, cochain, functoriality, and Mayer--Vietoris apparatus.
This page specialises those objects to real coefficients, builds the explicit
two-open cochain interface needed by de Rham, and develops smooth singular
chains and the smooth/continuous comparison. Relative homology, general
excision, cellular/simplicial homology, universal coefficients, and the cup
product remain AT-owned. Real singular cohomology is still treated as the
cohomology of the real singular **cochain** complex, not silently identified
with $\operatorname{Hom}(H_*(-),\mathbb R)$.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Singular simplices, chains, and cochains**

1. `def-continuous-singular-simplex-and-real-singular-chain-group` —
   **definition** `[LN]`; specialise AT-2's
   `def-singular-simplex-and-singular-chain-group-with-coefficients` to
   $\mathbb R$, so $C_k(X;\mathbb R)$ is the free real vector space on
   continuous maps $\Delta^k\to X$.
2. `def-real-singular-chain-complex` — **definition** `[LN]`; the
   real-coefficient instance of AT-2's singular chain complex and HA's general
   chain-complex definition.
3. `prop-singular-chains-are-covariantly-functorial` — **proposition** `[LL]`;
   the real-coefficient specialisation of AT-2's
   `prop-singular-chains-and-homology-are-covariantly-functorial`.
4. `def-real-singular-cochain-complex` — **definition** `[LN]`; specialise
   AT-6's `def-singular-cochain-complex-with-coefficients` to
   $C^k(X;\mathbb R)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb R}(C_k(X;\mathbb R),\mathbb R)$.
5. `def-real-singular-cohomology` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $H^k_{\mathrm{sing}}(X;\mathbb R)=H^k(C^\bullet(X;\mathbb R))$, the
   real-coefficient specialisation of AT-6 and HA's quotient definition.
6. `cor-singular-cohomology-is-homotopy-invariant` — **corollary** `[LA]`;
   apply AT-2's prism/chain-homotopy formula through AT-6's cochain functor.

**Barycentric subdivision and cover-small chains**

AT-3 supplies the subdivision operator, its prism homotopy, mesh decay,
eventual cover-smallness for each finite chain, and the cover-small inclusion
as a chain-homotopy equivalence. Items 7--11 use those exact results; DG does
not propose a second subdivision or cover-small package.

**Singular Mayer–Vietoris without injective coefficients**

7. `thm-short-exact-two-open-cover-small-singular-chain-sequence` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; specialise AT-3's cover-small chain construction to
   the two-open cover and real coefficients:
    \[
      0\to C_*(U\cap V)\xrightarrow{c\mapsto(-c,c)}
      C_*(U)\oplus C_*(V)\xrightarrow{(a,b)\mapsto a+b}
      C_*^{\{U,V\}}(X)\to0.
    \]
8. `lem-canonical-extension-by-zero-of-a-singular-cochain-on-a-simplex-basis` —
   **lemma** `[LA]`; extend a function on basis simplices in $U\cap V$ by
   zero on the other basis simplices of $U$. No injectivity theorem or basis
   choice is involved because the basis is supplied.
9. `thm-short-exact-two-open-singular-cochain-mayer-vietoris-sequence` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; dualize item 7 and print the actual sequence
    \[
      0\longrightarrow
      \operatorname{Hom}(C_*^{\{U,V\}}(X),\mathbb R)
      \longrightarrow C^*(U)\oplus C^*(V)
      \xrightarrow{(\varphi,\psi)\mapsto
        \psi|_{U\cap V}-\varphi|_{U\cap V}}
      C^*(U\cap V)\longrightarrow0.
    \]
    Item 8 proves the only nonformal surjectivity step. The first term is
    **not** replaced at chain level by $C^*(X)$.
10. `thm-mayer-vietoris-sequence-in-real-singular-cohomology` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; identify this explicit sequence with the real-coefficient instance
    of AT-6's `thm-mayer-vietoris-sequence-in-singular-cohomology`, using
    AT-3's `thm-cover-small-inclusion-is-a-chain-homotopy-equivalence`.
11. `thm-naturality-of-singular-mayer-vietoris-connectors` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; specialise AT-6 and HA connector naturality and display the
    cover-preserving map hypothesis.

**Smooth singular chains and comparison**

12. `def-smooth-singular-simplex` — **definition** `[LN]`; a map
    $\sigma:\Delta^k\to M$ extending smoothly to a neighbourhood of
    $\Delta^k$ in its affine span.
13. `def-smooth-singular-chain-and-cochain-complexes` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    faces of a smooth simplex are smooth, so this is a subcomplex.
14. `prop-smooth-singular-chains-and-cochains-are-functorial-for-smooth-maps` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
15. `prop-barycentric-subdivision-and-prism-preserve-smooth-singular-chains` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; the AT-2/AT-3 reparametrising maps are affine
    smooth.
16. `thm-smooth-singular-mayer-vietoris-sequence` — **theorem** `[LA]`; repeat
    AT-3's cover-small construction and items 7–11 inside the smooth
    subcomplex, not by assuming the continuous result automatically restricts.
17. `lem-relative-smoothing-of-a-continuous-simplex-along-its-faces` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`. If all proper faces have compatible prescribed smooth
    approximations, approximate the simplex relative to a collar of its
    boundary and connect it by a relative homotopy.
18. `thm-smooth-singular-chains-compute-singular-homology` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    apply item 17 inductively to the finitely many simplices of a cycle or
    bounding chain. No simultaneous smoothing of the proper class of all
    singular simplices is selected.
19. `def-restriction-from-continuous-to-smooth-singular-cochains` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; precompose a cochain with the inclusion of smooth
    chains.
20. `prop-smooth-continuous-singular-cohomology-comparison-is-an-isomorphism-on-convex-coordinate-domains` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; on any coordinate domain carried to a nonempty
    convex Euclidean open set, the common affine contraction gives explicit
    prism contractions of both complexes. This includes rational boxes and
    all their nonempty finite intersections.
21. `lem-de-rham-and-singular-cohomology-respect-countable-disjoint-unions` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`. For a supplied countable family $(M_i)$, the de Rham,
    smooth-singular, and continuous-singular cochain complexes on
    $\bigsqcup_iM_i$ are the products of the component complexes. Under
    $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$, their cohomologies are the corresponding products,
    and each comparison map is the product of the component comparison maps.
    The proof identifies kernels componentwise and records exactly where
    representatives of countably many componentwise boundaries are chosen.
22. `thm-countable-mayer-vietoris-open-set-principle` — **theorem** `[LA]`.
    A natural transformation between contravariant cohomology functors that
    commutes with two-open MV and disjoint unions is first proved to be an
    isomorphism on every open subset of $\mathbb R^n$: use a fixed countable
    basis of rational open boxes, closed under finite intersections, and the
    exhaustion plus odd/even locally finite union argument. Next cover the
    manifold by a countable coordinate cover. Every finite intersection is an
    open subset of its first chart, so the Euclidean stage applies; the same
    MV argument then proves the transformation is an isomorphism on the
    manifold. No unjustified claim that intersections of coordinate balls are
    convex is made.
23. `thm-smooth-and-continuous-real-singular-cohomology-agree` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; apply item 22 to item 19 using items 10, 16, 20, and 21. No universal
    coefficient or injective-$\mathbb R$ argument is used. LANDMARK.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-the-singular-boundary-of-a-simplex-is-the-unsigned-sum-of-its-faces` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; AT-2's alternating boundary signs make
  $\partial^2=0$.
- `fs-a-singular-cochain-is-a-finite-linear-combination-of-singular-simplices` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; chains have finite support, while a cochain is
  an arbitrary linear functional.
- `fs-dualizing-every-short-exact-sequence-is-automatically-exact-in-zf` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; item 8 supplies the exact extension actually
  needed.
- `fs-one-fixed-number-of-barycentric-subdivisions-makes-every-singular-simplex-cover-small` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; AT-3 proves that the depth depends on the simplex
  or finite chain.
- `fs-every-continuous-singular-simplex-is-smooth` — **false statement**
  `[LL]`.
- `fs-a-homology-isomorphism-of-chain-complexes-automatically-gives-a-cohomology-isomorphism-after-dualizing-without-further-machinery` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; AT-3's chain-homotopy equivalence or items
  19–23 are required.

### B page — `singular-and-smooth-singular-cochain-examples`

1. `ex-a-finite-chain-needing-different-subdivision-depths-on-its-simplices` —
   **example** `[AA]`.
2. `ex-canonical-zero-extension-of-an-overlap-cochain` — **example** `[LL]`.
3. `ex-smooth-singular-simplices-in-a-coordinate-ball` — **example** `[LL]`.
4. `cex-a-continuous-nowhere-differentiable-singular-one-simplex` —
   **counterexample** `[LL]`.
5. `ex-relative-smoothing-fixes-the-endpoints-of-a-path` — **example** `[LA]`.
6. `ex-singular-cohomology-of-a-point-from-the-cochain-complex` —
    **example** `[LL]`.
7. `cex-hom-of-homology-is-not-used-as-the-definition-of-singular-cohomology` —
    **counterexample/reminder** `[LN]`; it records the missing universal-
    coefficient/injectivity step, without asserting a false finite-dimensional
    identification.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Hatcher, *Algebraic Topology*, §2.1 from “Singular Homology” through
  “Exact Sequences and Excision” (pp. 108–132), §2.2 “Mayer–Vietoris
  Sequences” (pp. 149–150), and §3.1 headings “Cohomology of Spaces” and
  “Mayer–Vietoris Sequences” (pp. 197–204).** Primary open textbook source.
  Thm. 2.10 and Prop. 2.12 back prism homotopy; Prop. 2.21 backs cover-small
  chains. General relative homology/excision and simplicial comparison are
  out of scope; universal coefficients are deliberately not used.
- **Arvind Nair, *Topology II*, §6.13 “Singular cohomology and sheaf
  cohomology,” focused
  paragraph in the proof of Thm. 6.13.3 (p. 84).** The recalled theorem of
  small chains explicitly states that
  $C_*^{\mathcal U}(X)\hookrightarrow C_*(X)$ is a chain-homotopy
  equivalence; AT-3 owns that exact theorem and its recursive
  least-subdivision-depth/compatible-prism construction. DG cites it rather
  than weakening the statement to a homology isomorphism.
- **Lee, *Introduction to Smooth Manifolds*, 2nd ed., Ch. 18 “The de Rham
  Theorem,” all four substantive headings, especially Thms. 18.4/18.6
  (singular Mayer–Vietoris), 18.7 (smooth comparison), 18.12 (chain Stokes),
  Prop. 18.13, and Thm. 18.14.** Items 1–23 retain the real-coefficient
  specialisation and smooth-singular comparison material not owned by AT;
  chain integration and the final comparison are disposed to DG-17. Lee's
  shortcut defining real cohomology as
  $\operatorname{Hom}(H_p,\mathbb R)$ and invoking injectivity is explicitly
  rejected in favour of AT-6 and items 4–10.
- **Merry, *Differential Geometry*, Lecture 25 “Smooth Singular Cubes” and
  Lecture 27 after Cor. 27.10.** The cubical construction is an alternative
  `out-of-scope` route because it would require general corners; its comparison
  and de Rham results are statement-level controls only because their proofs
  use Zorn/filtered-limit sketches.
- **Park, “Proof of de Rham's Theorem,” §§1–2 “Introduction” and “Smooth
  Singular Homology,” Lem. 2.1 and Thms. 2.2/2.4.** Focused independent support
  for relative face smoothing. Section 3 is disposed to DG-17.
- **van der Vorst, *De Rham Cohomology*, §7 “Chains and cohomologies” and
  §10 through “smooth simplices.”** Secondary lecture-note control; its
  remaining de Rham-map headings go to DG-17.

### Proof strategy and traps

AT-2/AT-3 supply subdivision as a chain construction, its prism homotopy, mesh
decay, eventual cover-smallness, and the resulting chain-homotopy equivalence.
DG does not reprove those facts. In the two-open real-cochain presentation,
surjectivity after dualising is proved on the **supplied singular-simplex
basis** by item 8's extension by zero, so the sentence “$\mathbb R$ is
injective” and its set-theoretic cost never appear.

Relative smoothing proceeds dimension by dimension and only over the finite
support of a particular chain. Smooth versus continuous **cohomology** is then
proved by the natural restriction map, local contractions, both MV sequences,
and the countable open-set principle—not by turning the finite smoothing
argument into a global choice of one smoothing for every simplex.

**Well-definedness discharged:** ordinary face, boundary, prism, subdivision,
cover-small, and cochain facts are cited to AT-2/AT-3/AT-6; real-coefficient
specialisation and basis extension are items 1–11; neighbourhood extension in
the definition of a smooth simplex and compatibility on faces are items 12–18;
the natural cohomology comparison and countable-union globalization are items
19–23.
**Choice:** AT's choice ledger governs ordinary singular chains. Basiswise zero
extension here is ZF. Relative smoothing inherits DG-7's smooth approximation
boundary but makes no global selection. Item 21 and the countable open-set
principle need at most the same $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$ already charged by DG-2;
the charge is the simultaneous choice of representatives for countably many
componentwise boundaries, not full AC or an injective-module argument. HA's
own ledger is cited unchanged.
**Unresolved seam:** HA-1/HA-2/HA-4 and the named AT-2/AT-3/AT-6 interfaces
must be authored first; no unnamed algebraic-topology result is assumed.

## DG-17. The de Rham Theorem, Compact-Support Top Cohomology, and Degree

**A page:** `the-de-rham-theorem-and-degree`
**B page:** `de-rham-comparison-and-degree-examples`
**Requires:** DG-6, DG-7, and DG-13–DG-16; AT-6
`singular-cohomology-and-coefficient-theorems`, AT-7
`cup-cap-cross-products-and-cohomology-rings`, and AT-8
`orientations-poincare-lefschetz-and-alexander-duality`; HA pages
`chain-complexes-and-homology`, `chain-homotopy-and-the-homotopy-category`,
and `long-exact-sequences-in-homology`; published
`free-modules-and-exact-sequences` (including
`thm-five-lemma-for-modules`).

AT-6/AT-7 supply singular cohomology, the cup product, and its graded-
commutative ring structure; DG proves that de Rham integration identifies
wedge with that cup product, so the landmark comparison is a natural graded-
algebra isomorphism. AT-6 supplies the Kronecker pairing, while AT-8 supplies
fundamental classes and homological degree for closed oriented manifolds; DG
proves agreement with the regular-value/compact-support definition and retains
the proper noncompact extension. The second half is not a surrogate for
Poincaré duality.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Integration on smooth singular simplices**

1. `def-standard-orientation-of-the-affine-simplex` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   the ordered vertices induce the orientation, and the $i$th face carries
   the sign $(-1)^i$.
2. `def-integral-of-a-form-over-a-smooth-singular-simplex` — **definition**
   `[LN]`: $\int_\sigma\omega=\int_{\Delta^k}\sigma^*\omega$. For $k\geq1$,
   the right side is the published Riemann integral of a smooth coefficient
   over the compact Jordan simplex in its affine span. For $k=0$, set
   $\int_\sigma f=f(\sigma(v_0))$, equivalently DG-14's positively oriented
   point integral.
3. `lem-simplex-integrals-are-independent-of-affine-coordinate-identification` —
   **lemma** `[LA]`; orientation-preserving affine changes use the published
   compact-Jordan change of variables.
4. `lem-stokes-theorem-for-the-standard-simplex` — **lemma** `[LA]`, for
   $k\geq1$:
   $\int_{\Delta^k}d\eta=
   \sum_i(-1)^i\int_{\Delta^{k-1}}\delta_i^*\eta$. Prove this polytope-specific
   formula directly by slicing/FTC; no general manifold-with-corners theory is
   imported.
5. `thm-stokes-theorem-for-smooth-singular-chains` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
   $\int_c d\omega=\int_{\partial c}\omega$, by item 4 and linearity.
6. `def-de-rham-integration-cochain-map` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $I_M(\omega)(\sigma)=\int_\sigma\omega$ on smooth singular simplices.
7. `thm-de-rham-integration-is-a-cochain-map` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
   $\delta I_M=I_Md$, exactly item 5 in cochain notation.
8. `thm-the-de-rham-map-on-cohomology-is-well-defined` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
   cite HA's induced-cohomology theorem and also print the representative
   calculation.
9. `prop-naturality-of-the-de-rham-map` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
   $I_M(F^*\omega)=F^*I_N(\omega)$ for every smooth $F:M\to N$.

**Local-to-global comparison**

10. `lem-the-de-rham-map-is-an-isomorphism-on-convex-coordinate-domains` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; on a coordinate domain carried to a nonempty convex
    Euclidean open set, degree zero is evaluation on the one component and
    positive degrees vanish by the Poincaré lemma and the smooth singular
    affine-prism contraction.
11. `lem-the-de-rham-and-smooth-singular-mayer-vietoris-diagram-commutes-away-from-connectors` —
    **lemma** `[LL]`; restriction and difference squares follow from
    naturality.
12. `lem-the-de-rham-map-commutes-with-mayer-vietoris-connectors` — **lemma**
    `[LA]`. Use the explicit partition lift on forms and the basiswise
    singular lift, then show their difference is a coboundary with the chosen
    sign. This is a separate proof obligation, not “by naturality.”
13. `thm-the-de-rham-map-is-an-isomorphism-on-a-two-open-union-when-it-is-on-the-pieces-and-intersection` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; apply the published five lemma to the two long exact
    sequences.
14. `thm-de-rham-theorem-for-smooth-singular-cohomology` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    the two-stage Euclidean-open-set/manifold Mayer–Vietoris principle from
    DG-16 item 22, together with items 10–13 above, gives
    $H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)\cong H^k_{\mathrm{sing},\infty}(M;\mathbb R)$.
15. `thm-de-rham-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Compose item 14 with DG-16's
    natural smooth/continuous singular comparison and prove, using AT-7's
    `def-singular-cohomology-ring`, that simplex integration sends wedge to
    cup up to the standard natural cochain homotopy. Thus
    \[
       H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)\;\cong\;
       H^k_{\mathrm{sing}}(M;\mathbb R)
    \]
    naturally in every smooth manifold $M$ as a graded-algebra isomorphism;
    AT-7's `thm-singular-cohomology-is-graded-commutative` then agrees with
    DG-11's wedge signs. LANDMARK.
16. `cor-de-rham-cohomology-depends-only-on-the-underlying-homotopy-type` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; agrees with DG-15's direct homotopy proof.
17. `rem-ring-form-of-the-de-rham-theorem-needs-the-singular-cup-product` —
    **remark** `[LN]`; the former dependency is now discharged by AT-7:
    item 15 proves
    $I([\alpha\wedge\beta])=I[\alpha]\smile I[\beta]$ using its exact cup-ring
    interface. Compactness is not a hypothesis.

**Compact-support top cohomology**

18. `def-compactly-supported-de-rham-cochain-complex-and-cohomology` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; $d$ preserves support, so
    $(\Omega_c^\bullet(M),d)$ is a cochain complex.
19. `prop-proper-smooth-maps-pull-back-compactly-supported-forms` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; $\operatorname{supp}F^*\omega\subseteq
    F^{-1}(\operatorname{supp}\omega)$ is compact when $F$ is proper.
20. `prop-compactly-supported-de-rham-cohomology-is-contravariant-for-proper-smooth-maps` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`; arbitrary smooth maps are not claimed.
21. `thm-integration-descends-to-compactly-supported-top-de-rham-cohomology` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`: Stokes gives
    $\int_M:H_c^n(M)\to\mathbb R$ for an oriented boundaryless $n$-manifold.
22. `lem-zero-integral-compactly-supported-top-forms-on-euclidean-space-have-compactly-supported-primitives` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; construct the primitive coordinate by coordinate with
    correcting bump terms so support remains compact.
23. `lem-compactly-supported-top-cohomology-propagates-across-overlapping-oriented-coordinate-balls` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; exact transfer forms move normalized bumps between
    overlapping balls.
24. `thm-integration-is-an-isomorphism-on-top-compactly-supported-de-rham-cohomology` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`. If $M^n$ is nonempty, connected, oriented, and boundaryless,
    $\int_M:H_c^n(M)\xrightarrow\sim\mathbb R$. LANDMARK.
25. `cor-top-de-rham-cohomology-of-a-closed-connected-oriented-manifold-is-real` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; for a closed nonempty connected oriented manifold,
    compactness makes ordinary and compact supports coincide.

**Degree of proper maps**

26. `def-degree-of-a-proper-smooth-map-by-compact-support-cohomology` —
    **definition** `[LN]`. For nonempty connected oriented boundaryless
    $n$-manifolds,
    the scalar $\deg F$ is determined by
    $\int_MF^*\omega=(\deg F)\int_N\omega$ for all
    $\omega\in\Omega_c^n(N)$. For closed manifolds, the comparison target is
    AT-8's `def-degree-of-a-map-between-oriented-closed-manifolds`.
27. `lem-degree-is-well-defined-and-independent-of-the-normalized-top-form` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; use the one-dimensionality in item 24.
28. `def-local-orientation-sign-of-a-regular-preimage` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\operatorname{sgn}(dF_p)=\pm1$. In positive dimension this is the sign
    of the Jacobian determinant relative to the supplied orientations; in
    dimension zero it compares the source and target determinant-line rays
    directly, since the $0\times0$ determinant is always $+1$.
29. `thm-regular-value-formula-for-degree` — **theorem** `[LA]`. If $y$ is a
    regular value of the proper map $F$, then $F^{-1}(y)$ is finite and
    $\deg F=\sum_{p\in F^{-1}(y)}\operatorname{sgn}(dF_p)\in\mathbb Z$.
    When source and target are closed, evaluate item 15 against AT-8's
    `def-fundamental-class-of-a-compact-oriented-manifold` through AT-6's
    `def-kronecker-evaluation-pairing` to prove that this equals AT-8's
    homological degree.
30. `cor-degree-is-an-integer-and-independent-of-the-regular-value` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
31. `prop-degree-is-multiplicative-under-composition` — **proposition** `[LA]`;
    on closed manifolds it agrees with AT-8's
    `prop-manifold-degree-is-functorial-and-detected-by-top-cohomology`, and
    the compact-support pullback proof supplies the proper-map extension.
32. `prop-degree-of-an-orientation-preserving-or-reversing-diffeomorphism` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`: $+1$ or $-1$.
33. `thm-degree-is-invariant-under-proper-smooth-homotopy` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; the combined map must be proper, a hypothesis not inferred from
    proper endpoints.
34. `cor-a-nonzero-degree-map-to-a-connected-manifold-is-surjective` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; a missed point supports a normalized form whose
    pullback is zero.
35. `prop-degree-of-the-antipodal-map-on-the-sphere` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $(-1)^{n+1}$ on $S^n$ for $n\ge1$. The page's degree definition
    assumes connected source and target, so it does not apply this slogan to
    the disconnected zero-sphere.
36. `prop-degree-of-the-power-map-on-the-circle` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $z\mapsto z^m$ has degree $m$ for $m\in\mathbb Z$.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-the-de-rham-map-is-a-cochain-map-without-stokes-on-simplices` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; chain Stokes is exactly the cochain identity.
- `fs-naturality-alone-implies-that-two-comparison-maps-commute-with-mayer-vietoris-connectors` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; connector compatibility is item 12.
- `fs-the-de-rham-theorem-as-built-here-is-an-isomorphism-of-rings` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`: the vector-space comparison alone is not automatically
  a ring isomorphism; item 15 must consume AT-7 and prove the wedge/cup
  compatibility homotopy.
- `fs-compactly-supported-cohomology-is-contravariant-for-every-smooth-map` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; pullback needs properness.
- `fs-the-degree-of-a-proper-map-is-the-number-of-points-in-a-regular-fibre` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; local orientation signs matter.
- `fs-a-homotopy-between-proper-maps-is-automatically-a-proper-homotopy` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`.

### B page — `de-rham-comparison-and-degree-examples`

1. `ex-de-rham-integration-cochain-on-a-smooth-path` — **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-chain-stokes-on-an-oriented-two-simplex` — **example** `[LA]`.
3. `ex-the-de-rham-map-on-the-angular-form` — **example** `[LA]`.
4. `ex-the-local-de-rham-comparison-on-a-ball` — **example** `[LL]`.
5. `ex-connector-compatibility-for-a-two-arc-cover-of-the-circle` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
6. `ex-a-normalized-compactly-supported-top-form-on-euclidean-space` —
   **example** `[LL]`.
7. `ex-degree-of-a-reflection-of-a-sphere` — **example** `[LL]`.
8. `ex-degree-of-z-to-the-m-on-the-circle-from-a-regular-value` — **example**
   `[LL]`.
9. `ex-a-two-sheeted-orientation-preserving-cover-has-degree-two` —

   not general covering-space theory.

    use opposite local signs.
11. `cex-a-proper-endpoint-homotopy-that-is-not-a-proper-combined-map` —

12. `ex-nonzero-degree-forces-surjectivity-on-closed-oriented-manifolds` —

### Sources and exact locators

- **Lee, *Introduction to Smooth Manifolds*, 2nd ed., Ch. 18 “The de Rham
  Theorem,” especially Thm. 18.12 “Stokes for Chains,” Prop. 18.13
  (naturality), and Thm. 18.14 (de Rham), together with Ch. 17 heading “Degree
  Theory.”** Primary textbook source for items 1–36. The general five lemma is
  `already-published` as `thm-five-lemma-for-modules`, and generic long exact
  sequences are `deferred(HA-4 owns them but is scaffolded and unauthored)`.
- **Park, “Proof of de Rham's Theorem,” §§3 “De Rham Map” and 4 “Example:
  deRham Cohomology of $S^n$,” especially Thm. 3.1, Prop. 3.2, and Thm. 3.3.**
  Focused independent proof guide for chain Stokes, naturality, connector
  compatibility, and the five-lemma comparison.
- **van der Vorst, *De Rham Cohomology*, §10 headings “singular homology,”
  “singular cohomology,” “smooth simplices,” “de Rham homomorphism,” and “de
  Rham theorem,” especially Thm. 10.2, Lemmas 10.5–10.9, and Thm. 10.3.**
  Independent lecture-note control; minor expository slips are resolved in
  favour of Lee/Hatcher/Park.
- **Robbin--Salamon, *Introduction to Differential Topology*, Ch. 5 §5.3
  “Lie Derivative,” subheading “Integration and Exactness,” especially Thm.
  5.3.10 and Cor. 5.3.12.** Independent textbook backing for items 18–25.
- **Hatcher, *Algebraic Topology*, §3.1 “Cohomology of Spaces” and
  “Mayer–Vietoris Sequences.”** Control for the singular comparison target.
  Cup products later in Ch. 3 are consumed through AT-7 rather than silently
  imported or reconstructed here.

### Proof strategy and traps

The standard simplex is handled as one explicit Jordan polytope. Its face
orientation computation supplies the alternating boundary signs, so general
corners are unnecessary. Chain Stokes makes integration a cochain map; quotient
well-definedness then comes from HA. The global comparison is a five-lemma
argument only after the connector square has been checked at representatives.
The ring refinement uses AT-7's Alexander--Whitney cup product and proves the
standard cochain homotopy comparing cup after integration with integration of
wedge; graded multiplicativity is not inferred from vector-space naturality.

For compact-support top cohomology, the Euclidean zero-integral primitive is
the hard local lemma. A compact support meets finitely many coordinate balls;
connectedness lets normalized bump mass be transported across an overlapping
chain of balls, reducing the global kernel to the Euclidean lemma. Degree is
then a scalar on a one-dimensional space. To prove its regular-value formula,
choose a compactly supported form in a regular-value neighbourhood, use the
inverse theorem on each of the finitely many sheets, and apply manifold change
of variables with the local orientation sign. On closed manifolds the de Rham
pairing with AT-8's fundamental class identifies this integer with homological
degree, so the two definitions and their composition laws agree.

**Well-definedness discharged:** simplex orientation and coordinate choice
(items 1–5), representatives/naturality/connectors and AT-7 cup compatibility
(6–17), compact support
under proper pullback and primitive support control (18–25), and independence
of forms and regular values in degree (26–36).
**Choice:** simplex integration and finite five-lemma diagrams are ZF. The
global comparison inherits DG-16's $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$ open-set boundary.
Compact-support globalization uses finite covers of each compact support plus
the supplied connected manifold and adds no choice beyond DG-2. Degree adds
none. No full AC/injective-coefficient route is used.
**Unresolved seam:** HA-1/HA-2/HA-4 and the named AT-6/AT-7/AT-8 interfaces
remain build prerequisites. Poincaré duality beyond the consumed orientation/
fundamental-class interface, Thom isomorphism, Künneth, and general excision
remain precise algebraic-topology deferrals.

## DG-18. Riemannian Metrics, Length, Distance, and Volume

**A page:** `riemannian-metrics-length-distance-and-volume`
**B page:** `riemannian-metric-and-volume-examples`
**Requires:** DG-2, DG-3, DG-5, DG-11–DG-14, and published
`compactness` and `line-integrals-and-the-gradient-theorem`.

All metrics are positive definite. Pseudo-Riemannian terminology is kept out
of definitions whose proofs use norms or metric distance. DG-5 has already
proved that a Riemannian metric exists; this pair develops the geometry it
induces and reconciles signed volume forms with orientation-free densities.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Metrics, isometries, and musical maps**

1. `def-riemannian-metric-and-riemannian-manifold` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   a smooth positive-definite symmetric section of $T^0_2M$.
2. `prop-coordinate-criterion-for-a-riemannian-metric` — **proposition**
   `[LL]`; the matrix $(g_{ij})$ is smooth, symmetric, and positive definite,
   and transforms tensorially.
3. `thm-every-smooth-manifold-admits-a-riemannian-metric` — **theorem** `[LL]`;
   cite DG-5's bundle-metric theorem and do not repeat its partition proof.
4. `def-pullback-riemannian-metric` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $(F^*h)_p(v,w)=h_{F(p)}(dF_pv,dF_pw)$.
5. `prop-pullback-of-a-riemannian-metric-is-riemannian-exactly-for-immersions` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; without injectivity of $dF$ it is merely
   positive semidefinite.
6. `def-riemannian-isometry-and-local-isometry` — **definition** `[LN]`; an
   isometry here is a diffeomorphism with $F^*h=g$, while an isometric
   immersion is named separately.
7. `prop-riemannian-isometries-form-a-group-and-local-isometries-are-local-diffeomorphisms` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`.
8. `def-conformal-equivalence-of-riemannian-metrics` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $\widetilde g=e^{2u}g$.
9. `def-pointwise-norm-and-angle-from-a-riemannian-metric` — **definition**
   `[LN]`.
10. `def-musical-isomorphisms` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\flat:TM\to T^*M$, $v^\flat=g(v,\cdot)$, and its inverse $\sharp$.
11. `thm-the-musical-maps-are-smooth-inverse-bundle-isomorphisms` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; invert the smooth positive matrix $(g_{ij})$.
12. `def-riemannian-gradient` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\operatorname{grad}_g f=(df)^\sharp$.
13. `prop-the-gradient-is-characterized-by-inner-products` — **proposition**
    `[LL]`: $g(\operatorname{grad}f,X)=Xf$.
14. `prop-riemannian-metrics-induce-metrics-on-dual-tensor-and-exterior-bundles` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; define them on decomposable tensors and extend,
    proving independence through the universal property/basis formula.

**Length and the induced distance**

15. `def-piecewise-c-one-curve-on-a-manifold` — **definition** `[LN]`; finite
    subdivision and one-sided endpoint derivatives.
16. `def-riemannian-speed-and-length` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $L_g(\gamma)=\int_a^b|\dot\gamma(t)|_gdt$.
17. `lem-riemannian-length-is-independent-of-piecewise-smooth-subdivision` —
    **lemma** `[LL]`.
18. `thm-riemannian-length-is-invariant-under-orientation-preserving-piecewise-c-one-reparametrization` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; constant intervals are allowed in the monotone
    version and handled separately.
19. `prop-length-is-additive-under-concatenation-and-invariant-under-reversal` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
20. `lem-local-comparison-of-a-riemannian-metric-with-the-euclidean-metric` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`. On a relatively compact coordinate neighbourhood there
    are $0<c\le C$ with
    $c|v|^2\le g_{ij}(x)v^iv^j\le C|v|^2$.
21. `def-riemannian-distance-on-a-connected-manifold` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $d_g(p,q)=\inf L_g(\gamma)$ over piecewise smooth curves from $p$ to $q$.
22. `lem-any-two-points-in-a-connected-smooth-manifold-can-be-joined-by-a-piecewise-smooth-curve` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; connected plus local path connectedness gives path
    connectedness, then finitely many chart segments smooth the path.
23. `thm-riemannian-distance-is-a-metric` — **theorem** `[LA]`; positivity
    uses item 20 on a coordinate ball separating distinct endpoints.
24. `thm-the-riemannian-distance-topology-is-the-manifold-topology` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; prove both local inclusions from item 20. LANDMARK.
25. `prop-length-dominates-endpoint-distance` — **proposition** `[LL]`.
26. `prop-a-smooth-map-with-pointwise-operator-norm-at-most-c-is-c-lipschitz-for-riemannian-distance` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; push each competing curve forward and take infima.
27. `cor-riemannian-isometries-preserve-length-and-distance` — **corollary**
    `[LL]`.
28. `def-extended-riemannian-distance-on-a-disconnected-manifold` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; distance between components is $+\infty$, so no
    finite metric is falsely claimed globally.
29. `def-distance-from-a-point-to-a-subset` — **definition** `[LN]`.
30. `prop-distance-to-a-nonempty-subset-is-one-lipschitz` — **proposition**
    `[LL]`; on a connected Riemannian manifold it is finite-valued and
    $1$-Lipschitz. More generally, on a component $C$ with
    $A\cap C\ne\varnothing$, the restriction $x\mapsto d(x,A\cap C)$ is
    finite-valued and $1$-Lipschitz. No finite-valued assertion is made on
    a component disjoint from $A$ under the extended-distance convention.

**Riemannian volume and the Hodge star**

31. `def-riemannian-volume-density` — **definition** `[LN]`; in coordinates
    $dV_g=\sqrt{\det(g_{ij})},|dx^1\cdots dx^n|$.
32. `lem-the-riemannian-volume-density-is-coordinate-independent` — **lemma**
    `[LA]`; the metric determinant transforms by the square of the inverse
    Jacobian, leaving the density's absolute-Jacobian law.
33. `def-riemannian-volume-form-on-an-oriented-manifold` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; $dV_g=\sqrt{\det(g_{ij})}\,dx^1\wedge\cdots\wedge dx^n$ in every
    positively oriented chart.
34. `prop-the-riemannian-volume-form-is-the-unique-positive-unit-top-form` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.
35. `def-riemannian-volume-of-a-compactly-supported-smooth-density` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; cite DG-14.
36. `rem-riemannian-volume-measure` — **remark** `[LN]`; the Radon measure
    $\operatorname{vol}_g(A)=\int_A dV_g$ for measurable $A$, including
    arbitrary metric balls, cites measure theory's chartwise Lebesgue/Radon
    construction and is not used until DG-23.
37. `def-riemannian-divergence` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\mathcal L_XdV_g=(\operatorname{div}_gX)dV_g$; this is orientation
    independent because the density version agrees.
38. `prop-coordinate-formula-for-riemannian-divergence` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`:
    $\operatorname{div}_gX=|g|^{-1/2}\partial_i(|g|^{1/2}X^i)$.
39. `thm-riemannian-divergence-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. On an oriented
    manifold with boundary,
    $\int_M\operatorname{div}_gX\,dV_g=
    \int_{\partial M}g(X,\nu)\,dV_{\partial g}$ for compact support, with
    outward unit normal $\nu$.
40. `def-riemannian-hodge-star` — **definition** `[LN]`; the unique map
    $*: \bigwedge^kT^*M\to\bigwedge^{n-k}T^*M$ satisfying
    $\alpha\wedge *\beta=\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle_gdV_g$ on an oriented
    Riemannian manifold.
41. `thm-hodge-star-is-a-smooth-bundle-isomorphism` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    construct it in an oriented orthonormal coframe.
42. `prop-hodge-star-squared-sign` — **proposition** `[LL]`:
    $*^2=(-1)^{k(n-k)}$ on $k$-forms in the positive-definite convention.
43. `prop-riemannian-inner-product-of-compactly-supported-forms` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`:
    $(\alpha,\beta)=\int_M\alpha\wedge *\beta$ is positive definite.
44. `rem-the-codifferential-and-hodge-theory` — **remark** `[LN]`; the
    formal formula for $\delta$ may be recorded for orientation, but adjoints,
    $\Delta=d\delta+\delta d$, harmonic theory, and Hodge decomposition belong
    to the PDE/Hodge amendment and are not load-bearing here.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-the-pullback-of-a-riemannian-metric-by-every-smooth-map-is-a-riemannian-metric` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; an immersion is necessary and sufficient.
- `fs-every-riemannian-manifold-has-finite-distance-between-points-in-different-components` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`.
- `fs-riemannian-distance-is-defined-by-the-length-of-a-unique-shortest-curve` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; existence and uniqueness both fail in general.
- `fs-the-riemannian-volume-form-exists-on-every-riemannian-manifold` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the density always exists, the form needs an
  orientation.
- `fs-the-distance-function-is-smooth-on-all-of-m-times-m` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; it is nonsmooth on the diagonal and cut locus.
- `fs-the-hodge-star-is-defined-without-an-orientation` — **false statement**
  `[LL]` in the top-form convention used here.

### B page — `riemannian-metric-and-volume-examples`

1. `ex-the-euclidean-metric-and-its-musical-maps` — **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-the-round-metric-on-the-sphere-as-an-induced-metric` — **example**
   `[LA]`.
3. `ex-the-product-riemannian-metric` — **example** `[LL]`.
4. `ex-a-conformal-metric-on-the-plane` — **example** `[LL]`.
5. `ex-the-hyperbolic-upper-half-space-metric` — **example** `[LL]`.
6. `ex-the-flat-torus-metric-from-periodic-euclidean-coordinates` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
7. `ex-length-and-distance-on-the-circle` — **example** `[LA]`.
8. `ex-a-riemannian-distance-with-no-cross-component-finite-value` —
   **example** `[LL]`.
9. `ex-volume-density-in-polar-coordinates` — **example** `[LA]`.
10. `ex-hodge-star-on-euclidean-three-space` — **example** `[LL]`.
11. `ex-divergence-in-polar-coordinates` — **example** `[LA]`.
12. `cex-a-degenerate-pullback-metric-under-a-constant-map` —
    **counterexample** `[LL]`.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Lee, *Riemannian Manifolds: An Introduction to Curvature* (1997), Ch. 3
  “Definitions and Examples of Riemannian Metrics,” headings “Riemannian
  Metrics,” “Elementary Constructions Associated with Riemannian Metrics,”
  and “The Model Spaces of Riemannian Geometry”; and Ch. 6 opening “Lengths
  and Distances on Riemannian Manifolds.”** Primary textbook source for items
  1–39 and examples.
- **Datar, *Lectures on Riemannian Geometry*, Ch. 8 “Introduction to
  Riemannian metrics,” §§8.1 “Definitions and existence,” 8.2 “More
  Examples,” and Ch. 9 §§9.1 “Isometries,” 9.3 “Induced metric on tensors and
  forms,” 9.4 “Orientability and the Riemannian volume form.”** Independent
  full lecture-note treatment. Datar Thm. 9.1.5 Myers–Steenrod is
  `deferred`: its regularity proof is not used by this track.
- **Lee, Ch. 2 algebraic preliminaries for induced tensor metrics and Hodge
  star, cross-checked with Datar §9.3.** Items 40–44 stop before analytic
  Hodge theory.

### Proof strategy and traps

Positive definiteness and smoothness are checked after every pullback or
bundle operation. The local metric comparison follows by taking extremal
eigenvalue bounds of the continuous positive quadratic form on a compact
coordinate closure. It proves nondegeneracy of $d_g$ and both inclusions of
the metric and manifold topologies; neither is inferred from Hopf–Rinow, which
has not yet been proved.

The volume density is primary on a nonorientable manifold. The signed volume
form arises only after an orientation is supplied. All integrals on this page
are compactly supported smooth integrals from DG-14; arbitrary metric-ball
volumes wait for the measure seam.

**Well-definedness discharged:** metric tensor and pullback positivity (items
1–14), subdivision/reparametrization and finite path distance (15–30), density
and oriented form transformation laws (31–39), and uniqueness/sign of the
Hodge star (40–44).
**Choice:** pointwise and local metric arguments are ZF. Metric existence and
global volume integration inherit DG-2/DG-5's $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$ sufficient
hypothesis. The measurable volume extension inherits measure theory and is
not charged prematurely.
**Forward references:** DG-19 constructs Levi–Civita and parallel transport;
DG-20 proves minimizing geodesic and completeness theorems; DG-23 first uses
metric-ball volume. Hodge analysis is owed to PDE.

## DG-19. Connections, Levi–Civita, and Parallel Transport

**A page:** `connections-levi-civita-and-parallel-transport`
**B page:** `connections-and-parallel-transport-examples`
**Requires:** DG-2, DG-5, DG-8, DG-9, DG-11–DG-13, and DG-18.

Connections are first constructed on arbitrary smooth vector bundles; a
connection on $TM$ is then an affine connection. This separates fibrewise
parallel transport from torsion, which only makes sense after the bundle is
the tangent bundle. Levi–Civita is derived from the Koszul formula with both
existence and uniqueness proved.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Connections on vector bundles**

1. `def-connection-on-a-smooth-vector-bundle` — **definition** `[LN]`. An
   $\mathbb R$-linear map
   $\nabla:\Gamma(E)\to\Omega^1(M;E)$ satisfying
   $\nabla(fs)=df\otimes s+f\nabla s$.
2. `def-covariant-derivative-of-a-section-in-a-vector-field-direction` —
   **definition** `[LN]`: $\nabla_Xs=(\nabla s)(X)$.
3. `prop-connection-laws-in-directional-form` — **proposition** `[LL]`;
   $C^\infty$-linearity in $X$ and the Leibniz rule in $s$.
4. `def-connection-one-form-in-a-local-frame` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $\nabla e_j=\omega^i{}_j\otimes e_i$.
5. `prop-local-coordinate-formula-for-a-bundle-connection` — **proposition**
   `[LL]`: $\nabla_X(s^je_j)=X(s^i)e_i+s^j\omega^i{}_j(X)e_i$.
6. `thm-connection-one-form-transformation-law` — **theorem** `[LA]`; under
   $e'=eA$, $\omega'=A^{-1}\omega A+A^{-1}dA$ in the adopted column
   convention.
7. `thm-local-connection-forms-glue-exactly-when-they-obey-the-transformation-law` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; this is the coordinate-independent construction
   criterion.
8. `prop-the-difference-of-two-connections-is-an-endomorphism-valued-one-form` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`.
9. `prop-adding-an-endomorphism-valued-one-form-to-a-connection-gives-a-connection` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`; hence connections form an affine space.
10. `thm-every-smooth-vector-bundle-admits-a-connection` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    glue flat local connections by
    \(\nabla=\sum_i\rho_i\nabla^{(i)}\). The equality
    \(\sum_i\rho_i=1\), not a derivative-of-weights identity, gives the
    $df\otimes s$ term in the Leibniz rule. LANDMARK.
11. `def-pullback-connection` — **definition** `[LN]`; first on pulled-back
    frame sections, then extend by the Leibniz rule.
12. `thm-pullback-connection-is-well-defined-and-functorial` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; independent of local frame and compatible with composite pullbacks.
13. `def-dual-connection` — **definition** `[LN]` through
    \[
      X\langle\alpha,s\rangle
      =\langle\nabla_X\alpha,s\rangle
       +\langle\alpha,\nabla_Xs\rangle.
    \]
14. `def-product-connection-on-tensor-and-hom-bundles` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    apply the Leibniz rule in every tensor slot.
15. `prop-induced-connections-commute-with-contraction-and-permutation` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.
16. `prop-induced-connection-on-exterior-powers-is-a-degree-zero-derivation` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.

**Covariant differentiation and parallel transport along curves**

17. `def-vector-field-and-section-along-a-smooth-curve` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; a section of $\gamma^*E$.
18. `def-covariant-derivative-along-a-curve` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    $D_t$ is the directional derivative from the pullback connection on
    $\gamma^*E$.
19. `thm-covariant-derivative-along-a-curve-is-independent-of-frame-and-extension` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`.
20. `prop-local-frame-formula-for-covariant-differentiation-along-a-curve` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`; a linear time-dependent ODE in the coefficient
    vector.
21. `def-parallel-section-along-a-curve` — **definition** `[LN]`: $D_tV=0$.
22. `thm-existence-and-uniqueness-of-parallel-sections` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    apply DG-8 linear ODE theory and patch along the compact parameter
    interval.
23. `def-parallel-transport-along-a-piecewise-smooth-curve` — **definition**
    `[LN]`: $P_\gamma:E_{\gamma(a)}\to E_{\gamma(b)}$.
24. `thm-parallel-transport-is-a-linear-isomorphism` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    reverse the curve for the inverse.
25. `prop-parallel-transport-under-reparametrization-reversal-and-concatenation` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.
26. `prop-pullback-connections-intertwine-parallel-transport` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`.
27. `def-metric-compatible-connection-on-a-riemannian-vector-bundle` —
    **definition** `[LN]`:
    $X\langle s,t\rangle=\langle\nabla_Xs,t\rangle+
    \langle s,\nabla_Xt\rangle$.
28. `prop-a-connection-is-metric-compatible-iff-parallel-transport-is-isometric` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; one direction differentiates inner products, the
    other tests infinitesimal parallel extensions.

**Affine connections, torsion, and Levi–Civita**

29. `def-affine-connection-on-a-smooth-manifold` — **definition** `[LN]`; a
    connection on $TM$.
30. `def-christoffel-symbols-of-an-affine-connection` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\nabla_{\partial_i}\partial_j=\Gamma^k{}_{ij}\partial_k$.
31. `thm-christoffel-symbol-transformation-law` — **theorem** `[LA]`; the
    inhomogeneous second-derivative term explains why Christoffel symbols are
    not tensor components.
32. `def-torsion-tensor-of-an-affine-connection` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $T(X,Y)=\nabla_XY-\nabla_YX-[X,Y]$.
33. `lem-torsion-is-c-infinity-bilinear-and-skew-symmetric` — **lemma** `[LA]`;
    hence it is a type $(1,2)$ tensor.
34. `prop-torsion-free-is-equivalent-to-symmetric-christoffel-symbols-in-coordinate-frames` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
35. `def-levi-civita-connection` — **definition** `[LN]`; torsion free and
    compatible with $g$.
36. `lem-koszul-formula-is-necessary-for-a-levi-civita-connection` — **lemma**
    `[LA]`:
    \[
    2g(\nabla_XY,Z)=Xg(Y,Z)+Yg(Z,X)-Zg(X,Y)
    -g(X,[Y,Z])+g(Y,[Z,X])+g(Z,[X,Y]).
    \]
37. `thm-the-koszul-formula-defines-an-affine-connection` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    use nondegeneracy to define $\nabla_XY$ and verify smoothness,
    $C^\infty$-linearity, and Leibniz separately.
38. `thm-fundamental-theorem-of-riemannian-geometry` — **theorem** `[LA]`.
    Every Riemannian metric has a unique Levi–Civita connection. LANDMARK.
39. `prop-christoffel-formula-for-the-levi-civita-connection` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $\Gamma^k{}_{ij}=\frac12g^{k\ell}
    (\partial_ig_{j\ell}+\partial_jg_{i\ell}-\partial_\ell g_{ij})$.
40. `prop-levi-civita-parallel-transport-preserves-lengths-angles-and-volume` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; volume preservation follows from an oriented
    parallel orthonormal frame and is orientation-independent as a density.
41. `prop-levi-civita-connection-commutes-with-musical-isomorphisms` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`: $(\nabla_X\alpha)^\sharp=
    \nabla_X(\alpha^\sharp)$.
42. `prop-gradient-hessian-and-divergence-connection-formulas` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; define
    $\operatorname{Hess}f(X,Y)=g(\nabla_X\operatorname{grad}f,Y)$ and prove
    $\operatorname{div}X=\operatorname{tr}(Y\mapsto\nabla_YX)$.
43. `prop-the-riemannian-hessian-is-symmetric` — **proposition** `[LA]`; use
    torsion-freeness.
44. `rem-holonomy-of-a-connection` — **remark** `[LN]`; parallel transports
    around based loops form the holonomy group. Ambrose–Singer and holonomy
    classification are deferred; the notion is used only as orientation for
    curvature.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-a-connection-is-c-infinity-linear-in-the-section-being-differentiated` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the Leibniz term is essential.
- `fs-christoffel-symbols-are-components-of-a-tensor` — **false statement**
  `[LL]`; item 31 displays the inhomogeneous term.
- `fs-parallel-transport-depends-only-on-the-endpoints-of-a-curve` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; curvature/holonomy obstructs this.
- `fs-every-affine-connection-is-the-levi-civita-connection-of-a-riemannian-metric` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; torsion alone is already an obstruction.
- `fs-torsion-free-means-curvature-free` — **false statement** `[LL]`.
- `fs-every-connection-on-a-riemannian-vector-bundle-is-metric-compatible` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`.

### B page — `connections-and-parallel-transport-examples`

1. `ex-the-flat-connection-on-a-trivial-vector-bundle` — **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-a-connection-one-form-on-a-trivial-line-bundle` — **example** `[LL]`.
3. `ex-gauge-transformation-of-a-connection-one-form` — **example** `[LA]`.
4. `ex-pullback-of-the-flat-connection` — **example** `[LL]`.
5. `ex-parallel-transport-for-a-scalar-linear-ode` — **example** `[LL]`.
6. `ex-the-euclidean-levi-civita-connection` — **example** `[LL]`.
7. `ex-christoffel-symbols-in-polar-coordinates` — **example** `[LA]`.
8. `ex-levi-civita-connection-of-a-conformal-plane-metric` — **example**
   `[LA]`.
9. `ex-parallel-transport-on-the-round-sphere-along-the-equator` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
10. `cex-a-torsion-free-connection-that-is-not-metric-compatible` —
    **counterexample** `[LL]`.
11. `cex-path-dependent-parallel-transport-on-the-sphere` — **counterexample**
    `[LA]`.
12. `ex-hessian-and-divergence-in-euclidean-coordinates` — **example** `[LL]`.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Lee, *Riemannian Manifolds: An Introduction to Curvature* (1997), Ch. 4
  “Connections,” headings “Connections,” “Vector Fields Along Curves,” and
  “Geodesics,” and Ch. 5 “Riemannian Geodesics,” heading “The Riemannian
  Connection.”** Primary textbook treatment. Named controls are Thm. 4.11
  “Parallel Translation,” Thm. 4.12 “Existence and Uniqueness for Linear
  ODEs,” and Thm. 5.4 “Fundamental Lemma of Riemannian Geometry.” Geodesic
  headings beyond the connection are disposed to DG-20.
- **Datar, *Lectures on Riemannian Geometry*, Ch. 10 “The Levi-Civita
  connection,” §§10.1 “Levi-Civita connection: Existence and Uniqueness” and
  10.2 “Induced connection on tensor bundles.”** Independent full lecture-note
  proof of items 29–43.
- **Merry, *Differential Geometry*, Lecture 28 “Connections” and Lecture 37
  “Riemannian Vector Bundles” (connection/metric-compatible portions).**
  Independent vector-bundle route for items 1–28; its curvature-form material
  is disposed to DG-21.

### Proof strategy and traps

A connection is local in its section argument, but that fact is proved from a
bump function before local frames are used. The transformation rule for
$\omega$ is the well-definedness check. In gluing connections, the weights
sum to one; differentiating them would be a fatal extra term only if one
incorrectly tried to weight connection one-forms without their affine
transformation law.

Parallel transport is a linear ODE in each frame. Compactness of the parameter
interval permits finite patching, and uniqueness proves independence of the
frame, subdivision, and patch. The Koszul formula proves uniqueness first;
for existence it defines a smooth vector field by nondegeneracy and then each
connection axiom, torsion condition, and compatibility condition is checked
separately.

**Well-definedness discharged:** connection transformation/gluing (items
1–16), curve/frame/subdivision independence (17–28), tensorial torsion and
non-tensorial Christoffel symbols (29–34), and Koszul existence/uniqueness
(35–43).
**Choice:** local and curvewise constructions are ZF. Existence of an arbitrary
bundle connection inherits DG-2's partition-of-unity
$\mathrm{AC}_\omega$ sufficient hypothesis. Levi–Civita itself is canonical
from a supplied metric and adds no choice.
**Forward references:** DG-20 applies the Levi–Civita connection to geodesics;
DG-21 defines its curvature; holonomy theory beyond the definition is deferred.

## DG-20. Geodesics, the Exponential Map, Completeness, and Hopf–Rinow

**A page:** `geodesics-the-exponential-map-completeness-and-hopf-rinow`
**B page:** `geodesics-and-completeness-examples`
**Requires:** DG-8, DG-9, DG-18, and DG-19; published `compactness` and
`completeness-and-uniform-continuity`.

This pair distinguishes four ideas often blurred together: affine geodesics,
locally length-minimizing curves, the exponential map, and global
completeness. The implications between them are proved in the order supplied
by Gauss's lemma and Hopf–Rinow.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Geodesic equation and local existence**

1. `def-geodesic-of-an-affine-connection` — **definition** `[LN]`; a smooth
   curve $\gamma$ with $D_t\dot\gamma=0$.
2. `prop-geodesics-have-constant-speed-for-a-metric-compatible-connection` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`.
3. `prop-coordinate-geodesic-equation` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
   $\ddot x^k+\Gamma^k{}_{ij}(x)\dot x^i\dot x^j=0$.
4. `def-geodesic-spray` — **definition** `[LN]`; on $TM$ it has local formula
   $v^i\partial_{x^i}-\Gamma^k{}_{ij}v^iv^j\partial_{v^k}$.
5. `lem-the-geodesic-spray-is-a-well-defined-smooth-vector-field-on-tm` —
   **lemma** `[LA]`; coordinate invariance follows because its integral curves
   are exactly velocity lifts of the intrinsic equation.
6. `thm-existence-uniqueness-and-smooth-dependence-of-geodesics` — **theorem**
   `[LA]`. For every $(p,v)\in TM$ there is a unique maximal geodesic
   $\gamma_{p,v}$ with $(\gamma(0),\dot\gamma(0))=(p,v)$, depending smoothly
   on $(t,p,v)$ on an open domain.
7. `prop-affine-reparametrization-of-a-geodesic-is-a-geodesic` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`; nonlinear reparametrizations generally fail.
8. `lem-geodesic-scaling-identity` — **lemma** `[LL]`:
   $\gamma_{p,av}(t)=\gamma_{p,v}(at)$ wherever both sides are defined.
9. `def-geodesically-complete-riemannian-manifold` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   every maximal geodesic is defined on all $\mathbb R$.

**Exponential map and normal coordinates**

10. `def-domain-and-exponential-map-of-a-connection` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\mathcal E=\{v\in TM:1\in I_v\}$ and
    $\exp(v)=\gamma_v(1)$; $\exp_p$ is the fibre restriction.
11. `thm-the-exponential-domain-is-open-and-the-exponential-map-is-smooth` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; use the open flow domain of the spray.
12. `prop-exponential-map-scales-geodesic-time` — **proposition** `[LL]`:
    $\exp_p(tv)=\gamma_{p,v}(t)$ when defined.
13. `thm-the-differential-of-exp-p-at-zero-is-the-identity` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; differentiate $t\mapsto\exp_p(tv)$ at zero.
14. `thm-existence-of-normal-neighborhoods` — **theorem** `[LA]`; the
    Euclidean inverse theorem makes $\exp_p$ a diffeomorphism from a star-
    shaped neighbourhood of $0\in T_pM$ onto a neighbourhood of $p$.
15. `def-normal-neighborhood-and-normal-coordinate-chart` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; the linear identification $T_pM\cong\mathbb R^n$ uses a supplied
    basis.
16. `prop-properties-of-normal-coordinates-at-the-center` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`: $g_{ij}(p)=\delta_{ij}$ for an orthonormal starting basis,
    $\Gamma^k{}_{ij}(p)=0$, and radial lines are geodesics.
17. `thm-existence-of-geodesically-convex-neighborhoods` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    sufficiently small neighborhoods have a unique geodesic segment between
    any two points that stays inside and varies smoothly with the endpoints.
18. `def-injectivity-radius-at-a-point-and-of-a-manifold` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; pointwise supremum of radii on which $\exp_p$ is diffeomorphic,
    global infimum over $p$.
19. `prop-injectivity-radius-at-each-point-is-positive` — **proposition**
    `[LL]`; the global injectivity radius can be zero.

**Variations, Gauss lemma, and local minimization**

20. `def-smooth-variation-and-variation-field-of-a-curve` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; proper endpoint and fixed-endpoint variations are distinguished.
21. `def-energy-of-a-piecewise-smooth-curve` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $E(\gamma)=\frac12\int_a^b|\dot\gamma|^2dt$.
22. `prop-length-energy-inequality-and-constant-speed-equality-case` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; Cauchy–Schwarz gives
    $L^2\le2(b-a)E$.
23. `thm-first-variation-formula-for-energy` — **theorem** `[LA]`; the
    integral term is $-\int g(V,D_t\dot\gamma)$ and all endpoint terms are
    printed.
24. `cor-geodesics-are-exactly-critical-points-of-energy-with-fixed-endpoints` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; variations realize arbitrary compactly supported
    fields along the curve.
25. `thm-first-variation-formula-for-length` — **theorem** `[LA]`; assume the
    central curve is regular and record endpoint/tangential terms.
26. `thm-gauss-lemma` — **theorem** `[LA]`. In the exponential domain, radial
    directions are orthogonal to variations tangent to the sphere in $T_pM$.
    LANDMARK.
27. `cor-polar-form-of-the-metric-in-normal-coordinates` — **corollary**
    `[LA]`; $g=dr^2+g_r$ with no mixed radial-angular terms away from the
    center.
28. `thm-radial-geodesics-minimize-length-in-a-normal-neighborhood` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; equality forces the competing curve to be a monotone
    radial reparametrization.
29. `cor-local-formula-for-distance-from-the-center-of-a-normal-neighborhood` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`: $d(p,\exp_pv)=|v|$ within a normal ball.
30. `cor-sufficiently-short-geodesic-segments-are-uniquely-minimizing` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
31. `thm-a-length-minimizing-piecewise-smooth-curve-is-a-constant-speed-geodesic-up-to-reparametrization` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; corner smoothing removes breaks and first variation
    gives the equation.

**Completeness and Hopf–Rinow**

32. `lem-geodesics-can-be-continued-while-their-velocity-lifts-remain-in-a-compact-subset` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; cite DG-8 continuation for the spray.
33. `lem-a-finite-endpoint-of-a-maximal-unit-speed-geodesic-produces-a-cauchy-curve` —
    **lemma** `[LL]`.
34. `thm-metric-completeness-implies-geodesic-completeness` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; the Cauchy endpoint lies in $M$ and local ODE existence extends the
    geodesic.
35. `lem-radial-geodesics-from-one-point-reach-every-point-under-global-exp-domain` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; the set reached by minimizing radial geodesics is open
    and closed, using compactness of the unit sphere at the frontier.
36. `thm-hopf-rinow` — **theorem** `[LA]`. For a connected Riemannian
    manifold, the following are equivalent: metric completeness; geodesic
    completeness; $\exp_p$ is defined on all $T_pM$ for one/every $p$;
    closed bounded subsets are compact. Any two points are then joined by a
    minimizing geodesic. LANDMARK.
37. `cor-complete-connected-riemannian-manifolds-are-proper-length-spaces` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
38. `cor-compact-riemannian-manifolds-are-geodesically-complete` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
39. `cor-a-closed-embedded-submanifold-of-a-complete-riemannian-manifold-is-complete-in-the-induced-metric` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; closedness plus the ambient distance comparison
    gives metric completeness.
40. `cor-a-local-isometry-from-a-complete-connected-manifold-has-geodesically-complete-target-image` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; the covering conclusion is postponed to the
    unresolved covering seam.
41. `prop-a-riemannian-product-is-complete-iff-each-factor-is-complete` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.
42. `prop-a-connected-riemannian-manifold-is-incomplete-iff-some-unit-speed-geodesic-escapes-every-compact-set-in-finite-time` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; use item 32 and Hopf–Rinow.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-every-affinely-reparametrized-geodesic-remains-unit-speed` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; speed scales by the slope.
- `fs-the-exponential-map-is-defined-on-all-of-tm-for-every-riemannian-manifold` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; this is geodesic completeness.
- `fs-normal-coordinates-make-the-metric-euclidean-throughout-the-chart` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; only center values and first derivatives are
  normalized.
- `fs-every-geodesic-segment-is-globally-length-minimizing` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`.
- `fs-any-two-points-of-a-riemannian-manifold-are-joined-by-a-minimizing-geodesic` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; completeness is sufficient and missing in
  punctured-plane examples.
- `fs-geodesic-completeness-means-the-manifold-is-compact` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; Euclidean space is complete and noncompact.

### B page — `geodesics-and-completeness-examples`

1. `ex-straight-lines-as-euclidean-geodesics` — **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-great-circles-as-round-sphere-geodesics` — **example** `[LA]`.
3. `ex-geodesics-of-a-riemannian-product` — **example** `[LL]`.
4. `ex-geodesics-in-the-poincare-upper-half-plane` — **example** `[LA]`;
   vertical lines and orthogonal semicircles.
5. `ex-normal-coordinates-on-the-round-sphere` — **example** `[LA]`.
6. `ex-the-exponential-map-of-a-flat-torus-is-not-injective` — **example**
   `[LL]`.
7. `ex-the-punctured-euclidean-plane-is-geodesically-incomplete` —
   **example** `[LL]`.
8. `ex-an-open-unit-ball-with-euclidean-metric-is-metrically-incomplete` —
   **example** `[LL]`.
9. `ex-hyperbolic-space-is-complete` — **example** `[LA]`.
10. `cex-antipodal-points-on-a-round-sphere-have-many-minimizing-geodesics` —
    **counterexample** `[LL]`.
11. `cex-a-complete-manifold-with-zero-global-injectivity-radius` —
    **counterexample** `[LA]`; a sourced narrowing-neck example is required at
    build time and is non-load-bearing.
12. `ex-hopf-rinow-on-a-flat-cylinder` — **example** `[LL]`.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Lee, *Riemannian Manifolds: An Introduction to Curvature* (1997), Ch. 4 heading
  “Geodesics”; Ch. 5 headings “The Exponential Map” and “Normal Neighborhoods
  and Normal Coordinates”; and Ch. 6 headings “Lengths and Distances on
  Riemannian Manifolds,” “Geodesics and Minimizing Curves,” and
  “Completeness.”** Primary textbook route. Named controls are Thm. 4.10,
  Prop. 6.5 (first variation), Thm. 6.8 (Gauss lemma), Thm. 6.13
  (Hopf–Rinow), and Cors. 6.14–6.16.
- **Datar, *Lectures on Riemannian Geometry*, Chs. 15–20: §§15.2 “Local
  existence and uniqueness,” 16.3 “The first variation formula,” 17.1
  “Definition and local diffeomorphism property,” 17.2 “Geodesic normal
  coordinates,” 18.1 “Gauss Lemma,” 19.1 “The Riemannian distance function,”
  and 19.2 “Geodesic completeness and metric completeness.”** Independent
  full proof route; named controls include Thms. 15.2.1, 15.2.3, 19.2.1, and
  Ch. 20 Thm. 20.1.1 (Ambrose). Its complete-local-isometry covering
  conclusion is
  `deferred(the conclusion awaits covering-spaces-and-lifting)`; it is not
  described as a metric- or parallel-transport-comparison theorem.

### Proof strategy and traps

The geodesic equation is turned into the geodesic spray so DG-8 supplies a
jointly smooth maximal flow. Normal coordinates use
$d(\exp_p)_0=\mathrm{id}$ and the actually published Euclidean inverse
theorem. Gauss's lemma differentiates a two-parameter radial geodesic
variation; metric compatibility and torsion-freeness make the mixed derivative
constant. It is Gauss's lemma—not the geodesic equation alone—that proves
local minimality.

For Hopf–Rinow, use Datar's compact-unit-sphere minimizing-direction proof.
At a frontier distance from $p$, compactness of the unit sphere supplies a
convergent subsequence of directions, giving a new radial minimizer and
openness/closedness of the reachable set. This avoids an unrecorded countable
sequence of arbitrary approximate minimizers. Properness then follows by the
same radial compactness and finite subcovers.

**Well-definedness discharged:** spray and maximal exponential domain (items
1–13), basis-dependent normal coordinates versus intrinsic exponential
(14–19), endpoint and parameter terms in variation formulas (20–31), and
every completeness equivalence (32–42).
**Choice:** local ODE/geodesic arguments are ZF. The selected Hopf–Rinow proof
uses compactness of a supplied finite-dimensional unit sphere and makes no
countable arbitrary choices, so it is ZF relative to the preceding metric
construction.
**Unresolved seam:** the theorem that a complete local isometry is a covering
map, Cartan–Hadamard's covering conclusion, and universal covering statements
need the currently unauthored topology covering-space page unless the track
builds their path-lifting argument locally in DG-23/Lie theory.

## DG-21. Curvature and Riemannian Submanifolds

**A page:** `riemann-curvature-and-riemannian-submanifolds`
**B page:** `curvature-and-submanifold-examples`
**Requires:** DG-4, DG-5, DG-11, and DG-18–DG-20.

The sign convention from §3.2 is repeated on the page before any identity.
Curvature is first defined for an affine connection, proved tensorial, and
then specialized to Levi–Civita. The Gauss and Weingarten formulas finally
explain which part of submanifold curvature is intrinsic.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Curvature of a connection**

1. `def-curvature-of-an-affine-connection` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $R(X,Y)Z=\nabla_X\nabla_YZ-\nabla_Y\nabla_XZ-
   \nabla_{[X,Y]}Z$.
2. `lem-curvature-is-c-infinity-linear-in-all-three-vector-fields` —
   **lemma** `[LA]`; expand every product rule and cancel derivative terms.
3. `thm-curvature-is-a-type-one-three-tensor` — **theorem** `[LL]`.
4. `prop-curvature-is-skew-in-its-first-two-arguments` — **proposition**
   `[LL]`.
5. `prop-coordinate-formula-for-the-curvature-tensor` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
   $R^\ell{}_{kij}=\partial_i\Gamma^\ell{}_{jk}-
   \partial_j\Gamma^\ell{}_{ik}+\Gamma^m{}_{jk}\Gamma^\ell{}_{im}-
   \Gamma^m{}_{ik}\Gamma^\ell{}_{jm}$ in the adopted index convention.
6. `def-curvature-of-a-vector-bundle-connection` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   the same commutator formula on sections of $E$.
7. `prop-vector-bundle-curvature-is-an-endomorphism-valued-two-form` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`.
8. `thm-curvature-two-form-structure-equation` — **theorem** `[LA]`; in a
   local frame $\Omega=d\omega+\omega\wedge\omega$, with matrix multiplication
   order fixed.
9. `thm-second-bianchi-identity-for-a-bundle-connection` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
   $d^\nabla\Omega=0$, after defining the covariant exterior derivative on
   endomorphism-valued forms inline from DG-19's induced connection.
10. `prop-flat-connections-have-locally-path-independent-parallel-transport-on-a-coordinate-ball` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; prove through the parameter-variation equation,
    not by assuming a global homotopy theorem.
11. `thm-a-flat-connection-admits-local-parallel-frames` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    parallel transport from a star center and item 10 give the frame.

**Riemannian curvature and contractions**

12. `def-riemann-curvature-four-tensor` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\operatorname{Rm}(X,Y,Z,W)=g(R(X,Y)Z,W)$.
13. `thm-algebraic-symmetries-of-the-riemann-tensor` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    skewness in $(X,Y)$ and $(Z,W)$ and pair symmetry
    $\operatorname{Rm}(X,Y,Z,W)=\operatorname{Rm}(Z,W,X,Y)$.
14. `thm-first-bianchi-identity` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $R(X,Y)Z+R(Y,Z)X+R(Z,X)Y=0$.
15. `thm-differential-second-bianchi-identity` — **theorem** `[LA]`; the
    cyclic sum of $(\nabla_XR)(Y,Z)$ vanishes.
16. `def-sectional-curvature` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    \[
    K(\operatorname{span}\{X,Y\})=
    \frac{\operatorname{Rm}(X,Y,Y,X)}{|X|^2|Y|^2-g(X,Y)^2}.
    \]
17. `lem-sectional-curvature-is-independent-of-the-basis-of-the-plane` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; numerator and Gram determinant scale by the same squared
    determinant.
18. `thm-sectional-curvatures-determine-the-riemann-tensor` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; polarize the curvature quadratic form using the algebraic
    symmetries.
19. `def-constant-sectional-curvature-and-space-form` — **definition** `[LN]`.
20. `prop-curvature-tensor-of-constant-sectional-curvature` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`:
    $R(X,Y)Z=K(g(Y,Z)X-g(X,Z)Y)$.
21. `def-ricci-curvature` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\operatorname{Ric}(X,Y)=\operatorname{tr}(Z\mapsto R(Z,X)Y)$.
22. `lem-ricci-curvature-is-symmetric-and-basis-independent` — **lemma**
    `[LA]`.
23. `def-scalar-curvature` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $S=\operatorname{tr}_g\operatorname{Ric}$.
24. `prop-scalar-curvature-is-twice-the-sum-of-sectional-curvatures-of-coordinate-planes` —
    **proposition** `[LL]` in an orthonormal basis.
25. `def-kulkarni-nomizu-product-trace-free-ricci-and-weyl-curvature` —
    **definition** `[LN]`. For symmetric bilinear forms use the sign-aligned
    product
    \[
    (h\mathbin{\owedge}k)(X,Y,Z,W)=h(X,W)k(Y,Z)+h(Y,Z)k(X,W)
      -h(X,Z)k(Y,W)-h(Y,W)k(X,Z),
    \]
    so the constant-curvature tensor is
    $\frac K2(g\mathbin{\owedge}g)$ under §3.2's convention; define
    $\operatorname{Ric}_0=\operatorname{Ric}-\frac Sn g$ and define the Weyl
    tensor as the totally trace-free summand below.
26. `prop-ricci-decomposition-of-the-riemann-tensor-in-dimension-at-least-three` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`. If $n\ge3$, then orthogonally as algebraic
    curvature tensors
    \[
      \operatorname{Rm}=W+\frac1{n-2}
      (\operatorname{Ric}_0\mathbin{\owedge}g)
      +\frac{S}{2n(n-1)}(g\mathbin{\owedge}g).
    \]
    Prove the traces directly with item 25, prove $W$ is trace free and the
    splitting unique, and record that $W=0$ identically in dimension three;
    in dimension two $\operatorname{Rm}=\frac S4(g\mathbin{\owedge}g)$ is
    handled separately rather than by dividing by $n-2$.
27. `thm-contracted-second-bianchi-identity` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $\operatorname{div}(\operatorname{Ric}-\frac12Sg)=0$.
28. `thm-schurs-lemma-for-pointwise-constant-sectional-curvature` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`. On a connected Riemannian manifold of dimension at least three,
    if sectional curvature at each point is independent of the plane, then it
    is constant on the manifold.
29. `thm-a-riemannian-manifold-is-flat-iff-it-is-locally-isometric-to-euclidean-space` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; a local parallel orthonormal frame commutes by
    torsion-freeness and integrates to Euclidean coordinates by Frobenius.

**Riemannian submanifolds**

30. `def-tangential-and-normal-projections-along-a-riemannian-submanifold` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; $T\overline M|_M=TM\oplus NM$ orthogonally.
31. `def-induced-connection-and-second-fundamental-form` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; $\overline\nabla_XY=\nabla_XY+\mathrm{II}(X,Y)$.
32. `thm-the-induced-connection-is-levi-civita` — **theorem** `[LA]`; it is
    torsion free and compatible with the induced metric.
33. `lem-the-second-fundamental-form-is-a-symmetric-normal-bundle-valued-two-tensor` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`.
34. `def-normal-connection` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\nabla_X^\perp\nu=(\overline\nabla_X\nu)^\perp$.
35. `def-shape-operator` — **definition** `[LN]` with the fixed sign
    $S_\nu X=-(\overline\nabla_X\nu)^\top$.
36. `thm-weingarten-equation-and-adjointness-of-the-shape-operator` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $\overline\nabla_X\nu=-S_\nu X+\nabla_X^\perp\nu$ and
    $g(S_\nu X,Y)=\overline g(\mathrm{II}(X,Y),\nu)$.
37. `thm-gauss-equation-for-a-riemannian-submanifold` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    \[
    \operatorname{Rm}^M(X,Y,Z,W)=\operatorname{Rm}^{\overline M}(X,Y,Z,W)
    +\langle\mathrm{II}(X,W),\mathrm{II}(Y,Z)\rangle
    -\langle\mathrm{II}(X,Z),\mathrm{II}(Y,W)\rangle.
    \]
38. `thm-codazzi-equation-for-a-riemannian-submanifold` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    the normal component of ambient curvature is the skew covariant derivative
    of $\mathrm{II}$.
39. `thm-ricci-equation-for-the-normal-connection` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    relate normal curvature to commutators of shape operators.
40. `def-totally-geodesic-submanifold` — **definition** `[LN]`.
41. `thm-equivalent-characterizations-of-a-totally-geodesic-submanifold` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`: $\mathrm{II}=0$; induced geodesics are ambient
    geodesics; ambient covariant differentiation preserves tangent fields.
42. `def-principal-curvatures-gaussian-curvature-and-mean-curvature-of-an-oriented-hypersurface` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; eigenvalues, determinant, and normalized trace of
    the shape operator, with normal-reversal behavior printed.
43. `prop-euclidean-hypersurface-sectional-curvature-from-principal-curvatures` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; in principal directions $K_{ij}=\kappa_i\kappa_j$.
44. `thm-gausss-theorema-egregium` — **theorem** `[LA]`. The Gaussian
    curvature of a surface in Euclidean three-space is determined by its
    induced metric. LANDMARK.
45. `def-mean-curvature-vector` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    $H=(1/m)\operatorname{tr}_g\mathrm{II}$, independent of a choice of
    normal orientation.
46. `prop-first-variation-of-volume-for-a-normal-variation` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; the compactly supported first variation is
    $-m\int\langle V,H\rangle\,dV$ with the adopted normalization.
47. `rem-mean-curvature-and-minimal-submanifolds` — **remark** `[LN]`; $H=0$ is the critical
    condition, but regularity, existence, stability, and minimal-surface theory
    remain out of scope.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-curvature-is-obtained-by-commuting-two-covariant-derivatives-without-a-bracket-correction` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the correction is required outside coordinate

- `fs-christoffel-symbols-vanishing-at-one-point-implies-curvature-vanishes-there` —

- `fs-zero-mean-curvature-implies-a-submanifold-is-totally-geodesic` — **false

### B page — `curvature-and-submanifold-examples`

1. `ex-euclidean-space-has-zero-curvature` — **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-the-round-sphere-has-positive-constant-sectional-curvature` —

### Sources and exact locators

- **Lee, *Riemannian Manifolds: An Introduction to Curvature* (1997), Ch. 7
  “Curvature,” headings “Local Invariants,” “Flat Manifolds,” “Symmetries of
  the Curvature Tensor,” and “Ricci and Scalar Curvatures”; Ch. 8 headings
  “Riemannian Submanifolds and the Second Fundamental Form,” “Hypersurfaces
  in Euclidean Space,” and “Geometric Interpretation of Curvature in Higher
  Dimensions.”** Primary textbook source. Named controls are Thm. 7.3, Thm.
  8.2 (Gauss formula), Lem. 8.3 (Weingarten), Thm. 8.4 (Gauss equation), and
  Thm. 8.6 (Theorema Egregium).
- **Datar, *Lectures on Riemannian Geometry*, Chs. 11–14, §§11.1–11.4,
  12.1–12.3, 13.1–13.3, and 14.1–14.2.2.** Independent full lecture-note
  treatment. Named controls include Prop. 11.3.3, Cors. 12.1.7 and 12.1.9,
  and Thms. 13.1.10 and 13.2.1. The Yamabe theorem in §13.3 (Thm. 13.3.5 and
  Cor. 13.3.6) is `out-of-scope`: it requires nonlinear elliptic PDE.
- **Merry, connection-curvature portions following Lecture 28.** Independent
  vector-bundle curvature and structure-equation check; principal-connection
  and Chern–Weil developments are deferred.

### Proof strategy and traps

Curvature earns the word tensor only after all three $C^\infty$-linearity
checks. The Riemann symmetries are split: metric compatibility gives last-pair
skewness, torsion-freeness gives first Bianchi, and these yield pair symmetry.
Ricci and scalar contractions are then basis-independent tensor contractions,
not coordinate sums used as definitions.

For submanifolds, split the ambient covariant derivative orthogonally. The
tangent part is shown to be Levi–Civita before using uniqueness. Tangential and
normal components of the ambient curvature identity give Gauss, Codazzi, and
Ricci with the fixed signs. In dimension two and Euclidean ambient space,
Gauss immediately makes $\det S$ intrinsic, proving Theorema Egregium.

**Well-definedness discharged:** tensorial curvature and frame forms (items
1–11), all Riemann contractions and plane independence (12–27), orthogonal
splittings and normal-frame independence (28–44).
**Choice:** all constructions are local, algebraic, or use finite-dimensional
ODE already supplied; they are ZF. The existence of the starting metric and
connection retains the earlier DG-2 choice boundary, with no new choice here.
**Forward references:** DG-22 linearizes geodesics to Jacobi fields; DG-23
uses Ricci and sectional bounds in comparison; DG-24 integrates Gaussian
curvature. Einstein geometry, Yamabe theory, and Chern–Weil theory are
deferred; the algebraic Ricci–Weyl decomposition itself is included above.
