## DG-22. Jacobi Fields, Conjugate Points, and the Cut Locus

**A page:** `jacobi-fields-conjugate-points-and-the-cut-locus`
**B page:** `jacobi-field-and-cut-locus-examples`
**Requires:** DG-8 and DG-19–DG-21; a second-order Jacobi equation is converted
to the first-order Euclidean system supplied by DG-8 rather than citing an
unavailable general second-order ODE page.
MT-11/MT-20 are required only for the Radon-volume, Fubini, and null-set
claims in items 36–37.

Jacobi fields are treated simultaneously as solutions of a linear ODE and as
variation fields of geodesics. The equality of those models is what connects
curvature to the differential of the exponential map, second variation, and
the cut locus.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Geodesic variations and the Jacobi equation**

1. `def-geodesic-variation` — **definition** `[LN]`; every longitudinal curve
   $t\mapsto F(s,t)$ is a geodesic.
2. `lem-covariant-derivatives-commute-up-to-curvature-in-a-two-parameter-variation` —
   **lemma** `[LA]`:
   $D_sD_tV-D_tD_sV=R(\partial_sF,\partial_tF)V$ under the fixed convention.
3. `def-jacobi-field` — **definition** `[LN]`; along a geodesic $\gamma$,
   \[
      D_t^2J+R(J,\dot\gamma)\dot\gamma=0.
   \]
4. `thm-variation-field-of-a-geodesic-variation-is-a-jacobi-field` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; differentiate the geodesic equation using item 2.
5. `thm-existence-and-uniqueness-of-jacobi-fields-from-initial-data` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; in a parallel frame the Jacobi equation is a smooth
   linear second-order ODE.
6. `cor-the-space-of-jacobi-fields-along-a-geodesic-has-dimension-two-n` —
   **corollary** `[LL]` for an $n$-manifold.
7. `thm-every-jacobi-field-is-induced-by-a-geodesic-variation` — **theorem**
   `[LA]`; vary initial point and velocity through curves with the prescribed
   derivatives and use smooth dependence of the geodesic flow.
8. `prop-tangential-jacobi-fields-are-affine-multiples-of-the-velocity` —
   **proposition** `[LA]` along a nonconstant geodesic.
9. `prop-killing-fields-restrict-to-jacobi-fields-along-geodesics` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; differentiate the isometry flow applied to the
   geodesic. The formal definition of Killing field is included as
   $\mathcal L_Xg=0$.
10. `lem-wronskian-of-two-jacobi-fields-is-constant` — **lemma** `[LA]`:
    $g(D_tJ,K)-g(J,D_tK)$ is constant.

**Differential of the exponential map and conjugacy**

11. `thm-differential-of-the-exponential-map-in-terms-of-jacobi-fields` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`. For $v,w\in T_pM$, $d(\exp_p)_v(w)=J(1)$ where
    $J(0)=0$ and $D_tJ(0)=w$ along $t\mapsto\exp_p(tv)$, with the parameter
    scaling written consistently.
12. `def-conjugate-points-along-a-geodesic-and-their-multiplicity` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; $\gamma(b)$ is conjugate to $\gamma(a)$ if a
    nonzero Jacobi field vanishes at both, and multiplicity is the dimension
    of that vector space.
13. `thm-conjugate-points-are-critical-values-of-the-exponential-map-along-the-geodesic` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; multiplicity equals the kernel dimension of the
    relevant differential.
14. `prop-conjugate-instants-are-isolated-unless-the-geodesic-is-constant` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; use the linear fundamental matrix and nonvanishing
    Wronskian, on compact time intervals.
15. `prop-conjugate-points-and-multiplicity-are-invariant-under-affine-reparametrization` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.

**Second variation and index form**

16. `thm-second-variation-formula-for-energy` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Along a
    geodesic, print the full boundary terms and the interior expression
    \[
       \int_a^b\bigl(|D_tV|^2-
       g(R(V,\dot\gamma)\dot\gamma,V)\bigr)\,dt.
    \]
17. `def-index-form-of-a-geodesic-segment` — **definition** `[LN]`; the
    symmetric bilinear polarization of the interior expression.
18. `lem-integration-by-parts-for-the-index-form` — **lemma** `[LA]`:
    $I(V,W)=-\int_a^b
    g(D_t^2V+R(V,\dot\gamma)\dot\gamma,W)\,dt$ plus endpoints.
19. `prop-jacobi-fields-are-the-null-solutions-of-the-index-form-with-fixed-endpoints` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; specify whether one or both endpoints vanish.
20. `thm-index-lemma` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Before the first conjugate point,
    among fields with fixed endpoint values the Jacobi field minimizes the
    index form, with equality only for it.
21. `cor-a-geodesic-segment-before-its-first-conjugate-point-is-locally-energy-minimizing` —
    **corollary** `[LA]` for sufficiently small variations with fixed
    endpoints.
22. `thm-a-geodesic-does-not-minimize-past-its-first-conjugate-point` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; construct a negative second-variation field by
    truncating and smoothing the vanishing Jacobi field. LANDMARK.
23. `prop-at-a-conjugate-endpoint-the-index-form-is-degenerate` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`; strict local minimality need not hold.

**Cut time and cut locus**

For items 24–37, $M$ is complete and connected and $p\in M$ is fixed.
Completeness ensures every radial geodesic used in $c(v)$ and
\(\exp_p(c(v)v)\) exists for the required time; none of the global cut-domain
claims is silently asserted on an incomplete manifold.

24. `def-cut-time-in-a-unit-tangent-direction` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $c(v)=\sup\{t>0:d(p,\exp_p(tv))=t\}$, with $+\infty$ allowed.
25. `lem-minimizing-along-a-geodesic-is-an-initial-interval-property` —
    **lemma** `[LL]`; a nonminimizing earlier segment cannot be repaired later.
26. `def-cut-point-and-cut-locus-of-a-point` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\operatorname{Cut}(p)=\{\exp_p(c(v)v):c(v)<\infty\}$.
27. `thm-cut-time-is-positive-and-continuous` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    $c:S_pM\to(0,\infty]$ is continuous for the extended topology.
    Positivity is local normal geometry; both semicontinuity directions are
    proved using compact convergence of radial geodesics and the first-cut
    characterization, rather than inferring openness from upper
    semicontinuity alone.
28. `thm-characterization-of-a-cut-point` — **theorem** `[LA]`. If
    $q=\gamma(c)$ is the first cut point of $p$ along a minimizing geodesic,
    then either $q$ is conjugate to $p$ along $\gamma$ or a distinct minimizing
    geodesic joins $p$ to $q$; conversely either phenomenon bounds the cut time
    in the precise stated direction.
29. `cor-cut-time-does-not-exceed-first-conjugate-time` — **corollary** `[LL]`.
30. `prop-injectivity-radius-is-the-infimum-of-cut-times` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`, with the convention for infinite cut times.
31. `thm-exp-p-is-a-diffeomorphism-from-the-open-tangent-cut-domain-onto-m-minus-the-cut-locus-and-p` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; define the star-shaped domain
    $\{tv:|v|=1,0<t<c(v)\}$.
32. `thm-distance-from-p-is-smooth-off-p-and-the-cut-locus` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; $r_p(\exp_p(tv))=t$ in tangent polar coordinates.
33. `prop-gradient-of-the-distance-is-the-outward-unit-radial-field-off-the-cut-locus` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
34. `prop-hessian-of-distance-in-terms-of-radial-jacobi-fields` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; tangent directions to a distance sphere are
    extended by Jacobi fields and the shape operator is $J'J^{-1}$.
35. `thm-cut-locus-of-a-point-is-closed` — **theorem** `[LA]`; its complement
    is the open normal domain image.
36. `thm-cut-locus-of-a-point-has-riemannian-volume-zero` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    represent it locally as the radial graph of the continuous cut-time
    function, use one-dimensional nullity of graph fibres, product Fubini, and
    chartwise smooth/Lipschitz null preservation. Do not invoke an unavailable
    Rademacher theorem.
37. `cor-polar-integration-may-discard-the-cut-locus` — **corollary** `[LA]`;
    after the measure-theory volume seam is authored.
38. `rem-the-morse-index-theorem-for-geodesics` — **remark** `[LN]`; the full equality
    between the path-space energy index and conjugate-point multiplicities is
    a deferred infinite-dimensional/Morse-theoretic theorem. Items 20–23 are
    the finite variation consequences needed here.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-every-vector-field-along-a-geodesic-is-a-jacobi-field` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; it must solve a second-order equation.
- `fs-conjugacy-is-a-property-of-two-points-independent-of-the-geodesic-between-them` —

- `fs-a-geodesic-stops-minimizing-exactly-at-its-first-conjugate-point` —

- `fs-the-distance-from-p-is-smooth-on-m-minus-p` — **false statement** `[LL]`;

- `fs-the-cut-locus-of-a-point-is-always-a-smooth-hypersurface` — **false

- `fs-nullity-of-the-cut-locus-follows-merely-because-it-has-empty-interior` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the radial graph/Fubini proof is required.

### B page — `jacobi-field-and-cut-locus-examples`

1. `ex-jacobi-fields-in-euclidean-space` — **example** `[LL]`; $J(t)=A+tB$.
2. `ex-jacobi-fields-in-constant-sectional-curvature` — **example** `[LA]`;

### Sources and exact locators

- **Lee, *Riemannian Manifolds: An Introduction to Curvature* (1997), Ch. 10 “Jacobi
  Fields,” headings “The Jacobi Equation,” “Computations of Jacobi Fields,”
  “Conjugate Points,” “The Second Variation Formula,” and “Geodesics Do Not
  Minimize Past Conjugate Points.”** Primary textbook source. Named controls
  are Thm. 10.2, Lem. 10.3, Prop. 10.4, Thm. 10.12, and Thm. 10.15.
- **Datar, *Lectures on Riemannian Geometry*, Chs. 21–23: §§21.1 “Second
  variation formula,” 21.2 “The index form,” 22.2 “Variations through
  geodesics,” 22.3 “Conjugate points,” 23.1 “Index Lemma,” 23.2 “Cut locus
  and regularity of the distance function,” and 23.3 “Differentiability of
  the distance function.”** Independent full route; named controls include
  Props. 22.2.1, 22.3.1, 23.3.1 and Lemmas 23.2.1–23.2.2. Datar's Cor. 23.3.2
  is retained only with the non-Rademacher proof in item 36.
- **Eschenburg, *Comparison Theorems in Riemannian Geometry*, §2 “Jacobi and
  Riccati equations; equidistant hypersurfaces,” Def. 2.1 and Rem. 2.2, and
  §5 Lem. 5.3.** Independent comparison-oriented treatment of items 3–20 and
  34–37.

### Proof strategy and traps

The commutation formula linearizes the geodesic equation and fixes the Jacobi
sign. Conversely, vary initial data and use smooth ODE dependence; no abstract
surjectivity claim about variation fields is assumed. The differential of
$\exp$ follows from the same variation, making conjugacy exactly a failure of
local invertibility along a specified geodesic.

Second variation is integrated by parts only after full endpoint terms are
written. The index lemma compares a field with the Jacobi field sharing its
end values; positivity before conjugacy follows from the resulting square.
The cut-locus theorem then distinguishes loss of local invertibility from loss
of uniqueness. Its nullity proof uses the radial cut-time graph plus Fubini,
not the absent Rademacher theorem.

**Well-definedness discharged:** variation/Jacobi equivalence and ODE initial
data (items 1–15), boundary terms and index domains (16–23), cut-time
suprema/infinite values and exponential domains (24–35), and chartwise
measure-zero transfer (36–37).
**Choice:** Jacobi/index and cut-locus topology are ZF. The volume-zero result
inherits the measure track's Fubini/null-set ledger; its radial proof requires
no Rademacher theorem and adds no stronger choice.
**Forward references:** DG-23 uses radial Jacobi determinants and the index
lemma for comparison; its Bishop–Gromov integral cites item 36. Full Morse
index theory remains deferred.

## DG-23. Riemannian Comparison Theorems

**A page:** `riemannian-comparison-theorems`
**B page:** `riemannian-comparison-examples`
**Requires:** DG-18–DG-22; homological/topological page
`covering-spaces-and-lifting`; measure-theory pages supplying Lebesgue/Radon
integration, Fubini, and chartwise Riemannian volume.

The covering and measure pages named above are currently planned with empty
item arrays, so their uses are unresolved build-time seams. Rauch, Riccati,
Hessian comparison, Bonnet–Myers, and the local parts of Toponogov are
independent of those seams. Cartan–Hadamard's global covering conclusion and
Bishop–Gromov's metric-ball volumes must not be built first and justified
later.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Model functions and matrix Riccati theory**

1. `def-comparison-sine-cosine-and-cotangent-functions` — **definition**
   `[LN]`:
   $\operatorname{sn}_k(t)=\sin(\sqrt{k}t)/\sqrt{k}$, $t$, or
   $\sinh(\sqrt{-k}t)/\sqrt{-k}$ according as $k>0$, $=0$, or $<0$;
   $\operatorname{cs}_k=\operatorname{sn}_k'$ and
   $\operatorname{ct}_k=\operatorname{cs}_k/\operatorname{sn}_k$ on its
   natural interval.
2. `prop-model-functions-solve-the-constant-curvature-jacobi-equation` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`.
3. `def-radial-jacobi-tensor` — **definition** `[LN]`; a family
   $A(t):\dot\gamma(0)^\perp\to\dot\gamma(t)^\perp$ formed from normal
   Jacobi fields with $A(0)=0$, $A'(0)=I$.
4. `lem-radial-jacobi-tensor-is-invertible-before-the-first-conjugate-point` —
   **lemma** `[LL]`.
5. `def-radial-riccati-operator` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $S=A'A^{-1}$.
6. `thm-radial-riccati-equation` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
   $S'+S^2+R_{\dot\gamma}=0$, and $S$ is self-adjoint by the Jacobi
   Wronskian identity.
7. `lem-trace-riccati-inequality` — **lemma** `[LA]`:
   $(\operatorname{tr}S)'+(\operatorname{tr}S)^2/(n-1)+
   \operatorname{Ric}(\dot\gamma,\dot\gamma)\le0$.

**Rauch and Hessian comparison**

8. `thm-sturm-comparison-for-scalar-jacobi-equations` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
   state zero and positivity hypotheses exactly and derive the first-zero
   comparison.
9. `thm-rauch-comparison-theorem-first-form` — **theorem** `[LA]`. For
   normal Jacobi fields starting at zero with equal initial derivative norms,
   an upper bound on the relevant sectional curvatures gives a lower bound on
   Jacobi-field length, up to the comparison field's first zero. LANDMARK.
10. `thm-rauch-comparison-theorem-second-form` — **theorem** `[LA]`; the
    common nonzero initial-value version includes equality of initial norms
    and logarithmic derivatives and the no-zero hypotheses.
11. `prop-rigidity-in-rauch-comparison` — **proposition** `[LA]`; equality on
    an interval forces equality of the sectional curvatures in the swept
    planes and the compared fields under parallel identification.
12. `cor-upper-sectional-curvature-bounds-delay-conjugate-points` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; if $K\le k>0$, no conjugate point occurs before
    $\pi/\sqrt{k}$.
13. `cor-lower-positive-sectional-curvature-forces-conjugate-points` —
    **corollary** `[LA]` under the precise completeness/dimension hypotheses.
14. `def-laplace-beltrami-operator-as-trace-of-the-hessian` — **definition**
    `[LN]`: $\Delta f=\operatorname{tr}_g\operatorname{Hess}f=
    \operatorname{div}\operatorname{grad}f$. This geometric definition is
    coordinated with PDE; no harmonic-function theory is developed.
15. `thm-hessian-comparison-for-distance-under-sectional-curvature-bounds` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; off $p$ and its cut locus, compare
    $\operatorname{Hess}r_p$ on the orthogonal complement of $\nabla r_p$
    with $\operatorname{ct}_k(r)g$ in both curvature directions.
16. `thm-laplacian-comparison-for-distance-under-a-ricci-lower-bound` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`: if $\operatorname{Ric}\ge(n-1)kg$, then off the cut
    locus $\Delta r_p\le(n-1)\operatorname{ct}_k(r_p)$, before the positive-
    curvature model endpoint.
17. `rem-weak-laplacian-comparison-at-the-cut-locus` — **remark** `[AN]`;
    distribution/barrier formulations are source-stated but require PDE
    foundations and are non-load-bearing here.

**Cartan–Hadamard and Bonnet–Myers**

18. `thm-no-conjugate-points-under-nonpositive-sectional-curvature` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; the convexity of $|J|^2$ rules out a nonzero Jacobi
    field vanishing twice.
19. `thm-a-complete-local-isometry-is-a-covering-map` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    cited from `covering-spaces-and-lifting` if authored. Otherwise the future
    DG build must prove the specialized evenly-covered-ball/path-lifting lemma
    before this id and record the topology amendment.
20. `thm-cartan-hadamard` — **theorem** `[LA]`. For a complete connected
    Riemannian manifold with $K\le0$, each
    $\exp_p:T_pM\to M$ is a smooth covering map and hence a universal cover;
    if $M$ is simply connected, it is a diffeomorphism. LANDMARK.
21. `cor-simply-connected-complete-nonpositively-curved-manifolds-have-unique-geodesics-between-points` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
22. `cor-squared-distance-is-strictly-convex-along-geodesics-in-a-hadamard-manifold` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; state “Hadamard manifold” as complete, simply
    connected, $K\le0$. For fixed $p$, $d(p,\cdot)^2$ is strictly convex
    along every **nonconstant affinely parametrized** geodesic; constant
    geodesics are explicitly excluded from “strict.”
23. `thm-bonnet-conjugate-radius-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`; if
    $K\ge k>0$, every sufficiently long geodesic has a conjugate point by
    the index form with sine test fields.
24. `thm-bonnet-myers` — **theorem** `[LA]`. If a complete connected
    $n$-manifold satisfies $\operatorname{Ric}\ge(n-1)k g$ for $k>0$, then
    $\operatorname{diam}M\le\pi/\sqrt{k}$ and $M$ is compact. LANDMARK.
25. `cor-bonnet-myers-fundamental-group-is-finite` — **corollary** `[LA]`;
    only after the universal Riemannian covering and deck-action statements
    from the covering/Lie seam are available.
26. `thm-cheng-maximal-diameter-rigidity` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Equality
    $\operatorname{diam}M=\pi/\sqrt{k}$ under the Bonnet–Myers Ricci bound
    forces $M$ to be isometric to the round sphere of curvature $k$; prove by
    the equality case of distance comparison, not as a one-line corollary.

**Volume comparison**

27. `def-model-space-radial-area-and-ball-volume` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $A_k(r)=\omega_{n-1}\operatorname{sn}_k(r)^{n-1}$ and
    $V_k(r)=\int_0^rA_k(t)dt$, with $r<\pi/\sqrt{k}$ for $k>0$.
28. `def-radial-volume-jacobian` — **definition** `[LN]`; the determinant of
    the radial Jacobi tensor relative to parallel orthonormal frames.
29. `lem-logarithmic-derivative-of-the-radial-volume-jacobian-is-the-distance-laplacian` —
    **lemma** `[LA]` before cut/conjugate time.
30. `thm-relative-volume-density-comparison` — **theorem** `[LA]`; under
    $\operatorname{Ric}\ge(n-1)kg$, the ratio
    $J(r,v)/\operatorname{sn}_k(r)^{n-1}$ is nonincreasing along each radial
    direction.
31. `thm-bishop-gromov-volume-comparison` — **theorem** `[LA]`. On a complete
    connected $n$-manifold with that Ricci bound,
    \[
       r\longmapsto \frac{\operatorname{vol}_g(B(p,r))}{V_k(r)}
    \]
    is nonincreasing (within the positive-curvature model radius, with the
    terminal convention printed), and its limit at zero is one. LANDMARK.
32. `cor-bishop-volume-upper-bound` — **corollary** `[LL]`:
    $\operatorname{vol}B(p,r)\le V_k(r)$.
33. `cor-volume-doubling-under-a-nonnegative-ricci-lower-bound` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; print the scale-dependent constant for general
    lower bound and $2^n$ when $k=0$.
34. `prop-rigidity-in-bishop-gromov-on-an-interval` — **proposition** `[LA]`;
    equality forces the radial curvature/model metric on the corresponding
    ball, subject to the no-cut condition, with the global rigidity conclusion
    stated only when its hypotheses hold.
35. `cor-complete-noncompact-manifolds-with-nonnegative-ricci-curvature-have-at-most-euclidean-volume-growth` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.

**Triangle comparison**

36. `def-comparison-triangle-in-the-two-dimensional-space-form` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; for $k>0$ impose perimeter $<2\pi/\sqrt{k}$ and
    side lengths below the antipodal bound.
37. `lem-first-variation-hinge-derivative-formula` — **lemma** `[LA]`; the
    derivative of the opposite side with respect to a hinge side/angle is
    expressed through endpoint angles.
38. `thm-toponogov-hinge-comparison` — **theorem** `[LA]`. If $M$ is complete
    with sectional curvature $K\ge k$, a minimizing hinge with the stated
    positive-curvature restrictions has opposite side no longer than its
    constant-$k$ model hinge. LANDMARK.
39. `thm-toponogov-triangle-comparison` — **theorem** `[LA]`; each angle of a
    minimizing geodesic triangle is at least its model comparison angle, with
    the perimeter/domain restrictions explicit.
40. `prop-distance-between-corresponding-side-points-in-toponogov-comparison` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; state the inequality direction in the adopted
    lower-curvature convention and derive it by subdividing hinges.
41. `cor-diameter-rigidity-from-toponogov-under-a-sectional-lower-bound` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; the precise spherical equality hypothesis is stated,
    not conflated with Cheng's Ricci theorem.
42. `rem-alexandrov-and-differentiable-sphere-theorems` — **remark** `[LN]`; metric
    curvature bounds, Grove–Shiohama, quarter-pinched differentiable sphere
    theorems, and stability theory require additional topology and are
    deferred.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-higher-sectional-curvature-makes-jacobi-fields-spread-faster` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; it focuses them sooner.
- `fs-cartan-hadamard-says-exp-p-is-injective-without-simple-connectedness` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; it is generally a covering map.
- `fs-positive-ricci-curvature-without-a-uniform-lower-bound-implies-compactness` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; Bonnet–Myers needs a positive uniform bound.
- `fs-bishop-gromov-volume-ratio-is-nondecreasing-under-a-ricci-lower-bound` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; it is nonincreasing.
- `fs-a-section-curvature-lower-bound-makes-triangles-thinner-than-the-model` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the comparison angle is no larger than the
  actual angle.
- `fs-the-laplace-beltrami-definition-licenses-the-use-of-all-euclidean-harmonic-function-theory-on-manifolds` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; analytic results require the PDE seam.

### B page — `riemannian-comparison-examples`

1. `ex-model-jacobi-fields-in-positive-zero-and-negative-curvature` —
   **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-rauch-comparison-between-euclidean-and-spherical-geodesics` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
3. `ex-distance-hessian-and-laplacian-in-space-forms` — **example** `[LA]`.
4. `ex-cartan-hadamard-for-hyperbolic-space` — **example** `[LL]`.
5. `ex-a-flat-torus-showing-simple-connectedness-is-needed-for-global-exp-injectivity` —

6. `ex-bonnet-myers-for-the-round-sphere` — **example** `[LL]`.
7. `ex-bishop-gromov-ratio-is-constant-in-the-model-space` — **example**

8. `ex-volume-growth-in-euclidean-and-hyperbolic-space` — **example** `[LA]`.
9. `ex-toponogov-comparison-on-a-round-sphere` — **example** `[LA]`.
10. `cex-positive-sectional-curvature-with-no-fixed-lower-bound-on-a-noncompact-manifold` —
    **counterexample** `[LA]`; use a sourced surface of revolution and keep it

11. `cex-ricci-lower-bound-does-not-control-every-sectional-curvature-in-dimension-at-least-three` —
    **counterexample** `[LA]`.
12. `ex-equality-cases-as-diagnostics-for-all-comparison-signs` — **example**

### Sources and exact locators

- **Lee, *Riemannian Manifolds: An Introduction to Curvature* (1997), Ch. 11
  “Curvature and Topology,” headings “Some Comparison Theorems,” “Manifolds
  of Negative Curvature,” “Manifolds of Positive Curvature,” and “Manifolds
  of Constant Curvature.”** Primary textbook route for items 8–26. Named
  controls are Thms. 11.1, 11.2, 11.5 (Cartan–Hadamard), 11.7 (Bonnet), and
  11.8 (Myers), with Cors. 11.3–11.4.
- **Datar, *Lectures on Riemannian Geometry*, Chs. 24–28: §§24.1–24.3,
  25.1–25.3, 26.1–26.2, 27.1–27.2, and 28.1–28.3.** Independent full
  lecture-note treatment. Named controls are Props. 24.1.1 and 24.2.1, Thm.
  24.3.1 (Cartan–Hadamard), the Rauch proof in §26.2, and Thm. 28.2.1
  (Cheng maximal diameter).
- **Eschenburg, *Comparison Theorems in Riemannian Geometry*, §3
  “Comparison theory,” Thms. 3.1/3.3 (Rauch I/II); §4 “Average comparison
  theoremx,” Thms. 4.1/4.3; §5 “Bishop–Gromov inequality,” Thm. 5.5 and Cor.
  5.6; §6 Thm. 6.1 “Toponogov's triangle comparison theorem”; and §12 Thm.
  12.1 “Myers–Cheng.”** Primary proof source for items 27–41.

### Proof strategy and traps

Rauch is proved through the index lemma: transfer a comparison Jacobi field
by parallel orthonormal identification, compare curvature terms, and use the
minimizing property of the actual Jacobi field. Riccati comparison is the
matrix form of the same argument. Taking a trace uses
$\operatorname{tr}S^2\ge(\operatorname{tr}S)^2/(n-1)$ and therefore changes
sectional to Ricci hypotheses exactly once.

Cartan–Hadamard needs both absence of conjugate points and the complete-local-
isometry covering lemma. Bonnet–Myers uses $n-1$ parallel sine test fields in
the index form and Hopf–Rinow for compactness. Bishop–Gromov integrates the
radial Jacobian inequality over directions and radii, explicitly discarding
the null cut locus via DG-22. Toponogov is a major proof: establish local hinge
comparison by Hessian/Riccati comparison, extend across cut points by support
functions and subdivision, and pass to complete triangles. It is not labelled
a corollary of Rauch.

**Well-definedness discharged:** model domains and Riccati invertibility
(items 1–7), matched initial conditions and inequality directions (8–17),
covering versus diffeomorphism hypotheses (18–26), radial determinant/cut-locus
integration and positive-model radius (27–35), and comparison-triangle
existence/domain/signs (36–42).
**Choice:** differential comparison and Bonnet–Myers are ZF. Cartan–Hadamard
inherits the future covering-space ledger. Bishop–Gromov inherits measure
theory's Radon/Fubini/null-set ledger. Toponogov uses compactness and finite
subdivision but adds no set-theoretic choice.
**Amendment owed to PDE:** it owns analytic Laplace–Beltrami/harmonic theory;
this page mints only the geometric trace definition and pointwise distance-
Laplacian comparison.
**Unresolved seams:** authored covering-space items and measure-theoretic
Riemannian volume must precede items 19–20/25 and 27–35 respectively.

## DG-24. The Gauss–Bonnet Theorem for Riemannian Surfaces

**A page:** `the-gauss-bonnet-theorem-for-riemannian-surfaces`
**B page:** `gauss-bonnet-computations-and-examples`
**Requires:** DG-7, DG-13, DG-14, and DG-18–DG-21.

This is the classical two-dimensional theorem, including smooth boundary and
piecewise smooth corners. It builds the finite geodesic triangulation it needs
and does not rely on an unavailable surface-classification or general smooth-
triangulation page. Higher-dimensional Chern–Gauss–Bonnet remains a separate
denied theory.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Oriented frames, connection form, and geodesic curvature**

1. `def-oriented-riemannian-surface-and-positive-quarter-turn` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; $J$ is the unique metric-preserving bundle map with $J^2=-I$ and
   $(v,Jv)$ positively oriented.
2. `def-connection-one-form-of-an-oriented-orthonormal-frame` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; for $(E_1,E_2)$,
   $\omega(X)=g(\nabla_XE_1,E_2)$, so
   $\nabla_XE_1=\omega(X)E_2$ and
   $\nabla_XE_2=-\omega(X)E_1$.
3. `thm-connection-one-form-rotation-law-on-an-oriented-surface` — **theorem**
   `[LA]`; rotating the frame through an angle function $\varphi$ changes
   $\omega$ to $\omega+d\varphi$ in the adopted convention.
4. `thm-gaussian-curvature-structure-equation` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
   $d\omega=-K\,dA$ for the curvature convention of §3.2.
5. `def-signed-geodesic-curvature-of-an-oriented-unit-speed-curve` —
   **definition** `[LN]`:
   $k_g=g(\nabla_TT,JT)$; for a positively oriented boundary, $JT$ is the
   inward unit conormal.
6. `prop-geodesic-curvature-under-orientation-and-parameter-reversal` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; all signs are printed.
7. `prop-angle-derivative-formula-for-geodesic-curvature` — **proposition**
   `[LA]`. If $T=\cos\theta E_1+\sin\theta E_2$, then
   $k_g=\theta'+\omega(T)$.
8. `def-signed-exterior-angle-at-a-piecewise-smooth-corner` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; the signed rotation from incoming to outgoing unit tangent, chosen
   in $(-\pi,\pi)$ for an ordinary corner.
9. `prop-total-turning-is-the-integral-of-geodesic-curvature-plus-frame-holonomy` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; include corner jumps and frame changes.

**Plane turning and local Gauss–Bonnet**

10. `def-rotation-index-of-a-regular-closed-plane-curve` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; degree of its unit tangent map, stated through the integral of an
    angle lift.
11. `thm-hopf-turning-tangent-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`; a positively
    oriented simple closed piecewise smooth plane curve has total tangent
    turning $2\pi$, including exterior corner angles.
12. `cor-turning-angle-sum-for-a-simple-geodesic-polygon-in-the-plane` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
13. `def-regular-oriented-surface-region-with-piecewise-smooth-boundary` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; compact region, finitely many ordinary corners,
    and outward-normal-first boundary orientation.
14. `thm-local-gauss-bonnet-for-a-frameable-disk-region` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    \[
       \int_DK\,dA+\int_{\partial D}k_g\,ds+
       \sum_j\alpha_j=2\pi
    \]
    for a disk region carrying an oriented orthonormal frame. Use item 7,
    Stokes on $\omega$, item 4, and Hopf turning.
15. `thm-gauss-bonnet-for-a-geodesic-triangle` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $\int_TK\,dA=\alpha+\beta+\gamma-\pi$ for a positively oriented simple
    geodesic triangle.
16. `cor-angle-sum-comparison-for-small-geodesic-triangles` — **corollary**
    `[LL]`; positive/zero/negative curvature integral gives angle sum
    greater/equal/less than $\pi$.
17. `thm-local-gauss-bonnet-for-an-arbitrary-disk-region` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    cover by frameable pieces, subdivide finitely, and cancel internal edge
    integrals and exterior angles.

**Finite triangulations and Euler characteristic**

18. `def-curvilinear-triangulation-of-a-compact-surface` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; finitely many embedded closed triangular disks meet face-to-face,
    with boundary edges lying in $\partial M$.
19. `def-geodesic-triangulation` — **definition** `[LN]`; its edges are
    geodesic segments except prescribed boundary arcs in the boundary case.
20. `lem-a-compact-riemannian-surface-has-a-finite-strongly-convex-cover` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; the set of **all** strongly convex normal
    neighbourhoods is a canonically specified open cover, and compactness
    extracts a finite subcover. No family-wide choice of one neighbourhood at
    every point is hidden in the proof.
21. `lem-general-position-vertex-perturbation-for-a-finite-surface-cellulation` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; perturb the finite vertex data, relative to prescribed
    boundary vertices, into general position inside a fixed finite strongly
    convex cover. Edges are not perturbed after being made geodesic.
22. `thm-finite-geodesic-triangulation-of-a-compact-riemannian-surface` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; refine a finite cellulation subordinate to the convex
    cover, perturb its finite vertex data as in item 21, and **then** reconnect
    sufficiently close vertices by the unique short geodesics in the fixed
    strongly convex charts before triangulating the polygonal cells. No
    perturbation of already geodesic edges is claimed. LANDMARK.
23. `def-euler-characteristic-of-a-finitely-triangulated-compact-surface` —
    **definition** `[LN]`: $\chi(M;\mathcal T)=V-E+F$.
24. `lem-euler-characteristic-is-unchanged-by-edge-and-face-subdivision` —
    **lemma** `[LL]`.
25. `lem-local-gauss-bonnet-sums-over-a-supplied-finite-geodesic-triangulation` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; sum the disk formula over the given triangulation,
    cancel oppositely oriented interior edges and vertex-angle contributions,
    and obtain the boundary Gauss–Bonnet expression as
    $2\pi(V-E+F)$.
26. `thm-euler-characteristic-computed-by-a-finite-geodesic-triangulation-is-well-defined` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; item 25 identifies $V-E+F$ for **every** supplied
    finite geodesic triangulation with the same geometric integral divided by
    $2\pi$. This proves independence without claiming that arbitrary
    curvilinear edge systems have a finite common subdivision; write the
    common value as \(\chi(M)\).
27. `prop-euler-characteristic-is-additive-under-gluing-along-a-finite-one-dimensional-subcomplex` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; the exact inclusion–exclusion formula is printed.

**Global Gauss–Bonnet**

28. `thm-gauss-bonnet-for-compact-oriented-surfaces-with-boundary-and-corners` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`. For the outward-normal-first boundary orientation,
    \[
       \int_MK\,dA+\int_{\partial M}k_g\,ds+\sum_j\alpha_j
       =2\pi\chi(M).
    \]
    LANDMARK.
29. `cor-gauss-bonnet-for-compact-oriented-surfaces-with-smooth-boundary` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; the corner sum is absent.
30. `thm-global-gauss-bonnet-for-closed-oriented-riemannian-surfaces` —
    **theorem** `[LL]`:
    $\int_MK\,dA=2\pi\chi(M)$. LANDMARK.
31. `cor-total-gaussian-curvature-is-independent-of-the-riemannian-metric` —
    **corollary** `[LL]` on a fixed compact oriented surface.
32. `cor-a-flat-closed-oriented-surface-has-euler-characteristic-zero` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
33. `cor-a-positively-curved-closed-oriented-surface-has-positive-euler-characteristic` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; no surface classification/genus conclusion is
    asserted.
34. `thm-gauss-bonnet-for-closed-nonorientable-riemannian-surfaces` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; orient each triangulation face independently, cancel
    edge contributions pairwise, and integrate the curvature density to get
    $\int_MK\,dA=2\pi\chi(M)$ without choosing a global orientation.
35. `cor-gauss-bonnet-for-a-geodesic-polygon` — **corollary** `[LL]`:
    $\int_DK\,dA+\sum_j\alpha_j=2\pi$ for a disk with geodesic sides.
36. `rem-surface-gauss-bonnet-versus-higher-dimensional-cern-gauss-bonnet` — **remark** `[LN]`; genus
    formulas need surface classification, and higher-dimensional
    Chern–Gauss–Bonnet needs characteristic forms. Neither is inferred from
    item 30.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-geodesic-curvature-is-the-ordinary-curvature-of-a-space-curve` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; it is the tangential component intrinsic to the surface.
- `fs-the-connection-one-form-of-an-orthonormal-frame-is-frame-independent` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; its exterior derivative is the invariant
  curvature form.
- `fs-a-geodesic-triangle-on-every-surface-has-angle-sum-pi` — **false

- `fs-gauss-bonnet-for-a-surface-with-boundary-has-no-boundary-term` — **false

- `fs-euler-characteristic-is-defined-by-choosing-any-triangulation-without-proving-independence` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; items 22–26 discharge this.
- `fs-classical-gauss-bonnet-by-itself-classifies-compact-surfaces` — **false

5. `ex-hyperbolic-geodesic-triangle-area-defect` — **example** `[LA]`.

7. `ex-gauss-bonnet-for-a-spherical-cap` — **example** `[LA]`; checks the

8. `ex-a-polyhedral-style-geodesic-triangulation-angle-count` — **example**

    **example** `[LL]`.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Lee, *Riemannian Manifolds: An Introduction to Curvature* (1997), Ch. 9 “The
  Gauss–Bonnet Theorem,” headings “Some Plane Geometry,” “The Gauss–Bonnet
  Formula,” and “The Gauss–Bonnet Theorem.”** Primary textbook source. Named
  controls are Thm. 9.3 (formula), Thm. 9.7 (global theorem), and Problem 9-5,
  which supplies the finite geodesic-triangulation construction decomposed in
  items 18–26.
- **Datar, *Lectures on Riemannian Geometry*, Ch. 1 §§1.1–1.3, especially
  Thm. 1.3.2 “Hopf's Umlaufsatz,” and Ch. 2 §§2.1 “Proof of Theorem 2.0.1”
  and 2.2 “Global Gauss Bonnet.”** Independent full lecture-note treatment.
  Named controls are Thm. 2.0.1 (local Gauss–Bonnet), Thm. 2.2.1 (smooth
  triangulation statement), Prop. 2.2.3, and Thm. 2.2.4 (global
  Gauss–Bonnet). Datar Thm. 2.2.2, surface classification, is `deferred` for
  the specific reason that no classification result is needed for the
  curvature identity.
- **Lee, Ch. 8 Theorema Egregium and DG-21.** This is the independent
  intrinsic-curvature input ensuring $K$ is a metric invariant before it is
  integrated.

### Proof strategy and traps

In an oriented orthonormal frame, $d\omega=-K\,dA$ under the track's curvature
sign. If the tangent angle is $\theta$, then
$k_gds=d\theta+\omega$ along the boundary. Stokes and Hopf's turning theorem
therefore give the disk formula with exterior angles. This calculation fixes
every sign before triangulation.

Compactness gives a finite strongly convex cover. The scaffold perturbs finite
vertex data first and only then joins nearby vertices by unique short
geodesics. Summing local formulas over any resulting triangulation cancels
each interior edge with the reverse orientation; the vertex angle sum leaves
exactly $2\pi(V-E+F)$. Because this equals one fixed geometric integral,
independence of the geodesic triangulation follows without a false finite-
intersection/common-subdivision claim for arbitrary smooth arcs. The
nonorientable closed case orients faces separately, so it does not depend on a
not-yet-authored orientation-cover theorem.

**Well-definedness discharged:** frame rotation, curve/boundary signs, and
corner representatives (items 1–17); finite face-to-face geodesic
triangulation, internal cancellation, and (V-E+F) independence (18–27);
global boundary signs and orientation-free curvature density (28–35).
**Choice:** local formulas are ZF. Compactness extracts finite covers and all
triangulation decisions are finite, so Gauss–Bonnet adds no choice beyond the
earlier existence of the metric/partition structures.
**Scope boundary:** surface classification/genus, Poincaré–Hopf, Chern–Weil,
and Chern–Gauss–Bonnet are not proved or used.

---

## DG-25. Lie Groups, Invariant Fields, and the Exponential Map

**A page:** `lie-groups-invariant-fields-and-the-exponential-map`
**B page:** `lie-groups-and-exponential-map-examples`
**Requires:** DG-3, DG-4, DG-8–DG-12, DG-19–DG-20, and published
group/matrix pages `monoids-groups-and-subgroups`,
`matrices-and-the-matrix-of-a-linear-map`, and
`determinants-of-matrices-over-a-commutative-ring`.

Lie groups are finite-dimensional real smooth manifolds unless “complex” is
printed. The bracket is transported from left-invariant vector fields. This
fixes the signs of $\operatorname{ad}$, the Maurer–Cartan equation, and the
later fundamental-field convention once.

### A-page items in dependency order

**The Lie algebra of a Lie group**

1. `def-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`; a smooth manifold $G$ with smooth
   multiplication and inversion satisfying the group axioms.
2. `def-lie-group-homomorphism-isomorphism-and-automorphism` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; homomorphisms are smooth by definition at this point.
3. `def-left-and-right-translations-on-a-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $L_g(h)=gh$, $R_g(h)=hg$.
4. `prop-translations-are-diffeomorphisms-and-their-differentials-trivialize-the-tangent-bundle` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`:
   $(g,X)\mapsto dL_g|_eX$ identifies $G\times T_eG$ with $TG$.
5. `def-left-and-right-invariant-vector-fields` — **definition** `[LN]`.
6. `thm-left-invariant-vector-fields-evaluate-isomorphically-at-the-identity` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; inverse $X\mapsto(g\mapsto dL_gX)$.
7. `prop-the-lie-bracket-of-left-invariant-fields-is-left-invariant` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`; use naturality under $L_g$.
8. `def-lie-bracket-on-the-tangent-space-of-a-lie-group` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; transport the vector-field bracket through item 6.
9. `def-finite-dimensional-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`; a finite-
   dimensional vector space over a stated field with a bilinear alternating
   bracket satisfying Jacobi.
10. `thm-the-tangent-space-at-the-identity-is-a-lie-algebra` — **theorem**
    `[LL]`; denoted $\operatorname{Lie}(G)$ or $\mathfrak g$.
11. `prop-right-invariant-fields-carry-the-opposite-lie-bracket` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; record the sign rather than using the same
    identification silently.
12. `def-left-maurer-cartan-form` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\theta_g=dL_{g^{-1}}|_g:T_gG\to\mathfrak g$.
13. `prop-maurer-cartan-form-is-a-pointwise-isomorphism-and-left-invariant` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
14. `thm-maurer-cartan-structure-equation` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $d\theta+\frac12[\theta\wedge\theta]=0$, where the bracket-valued wedge is
    defined componentwise and normalized explicitly.

**One-parameter subgroups and exponential**

15. `def-one-parameter-subgroup-of-a-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`; a
    smooth homomorphism $\gamma:(\mathbb R,+)\to G$.
16. `thm-left-invariant-vector-fields-are-complete` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    translate a local integral curve repeatedly, or use the local group law
    and maximal-flow continuation.
17. `thm-one-parameter-subgroups-are-integral-curves-of-left-invariant-fields` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; each is uniquely determined by $\gamma'(0)$.
18. `def-exponential-map-of-a-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\exp X=\gamma_X(1)$.
19. `prop-exponential-scales-one-parameter-subgroups` — **proposition** `[LL]`:
    $\gamma_X(t)=\exp(tX)$ and
    $\exp((s+t)X)=\exp(sX)\exp(tX)$.
20. `thm-the-lie-group-exponential-map-is-smooth-with-identity-differential-at-zero` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; smooth flow dependence and
    $d\exp_0=\operatorname{id}_{\mathfrak g}$.
21. `cor-the-exponential-map-is-a-local-diffeomorphism-at-zero` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
22. `def-local-logarithm-on-a-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`; the inverse
    of a fixed exponential neighbourhood, with its domain recorded.
23. `thm-one-parameter-subgroups-are-exactly-exponentials` — **theorem**
    `[LL]`.
24. `prop-commuting-lie-algebra-elements-have-multiplicative-exponentials` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`: if $[X,Y]=0$, then
    $\exp(X+Y)=\exp X\exp Y$; prove through commuting invariant flows.

**Homomorphisms and adjoint representations**

25. `def-lie-algebra-homomorphism` — **definition** `[LN]`; a linear bracket-
    preserving map.
26. `thm-differential-of-a-lie-group-homomorphism-is-a-lie-algebra-homomorphism` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; related invariant fields preserve brackets.
27. `prop-exponential-map-is-natural-for-lie-group-homomorphisms` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`:
    $F(\exp_GX)=\exp_H(dF_eX)$.
28. `thm-a-homomorphism-from-a-connected-lie-group-is-determined-by-its-differential-at-the-identity` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; equality holds on an exponential neighbourhood and
    hence on the subgroup it generates, which is open and closed.
29. `def-conjugation-and-the-adjoint-representation-of-a-lie-group` —
    **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\operatorname{Ad}_g=d(C_g)_e$, $C_g(h)=ghg^{-1}$.
30. `prop-adjoint-is-a-smooth-lie-group-representation` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`:
    $\operatorname{Ad}_{gh}=\operatorname{Ad}_g\operatorname{Ad}_h$.
31. `def-adjoint-representation-of-a-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\operatorname{ad}_X(Y)=[X,Y]$.
32. `thm-the-differential-of-adjoint-is-ad` — **theorem** `[LA]`.
33. `prop-adjoint-intertwines-the-exponential-map` — **proposition** `[LL]`:
    $g\exp(X)g^{-1}=\exp(\operatorname{Ad}_gX)$.
34. `prop-adjoint-exponential-identity` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $\operatorname{Ad}_{\exp X}=e^{\operatorname{ad}_X}$.

**Local multiplication**

35. `def-baker-campbell-hausdorff-series` — **definition** `[LN]`; begin
    $X+Y+\frac12[X,Y]+\frac1{12}[X,[X,Y]]+\frac1{12}[Y,[Y,X]]+\cdots$ and
    define it by the convergent Dynkin series on a sufficiently small
    neighbourhood.
36. `lem-local-convergence-of-the-baker-campbell-hausdorff-series` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; choose a norm with $|[X,Y]|\le C|X||Y|$ and majorize the
    universal Lie series.
37. `thm-baker-campbell-hausdorff` — **theorem** `[LA]`. For sufficiently
    small $X,Y$,
    $\exp X\exp Y=\exp(\operatorname{BCH}(X,Y))$. LANDMARK.
38. `cor-the-local-lie-group-law-is-determined-by-the-lie-bracket` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; exponential coordinates have the BCH law.
39. `cor-commuting-nearby-group-elements-have-commuting-logarithms-under-the-stated-domain-hypotheses` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; do not assert this outside one BCH neighbourhood.
40. `def-real-and-complex-lie-groups` — **definition** `[LN]`; a complex Lie
    group has holomorphic operations, and its complex tangent bracket is
    $\mathbb C$-bilinear. The complex analytic theory itself is not rebuilt.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-the-exponential-map-of-a-lie-group-is-a-group-homomorphism` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; only commuting directions behave additively.
- `fs-the-exponential-map-is-globally-injective-on-every-connected-lie-group` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the circle is periodic.
- `fs-the-exponential-map-is-surjective-on-every-connected-lie-group` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; a real matrix-group example appears on B.
- `fs-right-invariant-fields-identify-t-e-g-with-the-same-bracket-as-left-invariant-fields` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the bracket is opposite.
- `fs-every-continuous-group-homomorphism-is-smooth-by-definition` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; automatic smoothness is a theorem after the closed-
  subgroup machinery.
- `fs-differential-at-the-identity-determines-a-homomorphism-from-a-disconnected-lie-group` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; component data remain.

### B page — `lie-groups-and-exponential-map-examples`

1. `ex-the-additive-and-multiplicative-real-lie-groups` — **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-general-and-special-linear-lie-groups` — **example** `[LA]`; compute
   tangent algebras by regular-level equations.
3. `ex-orthogonal-and-special-orthogonal-lie-groups` — **example** `[LA]`.
4. `ex-unitary-and-special-unitary-lie-groups` — **example** `[LA]`.
5. `ex-the-real-symplectic-matrix-group` — **example** `[LA]`; distinguish it
   from a symplectic manifold.
6. `ex-the-heisenberg-lie-group-and-algebra` — **example** `[LA]`.
7. `ex-the-affine-group-of-the-line` — **example** `[LA]`.
8. `ex-the-n-torus-and-its-exponential-lattice` — **example** `[LL]`.
9. `ex-matrix-exponential-as-the-lie-group-exponential` — **example** `[LA]`.
10. `ex-adjoint-and-ad-for-a-matrix-lie-group` — **example** `[LL]`:
    conjugation and commutator.
11. `cex-a-real-invertible-matrix-with-no-real-logarithm` — **counterexample**
    `[LA]`; use a sourced exact criterion/witness.
12. `cex-bch-truncation-fails-when-higher-commutators-do-not-vanish` —
    **counterexample** `[LL]`.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, digital 2nd ed., Ch. I
  §§10 “Elementary Theory of Lie Groups,” 14 “Automorphisms and
  Derivations,” 15 “Semidirect Products of Lie Groups,” and Appendix B §4
  “Campbell–Baker–Hausdorff Formula.”** Primary textbook route for the
  ordinary Lie-group, exponential, adjoint, and BCH items. Ch. I §13 real-
  analytic structures is inline in the local BCH proof; no separate analytic-
  manifold theory is minted. This range is not claimed to source the
  Maurer–Cartan form or equation.
- **Bryant, *An Introduction to Lie Groups and Symplectic Geometry*, Lecture
  2 heading “Left-Invariant Forms and the Structure Equations” (PDF pp.
  27–28), Definition 9 and Proposition 9.** Exact independent source for
  items 12–14: the canonical left Maurer–Cartan form and
  \(d\omega=-\tfrac12[\omega,\omega]\).
- **Kirillov, *Introduction to Lie Groups and Lie Algebras*, Ch. 2 §§2.1
  “Lie groups, subgroups, and cosets,” 2.4 “Left, right, and adjoint action,”
  2.5 “Classical groups,” and Ch. 3 §§3.1 “Exponential map,” 3.2 “The
  commutator,” 3.3 “Adjoint action and Jacobi identity,” 3.5 “Lie algebra of
  vector fields,” and 3.7 “Campbell–Hausdorff formula.”** Independent full
  lecture-text treatment and examples.
- **Conrad–Landesman, *Compact Lie Groups* notes, opening Lie-group,
  invariant-field, exponential, and adjoint sections.** Independent
  convention control; compact structure results are disposed to DG-33.

### Proof strategy and traps

Left translation provides a global frame for every basis of $T_eG$ and makes
left-invariant fields complete. One-parameter subgroups are their integral
curves, so all exponential identities follow first from ODE uniqueness.
Differentiating conjugation fixes $\operatorname{ad}_X=[X,-]$ and the sign in
the Maurer–Cartan equation.

BCH is not treated as a formal slogan. A norm majorizes the universal series
on a small ball; a differential equation for
$\log(\exp X\exp(tY))$ identifies its coefficients with Dynkin's series.
Every logarithm/corollary is restricted to the chosen exponential
neighbourhood.

**Well-definedness discharged:** tangent trivialization and bracket transport
(items 1–14), completeness/time scaling/local logarithm (15–24), functorial
differentials and adjoint signs (25–34), and convergence/domain of BCH
(35–39).
**Choice:** all constructions are finite-dimensional ODE/algebra and are ZF.
**Forward references:** DG-26 builds Lie subgroups, quotients, actions, and
homogeneous spaces; DG-27 develops abstract representation/enveloping theory.

## DG-26. Lie Subgroups, Actions, and Homogeneous Spaces

**A page:** `lie-subgroups-actions-and-homogeneous-spaces`
**B page:** `lie-subgroup-action-and-homogeneous-space-examples`
**Requires:** DG-7, DG-9–DG-10, DG-13, DG-20, and DG-25; published topology
`subspaces-products-and-quotients`; planned topology
`covering-spaces-and-lifting` for items 43–46 only.

“Lie subgroup” means an injectively immersed subgroup unless “embedded” or
“closed” is printed. This convention is essential: irrational windings are
Lie subgroups but are not embedded. Quotient manifolds $G/H$ require $H$ to
be closed; free proper actions provide the broader quotient theorem later
consumed by symplectic reduction.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Lie subalgebras and immersed subgroups**

1. `def-lie-subalgebra-and-ideal` — **definition** `[LN]`; a linear subspace
   closed under brackets, and an ideal closed under brackets with the ambient
   algebra.
2. `def-immersed-embedded-and-closed-lie-subgroup` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   an injectively immersed Lie-group homomorphism is identified with its image
   only together with its intrinsic manifold structure.
3. `prop-the-lie-algebra-of-a-lie-subgroup-is-a-lie-subalgebra` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`.
4. `def-left-translated-distribution-associated-to-a-lie-subalgebra` —
   **definition** `[LN]`: $\mathcal D_g=dL_g(\mathfrak h)$.
5. `lem-a-lie-subalgebra-distribution-is-involutive` — **lemma** `[LA]`; use a
   local frame of left-invariant fields and DG-10's frame criterion.
6. `thm-lie-subgroup-lie-subalgebra-correspondence` — **theorem** `[LA]`.
   Every Lie subalgebra $\mathfrak h\subseteq\mathfrak g$ integrates to a
   unique connected immersed Lie subgroup $H\to G$, its maximal Frobenius
   leaf through $e$; connected immersed subgroups arise this way. LANDMARK.
7. `cor-connected-lie-subgroups-with-the-same-lie-algebra-are-equal-as-immersed-subgroups` —
   **corollary** `[LL]`.
8. `prop-an-ideal-integrates-to-a-connected-immersed-normal-subgroup` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; it need not be closed.
9. `lem-no-small-subgroups-in-a-lie-group` — **lemma** `[LA]`; a sufficiently
   small exponential neighbourhood contains no nontrivial subgroup.
10. `thm-cartans-closed-subgroup-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Every closed
    subgroup of a finite-dimensional Lie group has a unique embedded Lie-
    subgroup structure. LANDMARK.
11. `cor-discrete-subgroups-of-lie-groups-are-closed-embedded-zero-dimensional-subgroups` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; discreteness is in the subspace topology.
12. `thm-continuous-homomorphisms-between-lie-groups-are-smooth` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; apply the closed subgroup theorem to the graph and compare its
    projection to the source.

**Homomorphisms, kernels, and quotients**

13. `thm-lie-group-homomorphisms-have-constant-rank` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    translate $dF_e$ to every point.
14. `thm-kernel-of-a-lie-group-homomorphism-is-a-closed-embedded-normal-lie-subgroup` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; its Lie algebra is $\ker dF_e$.
15. `thm-image-of-a-lie-group-homomorphism-is-an-immersed-lie-subgroup` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; its Lie algebra is $\operatorname{im}dF_e$, while the
    image need not be embedded or closed.
16. `prop-first-isomorphism-factorization-for-lie-group-homomorphisms` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; $G/\ker F$ maps injectively immersively to $H$ and
    diffeomorphically onto the image with its immersed structure; an embedded
    image needs the additional closedness hypothesis.
17. `def-homogeneous-space-of-a-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`; a smooth
    transitive $G$-manifold, with $G/H$ as the standard model.
18. `thm-quotient-manifold-by-a-closed-lie-subgroup` — **theorem** `[LA]`.
    For closed $H\le G$, the topological quotient $G/H$ has a unique smooth
    structure for which $q:G\to G/H$ is a surjective submersion, and the left
    $G$-action is smooth. LANDMARK.
19. `lem-the-smooth-structure-on-g-mod-h-is-independent-of-the-local-complement` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; exponential slices from two complements give compatible
    charts and the prescribed topology.
20. `prop-tangent-space-of-a-homogeneous-quotient` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $T_{eH}(G/H)\cong\mathfrak g/\mathfrak h$, transported equivariantly.
21. `prop-isotropy-action-on-g-mod-h-is-induced-by-adjoint-mod-h` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; $h\in H$ acts on
    $\mathfrak g/\mathfrak h$ by the map induced by $\operatorname{Ad}_h$.
22. `thm-quotient-by-a-closed-normal-subgroup-is-a-lie-group` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; multiplication and inversion descend smoothly and
    $\operatorname{Lie}(G/H)=\mathfrak g/\mathfrak h$.
23. `def-principal-h-bundle-g-to-g-mod-h` — **definition** `[LN]`; the right
    $H$-action is free and local quotient slices give equivariant
    trivializations.
24. `thm-g-to-g-mod-h-is-a-smooth-principal-h-bundle` — **theorem** `[LA]`.
25. `def-associated-bundle-to-a-principal-bundle-and-representation` —
    **definition** `[LN]`:
    $P\times_HV=(P\times V)/((p,v)\sim(ph,h^{-1}v))$.
26. `thm-associated-vector-bundle-is-well-defined` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    quotient charts prove Hausdorffness, second countability, smoothness, and
    independence of local principal trivializations.

**Actions, orbits, and proper quotients**

27. `def-smooth-left-action-of-a-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`.
28. `def-fundamental-vector-field-of-a-left-action` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $X_M(p)=\frac d{dt}|_0\exp(-tX)\cdot p$; the minus sign is chosen so
    $X\mapsto X_M$ is a Lie-algebra homomorphism.
29. `thm-fundamental-vector-fields-form-a-lie-algebra-homomorphism` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $[X_M,Y_M]=[X,Y]_M$. The alternative $\exp(tX)$ convention and its
    antihomomorphism sign are recorded.
30. `def-orbit-stabilizer-and-orbit-map-of-a-smooth-action` — **definition**
    `[LN]`.
31. `thm-stabilizers-are-closed-embedded-lie-subgroups` — **theorem** `[LL]`.
32. `prop-kernel-of-the-infinitesimal-orbit-map-at-a-point-is-the-stabilizer-lie-algebra` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; for $p\in M$,
    \(\mathfrak g_p=\{X:X_M(p)=0\}=\ker(d\operatorname{orb}_p)_e\). If the
    global map \(\mathfrak g\to\Gamma(TM)\) is considered, its kernel is the
    intersection \(\bigcap_{p\in M}\mathfrak g_p\), not one selected
    stabilizer algebra.
33. `thm-every-orbit-is-an-injectively-immersed-homogeneous-space` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; $G/G_p\to M$ is an injective immersion with image the
    orbit, not necessarily an embedding.
34. `cor-transitive-smooth-actions-identify-m-with-g-mod-h` — **corollary**
    `[LA]`; if $M$ has its given Hausdorff manifold topology, the orbit
    bijection is a diffeomorphism by the open submersion property.
35. `def-free-and-proper-lie-group-actions` — **definition** `[LN]`; proper
    means $(g,p)\mapsto(p,gp)$ is a proper map $G\times M\to M\times M$.
36. `prop-compact-lie-group-actions-are-proper` — **proposition** `[LA]`.
37. `lem-local-slice-for-a-free-proper-action` — **lemma** `[LA]`; choose a
    complement to the orbit tangent, use the inverse theorem, and use
    properness to shrink so distinct translates do not meet.
38. `thm-free-proper-action-quotient-manifold` — **theorem** `[LA]`. If $G$
    acts freely and properly on $M$, the orbit space $M/G$ is Hausdorff,
    second countable, and has a unique smooth structure for which
    $q:M\to M/G$ is a submersion. LANDMARK.
39. `thm-a-free-proper-action-makes-m-to-m-mod-g-a-principal-g-bundle` —
    **theorem** `[LA]` (right/left convention converted explicitly).
40. `prop-tangent-space-of-a-free-proper-quotient` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $T_{[p]}(M/G)\cong T_pM/T_p(Gp)$, independent up to the orbit action.
41. `def-equivariant-map-and-equivariant-vector-bundle` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; needed by moment maps and associated bundles.
42. `prop-equivariant-maps-descend-to-smooth-maps-on-free-proper-quotients` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; use the submersion descent theorem DG-4.

**Covering Lie groups**

43. `def-covering-homomorphism-of-lie-groups` — **definition** `[LN]`; cite
    the future topology definition and require a Lie-group homomorphism that is
    a covering map.
44. `thm-a-connected-covering-space-of-a-connected-lie-group-carries-a-unique-lifted-lie-group-structure` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; every **connected** covering
    $p:\widetilde G\to G$ of a connected Lie group, together with
    \(\widetilde e\in p^{-1}(e)\), carries a unique Lie-group structure making
    $p$ a covering homomorphism and \(\widetilde e\) the identity. Lift
    multiplication and inversion using the topology lifting theorem; no such
    assertion is made for an arbitrary disconnected covering.
45. `thm-universal-covering-lie-group` — **theorem** `[LA]`; every connected
    Lie group has a simply connected covering Lie group, unique up to unique
    covering isomorphism over $G$.
46. `cor-fundamental-group-of-a-connected-lie-group-is-abelian` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; concatenate and pointwise multiply based loops and
    use the Eckmann–Hilton argument, or identify the deck kernel as central.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-every-lie-subgroup-is-an-embedded-closed-subset` — **false statement**
  `[LL]`; irrational torus winding.
- `fs-every-lie-subalgebra-integrates-to-a-closed-lie-subgroup` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`.
- `fs-the-image-of-a-lie-group-homomorphism-is-always-embedded` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`.
- `fs-g-mod-h-is-a-quotient-lie-group-for-every-closed-subgroup-h` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; $H$ must be normal for group operations to descend.
- `fs-a-free-action-always-has-a-manifold-orbit-space` — **false statement**
  `[LL]`; properness is essential.
- `fs-the-exp-tx-fundamental-field-convention-is-a-bracket-homomorphism-for-left-actions` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; it is an antihomomorphism.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, Ch. I §§10 “Elementary
  Theory of Lie Groups,” 11 “Covering Groups,” and 15 “Semidirect Products of
  Lie Groups.”** Primary textbook route for items 1–26 and 43–46.
- **Kirillov, *Introduction to Lie Groups and Lie Algebras*, Ch. 2 §§2.1
  “Lie groups, subgroups, and cosets,” 2.2 “Action of Lie groups on manifolds
  and representations,” 2.3 “Orbits and homogeneous spaces,” and Ch. 3 §§3.6
  “Stabilizers and the center,” 3.8 “Fundamental theorems of Lie theory.”**
  Independent full lecture-text treatment.
- **Conrad–Landesman, *Compact Lie Groups* notes, sections on closed
  subgroups, quotients, homogeneous spaces, and group actions.** Independent
  proof source for quotient topology/smooth structure and proper compact
  actions.
- **Lee, *Introduction to Smooth Manifolds*, 2nd ed., Ch. 21 Lie group
  actions/quotient-manifold material and Ch. 19 “Lie Subalgebras and Lie
  Subgroups.”** Independent differential-geometric route through Frobenius
  and free proper quotients; other Ch. 19 headings were harvested on DG-10.

### Proof strategy and traps

A subalgebra integrates as the maximal Frobenius leaf through the identity;
translations make it a subgroup and give its intrinsic topology. Closedness
is a separate theorem: exponential coordinates identify the closed subgroup
near the identity with a linear subspace and rule out extra small elements.
For $G/H$, local exponential slices prove the quotient topology is Hausdorff
and second countable and make $q$ a submersion.

For a free proper action, properness is used twice: to make the orbit relation
closed (hence the quotient Hausdorff) and to shrink a transverse slice so only
the identity translate meets it. A mere free action cannot support this proof.
Covering Lie-group statements cite topology's lifting machinery and remain
blocked until it is authored.

**Well-definedness discharged:** intrinsic immersed-subgroup topology and
uniqueness (items 1–12), quotient slice/normality/associated-bundle relations
(13–26), fundamental-field signs and quotient topology (27–42), and lifted
group operations (43–46).
**Choice:** Frobenius, closed-subgroup, slices, and finite-dimensional quotients
are ZF. Items 43–46 inherit the future covering-space choice ledger.
**Unresolved seam:** `covering-spaces-and-lifting` must be authored before
43–46; none of its definitions or lifting theorems is re-minted here.

## DG-27. Lie Algebra Representations, Enveloping Algebras, and PBW

**A page:** `lie-algebra-representations-enveloping-algebras-and-pbw`
**B page:** `lie-algebra-representation-and-pbw-examples`
**Requires:** DG-11 and DG-25; abstract-algebra pages
`tensor-products-of-modules`, `modules-and-module-homomorphisms`,
`free-modules-and-exact-sequences`, `rings-subrings-and-integral-domains`,
and `ideals-and-quotient-rings`.

The general tensor-product page is presently planned but unauthored. Unlike
DG-11's finite-dimensional exterior workaround, PBW and arbitrary
representation tensor products need its actual universal property. This is a
declared build seam. Once available, the track constructs only the new Lie-
theoretic quotients and filtrations.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Basic algebra and representations**

1. `def-lie-algebra-over-a-field` — **definition** `[LN]`; cite DG-25's
   finite-dimensional definition and now permit arbitrary dimension for the
   algebraic constructions, with “finite-dimensional” always stated where
   later structure theorems need it.
2. `def-lie-subalgebra-ideal-and-center` — **definition** `[LN]`; reconcile
   DG-26's geometric subalgebra/ideal definitions and add
   $Z(\mathfrak g)$.
3. `lem-lie-algebra-quotient-bracket-is-well-defined` — **lemma** `[LA]`;
   $[x+\mathfrak i,y+\mathfrak i]=[x,y]+\mathfrak i$ is representative-
   independent exactly because $\mathfrak i$ is an ideal.
4. `def-quotient-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`; `justified_by` names
   item 3.
5. `prop-kernels-images-and-first-isomorphism-theorem-for-lie-algebras` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`.
6. `def-direct-product-and-direct-sum-of-lie-algebras` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   componentwise bracket, with finite and arbitrary-support distinctions.
7. `def-derivation-of-a-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $D[x,y]=[Dx,y]+[x,Dy]$.
8. `prop-derivations-form-a-lie-algebra-and-inner-derivations-form-an-ideal` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; $\operatorname{ad}:\mathfrak g\to
   \operatorname{Der}\mathfrak g$ has kernel the center.
9. `def-semidirect-product-of-lie-algebras` — **definition** `[LN]` for a
   homomorphism $\rho:\mathfrak g\to\operatorname{Der}\mathfrak h$.
10. `lem-semidirect-product-bracket-satisfies-jacobi` — **lemma** `[LA]`;
    expand mixed terms and use that $\rho$ is a Lie homomorphism by derivations.
11. `def-representation-of-a-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`; a Lie-
    algebra homomorphism $\rho:\mathfrak g\to\mathfrak{gl}(V)$.
12. `def-subrepresentation-quotient-representation-and-intertwiner` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; quotient well-definedness requires invariance.
13. `def-irreducible-completely-reducible-and-faithful-lie-algebra-representation` —
    **definition** `[LN]`.
14. `prop-representation-kernels-are-ideals-and-faithfulness-is-injectivity` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
15. `prop-direct-sum-dual-hom-and-tensor-representations` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; print
    $(X\cdot\lambda)(v)=-\lambda(X\cdot v)$ and
    $X\cdot(v\otimes w)=Xv\otimes w+v\otimes Xw$.
16. `prop-symmetric-and-exterior-powers-are-lie-algebra-representations` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; invariant ideals make the quotient actions
    well-defined.
17. `prop-lie-algebra-representations-are-the-same-as-modules-over-the-lie-algebra-ring-action-before-enveloping` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`; this is only the operator-action dictionary, not
    yet an associative-algebra module equivalence.

**Tensor, symmetric, and enveloping algebras**

18. `def-tensor-algebra-of-a-vector-space` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $T(V)=\bigoplus_{n\ge0}V^{\otimes n}$ with concatenation multiplication,
    citing MOD-3 for tensor powers and the direct-sum page for finite-support
    elements.
19. `thm-universal-property-of-the-tensor-algebra` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    every linear $V\to A$ into a unital associative algebra extends uniquely
    to an algebra homomorphism $T(V)\to A$.
20. `def-symmetric-algebra-of-a-vector-space` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $S(V)=T(V)/(x\otimes y-y\otimes x)$.
21. `thm-universal-property-of-the-symmetric-algebra` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    maps to commutative algebras factor uniquely.
22. `def-universal-enveloping-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $U(\mathfrak g)=T(\mathfrak g)/I$, where $I$ is generated by
    $x\otimes y-y\otimes x-[x,y]$; $\iota:\mathfrak g\to U(\mathfrak g)$ is
    the canonical linear map.
23. `lem-the-canonical-map-to-the-enveloping-algebra-is-a-lie-algebra-homomorphism-into-the-commutator-algebra` —
    **lemma** `[LL]`; injectivity is not assumed before PBW.
24. `thm-universal-property-of-the-universal-enveloping-algebra` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; every Lie homomorphism $\mathfrak g\to A_{\mathrm{Lie}}$ factors
    uniquely through a unital algebra map $U(\mathfrak g)\to A$.
25. `thm-lie-algebra-representations-are-equivalent-to-unital-modules-over-the-enveloping-algebra` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; use item 24 in both directions and check morphisms.
26. `prop-functoriality-of-the-universal-enveloping-algebra` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`.

**Filtration and PBW**

27. `def-pbw-filtration-on-the-universal-enveloping-algebra` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; $F_nU$ is the image of tensors of degree at most $n$.
28. `prop-the-pbw-filtration-is-multiplicative-and-its-associated-graded-algebra-is-commutative` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; a commutator lowers filtration degree.
29. `def-associated-graded-algebra-of-a-filtered-algebra` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; $\operatorname{gr}A=\bigoplus F_nA/F_{n-1}A$ with multiplication
    justified by multiplicativity.
30. `def-pbw-symbol-map-from-the-symmetric-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    commutativity of $\operatorname{gr}U$ gives
    $S(\mathfrak g)\to\operatorname{gr}U(\mathfrak g)$.
31. `lem-pbw-spanning-by-ordered-monomials` — **lemma** `[LA]`; swap adjacent
    inversions using $xy=yx+[x,y]$, whose correction has lower degree.
32. `lem-pbw-linear-independence-by-the-regular-representation-on-the-symmetric-algebra` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; construct operators whose leading terms are
    multiplication/differentiation and compare symbols, or use the Diamond
    Lemma route with all overlap ambiguities resolved by Jacobi.
33. `thm-poincare-birkhoff-witt` — **theorem** `[LA]`. For any ordered basis
    $(x_i)$ of $\mathfrak g$, the weakly ordered monomials
    $\iota(x_{i_1})\cdots\iota(x_{i_n})$ form a basis of $U(\mathfrak g)$;
    equivalently $S(\mathfrak g)\xrightarrow\sim
    \operatorname{gr}U(\mathfrak g)$. LANDMARK.
34. `cor-the-canonical-map-from-a-lie-algebra-to-its-enveloping-algebra-is-injective` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; this now licenses identifying $\mathfrak g$ with a
    commutator Lie subalgebra of $U(\mathfrak g)$.
35. `cor-the-enveloping-algebra-has-no-hidden-linear-relations-in-degree-one` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
36. `thm-pbw-symmetrization-is-a-vector-space-isomorphism-in-characteristic-zero` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $x_1\cdots x_n\mapsto\frac1{n!}\sum_{\sigma}x_{\sigma(1)}\cdots
    x_{\sigma(n)}$; it is not asserted as an algebra homomorphism.
37. `prop-enveloping-algebra-of-an-abelian-lie-algebra-is-its-symmetric-algebra` —
    **proposition** `[LL]` as algebras.
38. `prop-enveloping-algebra-of-a-direct-sum-is-the-tensor-product-of-enveloping-algebras` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; use the commuting images and universal properties.
39. `cor-schurs-lemma-for-irreducible-lie-algebra-representations` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; cite the published module Schur lemma through item
    25, with algebraically closed finite-dimensional scalar conclusion stated
    only under its hypotheses.
40. `rem-hopf-algebra-structure-on-the-enveloping-algebra` — **remark** `[AN]`;
    $\Delta X=X\otimes1+1\otimes X$ explains tensor representations, but
    Hopf-algebra theory is deferred and this statement is not load-bearing.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-every-lie-subalgebra-is-an-ideal` — **false statement** `[LL]`.
- `fs-the-dual-representation-has-x-lambda-v-equal-lambda-x-v-with-no-minus-sign` —

- `fs-the-universal-enveloping-algebra-is-commutative` — **false statement**

### B page — `lie-algebra-representation-and-pbw-examples`

2. `ex-standard-representations-of-classical-matrix-lie-algebras` —

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, Ch. I §§1 “Definitions and
  Examples,” 2 “Ideals,” 4 “Semidirect Products of Lie Algebras,” and 14
  “Automorphisms and Derivations.”** Primary source for items 1–17.
- **Kirillov, *Introduction to Lie Groups and Lie Algebras*, Ch. 4 §§4.1
  “Basic definitions,” 4.2 “Operations on representations,” 4.3
  “Irreducible representations,” 4.4 “Intertwining operators and Schur
  lemma,” 4.8 “Universal enveloping algebra,” and 4.9
  “Poincare–Birkhoff–Witt theorem.”** Primary full lecture-text route for
  items 11–40.
- **Etingof, *Introduction to Representation Theory* / Lie-groups notes,
  sections on tensor/symmetric algebras, enveloping algebras, and the PBW
  theorem.** Independent proof route, especially the filtration/symbol proof.
- **Knapp's PBW and enveloping-algebra appendices/early algebra chapter.**
  Independent textbook control of ordered monomials and characteristic-zero
  symmetrization.

### Proof strategy and traps

All quotient brackets and quotient representations are licensed before being
named. The tensor algebra is cited from the actual MOD-3 universal tensor
product, and $U(\mathfrak g)$ is characterized by its universal property.
PBW first proves ordered spanning by lowering inversions; independence uses a
filtered representation/Diamond-Lemma argument whose only critical overlap is
resolved by Jacobi. Only after PBW is $\mathfrak g\hookrightarrow U(\mathfrak
g)$ asserted.

**Well-definedness discharged:** quotient/semidirect brackets and representation
operations (items 1–17), associative quotients and universal factorization
(18–26), associated-graded multiplication and PBW basis independence
(27–38).
**Choice:** for a supplied ordered basis, the algebraic proofs are ZF. A
statement that every arbitrary vector space admits a basis inherits the
published full-AC basis theorem; all later finite-dimensional applications
use finite bases and add no choice.
**Unresolved seam:** MOD-3's tensor-product universal property must be authored
before items 15–38. The future build must substitute its final stable ids.
