## DG-28. Solvable and Nilpotent Lie Algebras

**A page:** `solvable-and-nilpotent-lie-algebras`
**B page:** `solvable-and-nilpotent-lie-algebra-examples`
**Requires:** DG-27; published finite-dimensional linear algebra
`eigenvalues-eigenvectors-and-the-characteristic-polynomial`,
`linear-maps-rank-nullity-and-quotient-spaces`.

Every field hypothesis appears at theorem level. Lie's theorem uses an
algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Engel's theorem is kept in
its field-general finite-dimensional form. “Solvable” and “nilpotent” never
inherit group-theoretic ids or definitions.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Series and closure properties**

1. `def-derived-series-and-solvable-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $\mathfrak g^{(0)}=\mathfrak g$,
   $\mathfrak g^{(i+1)}=[\mathfrak g^{(i)},\mathfrak g^{(i)}]$.
2. `lem-derived-series-terms-are-characteristic-ideals` — **lemma** `[LL]`.
3. `def-solvable-length-of-a-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`; least
   $r$ with $\mathfrak g^{(r)}=0$.
4. `def-lower-central-series-and-nilpotent-lie-algebra` — **definition**
   `[LN]`: $\gamma_1\mathfrak g=\mathfrak g$,
   $\gamma_{i+1}\mathfrak g=[\mathfrak g,\gamma_i\mathfrak g]$.
5. `lem-lower-central-series-terms-are-characteristic-ideals` — **lemma**
   `[LL]`.
6. `def-nilpotency-class-of-a-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`.
7. `def-upper-central-series-of-a-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   $Z_0=0$ and $Z_{i+1}/Z_i=Z(\mathfrak g/Z_i)$.
8. `thm-lower-and-upper-central-series-characterize-nilpotence` — **theorem**
   `[LA]`; terminate simultaneously, with the class relation proved.
9. `prop-nilpotent-lie-algebras-are-solvable` — **proposition** `[LA]`;
   $\mathfrak g^{(r)}\subseteq\gamma_{2^r}\mathfrak g$.
10. `prop-subalgebras-quotients-and-extensions-of-solvable-lie-algebras` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; all three closure statements and the extension
    derived-length bound are printed.
11. `prop-subalgebras-quotients-and-finite-products-of-nilpotent-lie-algebras` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; extensions are deliberately not included.
12. `prop-a-central-extension-of-a-nilpotent-lie-algebra-is-nilpotent` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; contrasts with arbitrary extensions.
13. `prop-a-nonzero-nilpotent-lie-algebra-has-nonzero-center` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.

**Engel theory**

14. `def-nilpotent-linear-transformation-and-nil-representation` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; every representing operator is nilpotent, not just
    each basis generator.
15. `lem-engel-common-zero-vector` — **lemma** `[LA]`. A finite-dimensional
    Lie algebra of nilpotent endomorphisms has a nonzero vector annihilated by
    every member.
16. `thm-engels-triangularization-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`; there is a
    basis in which every operator in a finite-dimensional nil Lie algebra is
    strictly upper triangular.
17. `thm-engels-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. A finite-dimensional Lie
    algebra is nilpotent iff every $\operatorname{ad}_X$ is nilpotent. LANDMARK.
18. `cor-a-lie-algebra-with-nilpotent-adjoint-representation-has-a-central-series` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; the proof constructs one inductively rather than
    quoting the equivalent definition.
19. `cor-a-finite-dimensional-nilpotent-lie-algebra-has-a-codimension-one-ideal-containing-any-given-proper-subalgebra` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`.

**Lie's theorem and solvable representations**

20. `lem-a-finite-dimensional-solvable-lie-algebra-has-a-codimension-one-ideal-over-an-algebraically-closed-characteristic-zero-field` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; every **nonzero** finite-dimensional solvable Lie algebra
    under these field hypotheses has such an ideal, obtained by pulling back
    a hyperplane from the nonzero abelianization. The zero algebra is excluded.
21. `thm-lies-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. A finite-dimensional solvable
    Lie algebra acting on a nonzero finite-dimensional vector space over an
    algebraically closed field of characteristic zero has a common eigenvector.
    LANDMARK.
22. `cor-simultaneous-upper-triangularization-of-solvable-lie-algebra-representations` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; induct on the quotient by the common eigenline.
23. `cor-finite-dimensional-irreducible-representations-of-a-solvable-complex-lie-algebra-are-one-dimensional` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
24. `thm-derived-algebra-of-a-solvable-linear-lie-algebra-is-nilpotent` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; in the triangular basis, commutators are strictly
    upper triangular, then apply Engel.
25. `cor-the-derived-algebra-of-a-finite-dimensional-solvable-lie-algebra-is-nilpotent-in-characteristic-zero` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; pass to the adjoint representation and handle its
    central kernel explicitly, including scalar extension when the base field
    is not algebraically closed.
26. `thm-lies-criterion-for-solvability-by-the-derived-algebra` — **theorem**
    `[LL]`; in finite dimension and characteristic zero,
    $\mathfrak g$ is solvable iff $[\mathfrak g,\mathfrak g]$ is nilpotent.

**Radical and nilradical**

27. `def-radical-of-a-finite-dimensional-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    the largest solvable ideal, after existence is proved in item 28.
28. `thm-sum-of-solvable-ideals-is-solvable` — **theorem** `[LA]`; a finite
    sum suffices by finite dimension and extension closure, yielding the
    radical.
29. `prop-the-radical-is-characteristic-and-the-radical-quotient-has-zero-radical` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.
30. `def-nilradical-of-a-finite-dimensional-lie-algebra` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; the largest nilpotent ideal, licensed by item 31 under the printed
    characteristic-zero hypothesis.
31. `thm-existence-and-characteristicity-of-the-nilradical-in-characteristic-zero` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; sums of nilpotent ideals are nilpotent in this setting.
32. `thm-the-commutator-of-a-lie-algebra-with-its-radical-lies-in-the-nilradical` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $[\mathfrak g,\operatorname{rad}\mathfrak g]\subseteq
    \operatorname{nilrad}\mathfrak g$.
33. `cor-the-derived-algebra-of-the-radical-lies-in-the-nilradical` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
34. `prop-derivations-preserve-the-nilradical-in-characteristic-zero` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.
35. `def-semisimple-lie-algebra-by-vanishing-radical` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    this bridge definition is expanded and related to the Killing form in
    DG-29.
36. `def-reductive-lie-algebra-by-semisimple-derived-algebra-and-center` —
    **definition** `[LN]` in characteristic zero; equivalent formulations are
    deferred until semisimplicity is developed.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-every-solvable-lie-algebra-is-nilpotent` — **false statement** `[LL]`.
- `fs-an-extension-of-a-nilpotent-lie-algebra-by-a-nilpotent-lie-algebra-is-always-nilpotent` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the affine algebra is the basic witness.
- `fs-it-is-enough-that-a-chosen-basis-act-nilpotently-in-engels-theorem` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; every algebra element must act nilpotently.
- `fs-lies-theorem-holds-over-every-field-and-in-every-characteristic` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; its algebraic-closure/characteristic-zero
  hypotheses are essential.
- `fs-every-irreducible-representation-of-a-solvable-real-lie-algebra-is-one-dimensional` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; real rotation gives a two-dimensional example.
- `fs-the-nilradical-is-defined-as-the-set-of-all-ad-nilpotent-elements` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; that set need not be a linear ideal in the
  asserted generality.

### B page — `solvable-and-nilpotent-lie-algebra-examples`

1. `ex-abelian-lie-algebras-are-nilpotent-of-class-one` — **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-the-heisenberg-lie-algebra-is-two-step-nilpotent` — **example** `[LL]`.
3. `ex-strictly-upper-triangular-matrices-form-a-nilpotent-lie-algebra` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
4. `ex-upper-triangular-matrices-form-a-solvable-nonnilpotent-lie-algebra` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
5. `ex-the-two-dimensional-affine-lie-algebra-is-solvable-not-nilpotent` —
   **example** `[LL]`.
6. `ex-the-euclidean-motion-lie-algebra-is-solvable-in-dimension-two` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
7. `ex-derived-and-lower-central-series-of-a-filiform-lie-algebra` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
8. `ex-radical-and-nilradical-of-the-affine-lie-algebra` — **example** `[LL]`.
9. `cex-a-nilpotent-by-nilpotent-extension-that-is-not-nilpotent` —
   **counterexample** `[LL]`.
10. `cex-a-two-dimensional-irreducible-real-representation-of-an-abelian-lie-algebra` —
    **counterexample** `[LA]`; rotation.
11. `cex-positive-characteristic-failure-of-lies-theorem` — **counterexample**
    `[LL]` with a source-cited finite-dimensional representation.
12. `ex-engels-theorem-on-strictly-upper-triangular-matrices` — **example**
    `[LL]`.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, Ch. I §5 “Solvable Lie
  Algebras and Lie's Theorem” and §6 “Nilpotent Lie Algebras and Engel's
  Theorem.”** Primary textbook treatment for items 1–36.
- **Kirillov, *Introduction to Lie Groups and Lie Algebras*, Ch. 6 sections
  on ideals/commutants, solvable Lie algebras and Lie's theorem, nilpotent Lie
  algebras and Engel's theorem, and radical/nilradical.** Independent full
  lecture-text treatment and examples.
- **Etingof, Lie algebra notes, sections “Engel's theorem,” “Lie's theorem,”
  and “Solvable and nilpotent Lie algebras.”** Independent induction and
  triangularization proofs, used to make every field hypothesis explicit.
- **Humphreys, *Introduction to Lie Algebras and Representation Theory*, Ch.
  2 “Ideals and Homomorphisms” and Ch. 3 “Solvable and Nilpotent Lie
  Algebras.”** Further standard statement control; only legally accessible
  locators may appear in final item citations.

### Proof strategy and traps

The series definitions are compared by explicit inclusions. Engel's common-
zero-vector lemma is proved by induction on the operator algebra; it yields a
complete invariant flag and then the central series. Lie's theorem similarly
uses a codimension-one ideal, its common weight space, and the trace/commutator
argument that forces invariance under the remaining generator. The field
hypotheses enter precisely there.

The radical exists because a finite-dimensional sum of solvable ideals is a
finite sum and extensions preserve solvability. Nilradical existence is more
delicate and is stated only in the characteristic-zero setting supported by
the sources. No set of “nilpotent elements” is assumed linear.

**Well-definedness discharged:** characteristic ideal series and extension
bounds (items 1–13), quantification over every element in Engel (14–19),
weight/scalar-extension hypotheses in Lie's theorem (20–26), and maximal-ideal
existence for radical/nilradical (27–36).
**Choice:** all finite-dimensional induction and flag constructions use finite
choice only and are ZF.
**Forward references:** DG-29 studies the semisimple quotient, Cartan criteria,
Levi decomposition, Ado, and integration of Lie algebras.

## DG-29. Semisimple Lie Algebras, Cohomology, Levi Theory, and Integration

**A page:** `semisimple-lie-algebras-cohomology-and-levi-theory`
**B page:** `semisimple-and-levi-theory-examples`
**Requires:** DG-26–DG-28; HA pages `chain-complexes-and-homology` and
`long-exact-sequences-in-homology`; planned topology
`covering-spaces-and-lifting` for the simply connected/global integration
statements.

This pair closes the existence side of Lie theory before roots classify the
semisimple objects. Algebraic structure results are finite-dimensional over a
field of characteristic zero unless a narrower hypothesis is printed. Lie
group integration statements are real.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Killing form and Cartan criteria**

1. `def-simple-semisimple-and-reductive-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   simple means nonabelian with no nonzero proper ideals; semisimple means
   zero radical; reductive initially means a direct sum of a semisimple
   algebra and its center.
2. `def-trace-form-of-a-representation` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $B_\rho(X,Y)=\operatorname{tr}(\rho(X)\rho(Y))$.
3. `def-killing-form` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $B(X,Y)=\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{ad}_X\operatorname{ad}_Y)$.
4. `prop-trace-forms-are-symmetric-and-invariant` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
   $B_\rho([X,Y],Z)=B_\rho(X,[Y,Z])$ by cyclicity of trace.
5. `lem-orthogonal-complement-of-an-ideal-under-an-invariant-form-is-an-ideal` —
   **lemma** `[LA]`.
6. `thm-cartans-criterion-for-solvability` — **theorem** `[LA]`. In finite
   dimension and characteristic zero,
   $\mathfrak g$ is solvable iff
   $B(\mathfrak g,[\mathfrak g,\mathfrak g])=0$; include the more general
   faithful-linear trace criterion used in the proof. LANDMARK.
7. `thm-cartans-criterion-for-semisimplicity` — **theorem** `[LA]`. A finite-
   dimensional characteristic-zero Lie algebra is semisimple iff its Killing
   form is nondegenerate. LANDMARK.
8. `cor-semisimple-lie-algebras-are-centerless-and-perfect` — **corollary**
   `[LA]`.
9. `thm-semisimple-lie-algebra-is-a-direct-sum-of-simple-ideals` — **theorem**
   `[LA]`; use a minimal ideal and its Killing-orthogonal complement,
   inducting on dimension.
10. `prop-ideals-and-quotients-of-semisimple-lie-algebras-are-semisimple` —
    **proposition** `[LA]` and split as sums of simple factors.
11. `thm-equivalent-characterizations-of-reductive-lie-algebras-in-characteristic-zero` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; radical equals center; adjoint representation is
    completely reducible; and $\mathfrak g=Z(\mathfrak g)\oplus
    [\mathfrak g,\mathfrak g]$ with semisimple derived algebra.

**Complete reducibility, derivations, and Casimir**

12. `def-casimir-operator-of-a-representation-relative-to-a-nondegenerate-invariant-form` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; sum $\sum_i\rho(x_i)\rho(x^i)$ over dual bases.
13. `lem-the-casimir-operator-is-basis-independent-and-intertwines-the-representation` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`.
14. `thm-weyls-complete-reducibility` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Every finite-
    dimensional representation of a finite-dimensional semisimple Lie algebra
    over a characteristic-zero field is completely reducible (reduce by
    scalar extension where appropriate). LANDMARK.
15. `cor-adjoint-representation-splits-every-ideal-in-a-semisimple-lie-algebra` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
16. `thm-every-derivation-of-a-semisimple-lie-algebra-is-inner` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; identify a derivation with a cocycle or split the semidirect
    extension via complete reducibility.
17. `cor-automorphism-lie-algebra-of-a-semisimple-lie-algebra-is-ad-g` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.

**Chevalley–Eilenberg cohomology and Whitehead lemmas**

18. `def-chevalley-eilenberg-cochains` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $C^p(\mathfrak g,V)=\operatorname{Alt}^p(\mathfrak g,V)$ for a
    $\mathfrak g$-module $V$.
19. `def-chevalley-eilenberg-differential` — **definition** `[LN]`; print both
    the representation-action sum and the bracket sum with exact signs.
20. `lem-chevalley-eilenberg-differential-squares-to-zero` — **lemma** `[LA]`;
    action terms cancel by representation compatibility and bracket terms by
    Jacobi.
21. `def-lie-algebra-cohomology` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $H^p(\mathfrak g,V)$ is the HA cohomology of the Chevalley–Eilenberg
    complex, not a newly defined quotient theory.
22. `prop-zero-th-lie-algebra-cohomology-is-the-invariant-subspace` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`: $H^0(\mathfrak g,V)=V^{\mathfrak g}$.
23. `prop-first-lie-algebra-cohomology-is-derivations-modulo-inner-derivations` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; specify the module-dependent derivation equation.
24. `prop-second-lie-algebra-cohomology-classifies-abelian-extensions` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; construct cocycles from linear splittings, prove
    change of splitting adds a coboundary, and construct the inverse extension.
25. `thm-first-whitehead-lemma` — **theorem** `[LA]`: for semisimple finite-
    dimensional $\mathfrak g$ and finite-dimensional $V$ in characteristic
    zero, $H^1(\mathfrak g,V)=0$.
26. `thm-second-whitehead-lemma` — **theorem** `[LA]` under the same
    hypotheses: $H^2(\mathfrak g,V)=0$.
27. `prop-long-exact-sequence-in-lie-algebra-cohomology` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; a short exact sequence of $\mathfrak g$-modules gives a degreewise
    short exact CE sequence (finite-dimensional $\mathfrak g$), then cite
    HA's `thm-long-exact-sequence-in-cohomology`.

**Levi–Malcev and Ado**

28. `def-levi-subalgebra-and-levi-decomposition` — **definition** `[LN]`; a
    semisimple complement $\mathfrak s$ to the radical, so
    $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak s\ltimes\operatorname{rad}\mathfrak g$.
29. `thm-levi-decomposition` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Every finite-dimensional
    characteristic-zero Lie algebra has a Levi subalgebra. LANDMARK.
30. `thm-malcev-conjugacy-of-levi-subalgebras` — **theorem** `[LA]`; any two
    are conjugate by an automorphism generated by
    $\exp(\operatorname{ad}x)$ with $x$ in the nilradical (precise generated
    subgroup stated).
31. `cor-levi-decomposition-is-not-canonical-but-its-semisimple-factor-is-unique-up-to-inner-unipotent-conjugacy` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
32. `thm-ados-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Every finite-dimensional Lie
    algebra over a characteristic-zero field has a faithful finite-dimensional
    representation; it can be chosen so that the nilradical acts by nilpotent
    endomorphisms. The proof records the Zassenhaus extension step rather than
    assuming a matrix realization. LANDMARK.
33. `cor-every-finite-dimensional-characteristic-zero-lie-algebra-is-isomorphic-to-a-matrix-lie-algebra` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.

**Lie II, Lie III, and global integration**

34. `thm-integrating-a-lie-algebra-homomorphism-from-a-simply-connected-lie-group` —
    **theorem** `[LA]` (Lie II). If $G$ is connected and simply connected,
    every Lie algebra homomorphism
    $\operatorname{Lie}(G)\to\operatorname{Lie}(H)$ integrates uniquely to a
    smooth Lie-group homomorphism $G\to H$. LANDMARK.
35. `thm-lies-third-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Every finite-dimensional
    real Lie algebra is the Lie algebra of a connected simply connected Lie
    group, unique up to isomorphism. LANDMARK.
36. `thm-equivalence-between-simply-connected-lie-groups-and-finite-dimensional-real-lie-algebras` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; Lie and differential are quasi-inverse on objects and
    homomorphisms.
37. `cor-every-connected-lie-group-is-a-quotient-of-its-simply-connected-lie-group-by-a-discrete-central-subgroup` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; the kernel of the universal covering homomorphism is
    discrete central.
38. `thm-exponential-is-a-diffeomorphism-for-connected-simply-connected-nilpotent-lie-groups` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; the BCH series terminates, induction through the center
    gives global bijectivity, and smooth inverse follows in exponential
    coordinates.
39. `cor-connected-nilpotent-lie-groups-are-central-discrete-quotients-of-their-bch-groups` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`.
40. `prop-connected-lie-groups-with-isomorphic-lie-algebras-are-locally-isomorphic-but-need-not-be-globally-isomorphic` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; the simply connected integrations agree and global
    groups differ by discrete central subgroups.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-a-lie-algebra-is-semisimple-iff-it-has-zero-center` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; centerless solvable examples exist.
- `fs-the-killing-form-is-nondegenerate-for-every-reductive-lie-algebra` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; it vanishes on the center.
- `fs-every-finite-dimensional-lie-algebra-representation-is-completely-reducible` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; semisimplicity of the algebra matters.
- `fs-h-two-classifies-all-nonabelian-extensions-with-no-extra-data` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; item 24 concerns abelian extensions with a fixed
  module action.
- `fs-a-levi-subalgebra-is-unique-as-a-literal-subalgebra` — **false
  statement** `[LL]`; only conjugacy is promised.
- `fs-isomorphic-lie-algebras-determine-isomorphic-connected-lie-groups` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; global central quotients differ.

### B page — `semisimple-and-levi-theory-examples`

1. `ex-killing-form-of-sl-two` — **example** `[LA]`.
2. `ex-classical-simple-lie-algebras-and-their-killing-forms` — **example**
   `[LA]`, before classification only as verified examples.
3. `ex-a-reductive-algebra-with-degenerate-killing-form` — **example** `[LL]`:
   $\mathfrak{gl}_n$.
4. `ex-direct-sum-decomposition-of-a-semisimple-lie-algebra` — **example**
   `[LL]`.
5. `ex-first-cohomology-with-trivial-coefficients-is-the-dual-abelianization` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
6. `ex-an-abelian-extension-from-a-two-cocycle` — **example** `[LA]`; the
   Heisenberg algebra.
7. `ex-a-levi-decomposition-of-the-euclidean-motion-algebra` — **example**

8. `ex-distinct-conjugate-levi-subalgebras` — **example** `[LA]`.
9. `ex-su-two-and-so-three-have-isomorphic-real-lie-algebras-locally-but-different-global-groups` —

10. `ex-the-bch-group-of-a-nilpotent-lie-algebra` — **example** `[LA]`.
11. `cex-centerless-does-not-imply-semisimple` — **counterexample** `[LL]`.
12. `cex-the-circle-and-line-have-isomorphic-one-dimensional-lie-algebras-but-are-not-isomorphic-lie-groups` —
    **counterexample** `[LL]`.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, Ch. I §§3 “Field Extensions
  and the Killing Form,” 7 “Cartan's Criterion for Semisimplicity,” 8
  “Examples of Semisimple Lie Algebras,” 14 “Automorphisms and Derivations,”
  and Appendix B §§1 “Levi Decomposition,” 2 “Lie's Third Theorem,” and 3
  “Ado's Theorem.”** Primary textbook route for Killing/Cartan/semisimple
  structure, derivations, and the real Levi/Lie III/Ado results. It is not
  claimed as the source of the CE complex or both Whitehead lemmas.
- **Etingof, *18.745: Lie Groups and Lie Algebras I*, §§47.1–47.3 and the
  cited results there.** The CE complex, both Whitehead statements, and
  extension classification back items 18–27; §47.1 Proposition 47.1 and
  §47.3 Proposition 47.3 are the exact controls.
- **MIT 18.745, Lecture 23, “Decomposition of Semisimple Lie Algebras,”
  Theorems 23.5 (Levi) and 23.6 (Mal'cev).** Independent
  characteristic-zero proof source for Levi existence (items 28–29) and a
  statement-level cross-check for Mal'cev conjugacy (items 30–31); the
  obtained lecture prints the latter statement but not its proof. Milne
  supplies that proof. Kirillov Theorem 6.25 is retained only as a
  statement-level Levi cross-check.
- **Milne, *Lie Algebras, Algebraic Groups, and Lie Groups*, §6 “Levi
  subalgebras” through “Ado's theorem,” Theorem 6.27.** Exact arbitrary-
  characteristic-zero-field source for item 32; this scope is wider than the
  real Knapp and complex Etingof formulations.
- **Knapp Appendix B and Kirillov Ch. 3 §3.8 “Fundamental theorems of Lie
  theory.”** Independent controls on Lie II/III and the discrete-central-
  quotient statement.

### Proof strategy and traps

Cartan solvability uses scalar extension, Jordan decomposition of endomorphisms,
the trace criterion, and Engel; semisimplicity then uses the Killing-
orthogonal complement of the radical. Weyl and Whitehead use a Casimir
operator and induction across irreducible factors. CE signs are checked by a
direct $d^2$ cancellation before HA is invoked.

Levi can be proved by induction on a minimal ideal, using Whitehead $H^2=0$
for the abelian extension case; Malcev uses $H^1=0$ to conjugate complements.
Ado uses PBW to construct a finite-codimensional invariant ideal in
$U(\mathfrak g)$, treats the solvable radical faithfully, and combines it with
a Levi factor. Lie II integrates the graph distribution and uses simple
connectedness for path independence. Lie III embeds by Ado, integrates the
matrix subalgebra by DG-26, and passes to the universal cover.

**Well-definedness discharged:** trace/invariant orthogonals and field
hypotheses (items 1–17), CE differential and extension/splitting choices
(18–27), noncanonical Levi complements and finite quotient representation in
Ado (28–33), and covering kernels/global uniqueness (34–40).
**Choice:** finite-dimensional algebra is ZF. CE invokes HA's ledger.
Simply connected integrations and discrete-central quotient statements inherit
the unresolved topology covering-space ledger.
**Unresolved seams:** HA-1/HA-4 precede 18–27; the topology covering pages
precede 34–40. These are not re-minted.

## DG-30. Cartan Subalgebras and Root-Space Decompositions

**A page:** `cartan-subalgebras-and-root-space-decompositions`
**B page:** `cartan-subalgebra-and-root-space-examples`
**Requires:** DG-27–DG-29 and abstract algebra X-2
`the-spectral-theorem-and-singular-value-decomposition`, which supplies the
finite-dimensional additive Jordan--Chevalley decomposition. R-5 forbids the
former local fallback.

The main classification block now works over $\mathbb C$ (more generally an
algebraically closed characteristic-zero field where printed). A Cartan
subalgebra of an arbitrary Lie algebra is **nilpotent and self-normalizing**;
“maximal toral” is proved equivalent only in the semisimple setting.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Jordan decomposition and Cartan subalgebras**

1. `rem-additive-jordan-chevalley-is-supplied-by-x-two` — **remark** `[LN]`;
   cite X-2's stable finite-dimensional endomorphism definition and theorem.
2. `rem-jordan-chevalley-parts-agree-under-the-adjoint-representation` —
   **remark** `[LA]`; records the exact cited operator decomposition consumed
   by items 3--4. No operator definition or theorem is re-minted here.
3. `def-abstract-jordan-decomposition-in-a-lie-algebra` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; $x=x_s+x_n$ with commuting parts such that
   $\operatorname{ad}x_s$ is semisimple and $\operatorname{ad}x_n$ nilpotent.
4. `thm-jordan-decomposition-lies-inside-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; the endomorphism decomposition of
   $\operatorname{ad}x$ equals $\operatorname{ad}x_s+\operatorname{ad}x_n$
   for unique $x_s,x_n\in\mathfrak g$.
5. `def-normalizer-of-a-lie-subalgebra` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $N_\mathfrak g(\mathfrak h)=\{x:[x,\mathfrak h]\subseteq\mathfrak h\}$.
6. `def-cartan-subalgebra-of-a-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`; a
   nilpotent self-normalizing subalgebra.
7. `def-toral-and-maximal-toral-subalgebra` — **definition** `[LN]`; toral is
   abelian and consists of semisimple elements.
8. `def-regular-element-and-rank-of-a-complex-lie-algebra` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; regular means its centralizer has minimal dimension, and rank is
   that dimension in the semisimple case.
9. `thm-centralizer-of-a-regular-semisimple-element-is-a-cartan-subalgebra` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`.
10. `thm-cartan-subalgebras-exist-in-complex-semisimple-lie-algebras` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; regular semisimple elements exist as the complement of
    a proper algebraic discriminant locus.
11. `thm-cartan-subalgebras-of-complex-semisimple-lie-algebras-are-exactly-maximal-toral-subalgebras` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`. LANDMARK.
12. `thm-cartan-subalgebras-of-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra-are-conjugate` —
    **theorem** `[LA]` under the connected adjoint group; hence rank is
    independent of the chosen Cartan subalgebra.

**Root-space decomposition**

13. `def-root-and-root-space-relative-to-a-cartan-subalgebra` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; for $0\ne\alpha\in\mathfrak h^*$,
    $\mathfrak g_\alpha=\{x:[h,x]=\alpha(h)x\ \forall h\in\mathfrak h\}$.
14. `thm-root-space-decomposition-of-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak h\oplus\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Phi}
    \mathfrak g_\alpha$. LANDMARK.
15. `prop-brackets-of-root-spaces` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $[\mathfrak g_\alpha,\mathfrak g_\beta]\subseteq
    \mathfrak g_{\alpha+\beta}$, interpreting a nonexistent root space as
    zero.
16. `prop-killing-form-orthogonality-of-root-spaces` — **proposition** `[LA]`;
    $B(\mathfrak g_\alpha,\mathfrak g_\beta)=0$ unless
    $\alpha+\beta=0$, and $B|_\mathfrak h$ is nondegenerate.
17. `cor-opposite-root-spaces-pair-nondegenerately` — **corollary** `[LL]`;
    in particular $-\alpha$ is a root whenever $\alpha$ is.
18. `def-killing-dual-vector-of-a-root` — **definition** `[LN]`; $H_\alpha$
    satisfies $B(H_\alpha,h)=\alpha(h)$.
19. `prop-bracket-of-opposite-root-spaces-is-the-root-line-in-the-cartan-subalgebra` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $[\mathfrak g_\alpha,\mathfrak g_{-\alpha}]=\mathbb C H_\alpha$.
20. `prop-no-nonzero-multiple-of-a-root-beyond-sign-is-a-root` —
    **proposition** `[LA]` after the $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ analysis; this proves
    reducedness rather than assuming it.

**Root $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ theory and strings**

21. `def-coroot-of-a-lie-algebra-root` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $h_\alpha=2H_\alpha/B(H_\alpha,H_\alpha)$.
22. `thm-root-sl-two-triple` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Choose
    $e_\alpha\in\mathfrak g_\alpha$ and
    $f_\alpha\in\mathfrak g_{-\alpha}$ so
    $[e_\alpha,f_\alpha]=h_\alpha$,
    $[h_\alpha,e_\alpha]=2e_\alpha$, and
    $[h_\alpha,f_\alpha]=-2f_\alpha$.
23. `thm-finite-dimensional-representations-of-sl-two` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    every irreducible has highest weight $m\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$, dimension
    $m+1$, and weights $m,m-2,\ldots,-m$; arbitrary finite-dimensional
    modules are completely reducible. This focused theorem is proved here for
    root strings and expanded in DG-32.
24. `thm-root-string-property` — **theorem** `[LA]`. For roots $\alpha,\beta$,
    the roots $\beta+k\alpha$ form one consecutive string
    $\beta-p\alpha,\ldots,\beta+q\alpha$ and
    $p-q=\beta(h_\alpha)\in\mathbb Z$.
25. `cor-cartan-integers-are-integral` — **corollary** `[LL]`:
    $\langle\beta,\alpha^\vee\rangle=\beta(h_\alpha)\in\mathbb Z$.
26. `thm-root-spaces-of-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra-are-one-dimensional` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; use the root-string $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ module and
    Killing pairing.
27. `cor-the-only-scalar-multiples-of-a-root-that-are-roots-are-plus-or-minus-the-root` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; reconciles item 20 after its proof dependency.
28. `def-root-reflection-from-a-coroot` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $s_\alpha(\lambda)=\lambda-\lambda(h_\alpha)\alpha$.
29. `thm-root-reflections-preserve-the-root-set` — **theorem** `[LA]`; reverse
    each $\alpha$-string using $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ weights.
30. `prop-root-reflections-are-induced-by-inner-automorphisms` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; the product
    $\exp(\operatorname{ad}e_\alpha)
    \exp(-\operatorname{ad}f_\alpha)
    \exp(\operatorname{ad}e_\alpha)$ normalizes $\mathfrak h$ and restricts
    to $s_\alpha$.
31. `thm-roots-of-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra-form-a-reduced-crystallographic-root-system` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; finiteness, spanning, reducedness, reflection
    invariance, and integrality are each cited to items above. LANDMARK.

**Consequences**

32. `prop-dimension-formula-from-roots` — **proposition** `[LL]`:
    $\dim\mathfrak g=\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak g+|\Phi|$.
33. `prop-the-center-is-the-common-kernel-of-all-roots-inside-the-cartan-subalgebra` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`; it is zero for semisimple $\mathfrak g$, so roots
    span $\mathfrak h^*$.
34. `def-regular-root-hyperplanes` — **definition** `[LN]`; regular elements
    of $\mathfrak h$ avoid $\bigcup_{\alpha\in\Phi}\ker\alpha$.
35. `prop-centralizer-dimension-from-vanishing-roots` — **proposition** `[LA]`;
    $Z_\mathfrak g(h)=\mathfrak h\oplus
    \bigoplus_{\alpha(h)=0}\mathfrak g_\alpha$.
36. `cor-regular-elements-form-a-dense-zariski-open-subset-of-a-cartan-subalgebra` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; this is a finite hyperplane complement, so no
    algebraic-geometry machinery beyond polynomials is invoked.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-a-cartan-subalgebra-of-an-arbitrary-lie-algebra-means-a-maximal-abelian-subalgebra` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the general definition is nilpotent and self-
  normalizing.
- `fs-every-element-of-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra-is-semisimple` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; root vectors are nilpotent.
- `fs-root-spaces-can-have-arbitrary-dimension-in-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; they are one-dimensional.
- `fs-if-alpha-and-beta-are-roots-then-alpha-plus-beta-is-always-a-root` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; item 15 is only an inclusion when the sum root
  exists.
- `fs-all-integer-multiples-of-a-root-are-roots` — **false statement** `[LL]`;
  the root system is reduced.
- `fs-the-root-space-decomposition-classifies-real-semisimple-lie-algebras-with-no-extra-data` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; real forms are DG-34.

### B page — `cartan-subalgebra-and-root-space-examples`

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, Ch. II §§1 “Classical
  Root-space Decompositions,” 2 “Existence of Cartan Subalgebras,” 3
  “Uniqueness of Cartan Subalgebras,” and 4 “Roots.”** Primary textbook route
  for items 1–36.
- **Kirillov, *Introduction to Lie Groups and Lie Algebras*, semisimple Lie
  algebra chapters covering Cartan subalgebras, root decomposition,
  $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ representations, and root strings.** Independent full
  lecture-text treatment.
- **Etingof, Lie algebra notes, sections on Cartan subalgebras, roots of
  semisimple Lie algebras, and $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ theory.** Independent proof
  source for items 9–31.
- **Humphreys, *Introduction to Lie Algebras and Representation Theory*, Chs.
  7–10 (Cartan subalgebras, root-space decomposition, and root strings).**
  Standard statement control, subject to legal source availability at build
  time.

### Proof strategy and traps

Jordan decomposition is first a polynomial theorem for one endomorphism and
then shown to remain inside $\operatorname{ad}\mathfrak g$ using
semisimplicity. A regular semisimple centralizer supplies a Cartan subalgebra;
conjugacy uses the connected adjoint group and a dimension/open-orbit argument.

Simultaneous diagonalization of $\operatorname{ad}\mathfrak h$ gives the root
decomposition. Killing invariance pairs only opposite roots. Each root then
generates an $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ triple, and finite-dimensional
$\mathfrak{sl}_2$ representation theory proves integrality, root strings,
one-dimensional root spaces, reducedness, and reflection invariance. None of
those is smuggled into the definition of a root.

**Well-definedness discharged:** endomorphism versus abstract Jordan parts
(items 1–4), general versus semisimple Cartan definitions (5–12), Killing-
dual roots and bracket spaces (13–20), normalization of coroots and reflection
integrality (21–31), and chosen-Cartan independence of rank (32–36).
**Choice:** finite-dimensional eigen-decomposition and finite root arguments
are ZF.
**Forward references:** DG-31 abstracts and classifies the resulting root
systems; DG-32 builds highest-weight representations; DG-34 treats real and
restricted roots separately.

## DG-31. Root Systems, Dynkin Diagrams, and Cartan–Killing Classification

**A page:** `root-systems-dynkin-diagrams-and-cartan-killing-classification`
**B page:** `root-system-and-dynkin-diagram-examples`
**Requires:** DG-27, DG-29, and DG-30; published Euclidean inner-product and
finite-graph/tree machinery.

This pair proves both halves of classification: a complex semisimple Lie
algebra determines a Dynkin diagram, and every finite-type Dynkin diagram is
realized by a unique complex semisimple Lie algebra. Root systems alone do not
classify global Lie groups; DG-33 adds the lattice data needed for that.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Abstract root systems and Weyl groups**

1. `def-reduced-crystallographic-euclidean-root-system` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; a finite spanning subset
   \(\Phi\subset V\setminus\{0\}\) of a finite-dimensional real inner-
   product space, with $\Phi\cap\mathbb R\alpha=\{\pm\alpha\}$,
   $s_\alpha\Phi=\Phi$, and
   $2(\beta,\alpha)/(\alpha,\alpha)\in\mathbb Z$.
2. `def-rank-and-isomorphism-of-root-systems` — **definition** `[LN]`.
3. `def-coroot-and-dual-root-system` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $\alpha^\vee=2\alpha/(\alpha,\alpha)$ and
   $\Phi^\vee=\{\alpha^\vee\}$.
4. `def-weyl-group-of-a-root-system` — **definition** `[LN]`; subgroup of
   $O(V)$ generated by the root reflections.
5. `prop-the-weyl-group-is-finite-and-acts-faithfully-on-the-root-system` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; inject into the finite permutation group of $\Phi$.
6. `def-reducible-and-irreducible-root-system` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   orthogonal disjoint union versus no such decomposition.
7. `prop-root-systems-decompose-uniquely-into-irreducible-components` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; connected components of the nonorthogonality graph.
8. `thm-rank-two-root-system-classification` — **theorem** `[LA]`; irreducible
   rank two gives $A_2$, $B_2=C_2$, or $G_2$, while the reducible case is
   $A_1\sqcup A_1$; derive angles and length ratios from the two Cartan
   integers.

**Positive roots, simple roots, and chambers**

9. `def-positive-system-and-base-of-simple-roots` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   choose a regular linear functional, call positive roots those on which it
   is positive, and indecomposable positive roots simple.
10. `thm-simple-roots-form-a-basis-and-every-root-has-one-sign-of-integral-coordinates` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; every positive root is a nonnegative integer sum of
    simple roots. LANDMARK.
11. `prop-distinct-simple-roots-have-nonpositive-inner-product` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.
12. `def-height-of-a-root-and-highest-root` — **definition** `[LN]`; highest
    roots are defined componentwise after a base is chosen.
13. `def-root-lattice-coroot-lattice-weight-lattice-and-coweight-lattice` —
    **definition** `[LN]`:
    $Q\subseteq P$ and $Q^\vee\subseteq P^\vee$ with the integral pairing
    conditions.
14. `def-fundamental-weights` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\langle\omega_i,\alpha_j^\vee\rangle=\delta_{ij}$.
15. `def-open-and-closed-weyl-chambers` — **definition** `[LN]`; connected
    components of the complement of root hyperplanes and their closures.
16. `thm-the-weyl-group-acts-simply-transitively-on-weyl-chambers` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; reflect across walls to reach the fundamental chamber,
    and use a length argument for uniqueness.
17. `prop-every-positive-system-is-weyl-conjugate-and-bases-correspond-to-chambers` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
18. `def-length-and-longest-element-of-a-finite-weyl-group` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; length is the number of positive roots sent negative, and the
    unique longest element sends the chosen positive system to its negative.

**Cartan matrices and Dynkin diagrams**

19. `def-cartan-matrix-of-a-based-root-system` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $a_{ij}=\langle\alpha_j,\alpha_i^\vee\rangle$, so rows index coroots.
20. `prop-finite-type-cartan-matrix-properties` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
    diagonal entries $2$, off-diagonal nonpositive integers, simultaneous
    zero pattern, and positive-definite symmetrizability.
21. `def-dynkin-diagram-with-edge-multiplicity-and-arrow-convention` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; $a_{ij}a_{ji}$ determines zero through triple
    edges, and an arrow points toward the shorter root.
22. `thm-a-based-root-system-is-determined-up-to-isomorphism-by-its-cartan-matrix` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; reconstruct the Gram matrix up to componentwise scale.
23. `prop-irreducibility-corresponds-to-connectedness-of-the-dynkin-diagram` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
24. `lem-dynkin-diagrams-of-irreducible-finite-root-systems-are-trees-with-controlled-branching` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; the positive-definite Gram form excludes cycles and
    excessive multiple edges/branching.
25. `thm-classification-of-irreducible-reduced-crystallographic-root-systems` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`. The complete nonduplicate list is
    \[
    A_n\ (n\ge1),\quad B_n\ (n\ge2),\quad C_n\ (n\ge3),\quad
    D_n\ (n\ge4),\quad E_6,E_7,E_8,F_4,G_2,
    \]
    with $B_1=C_1=A_1$, $B_2=C_2$,
    $D_2=A_1\sqcup A_1$, and $D_3=A_3$ recorded. LANDMARK.
26. `thm-existence-of-each-classified-root-system` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    construct the classical systems in Euclidean coordinates and exceptional
    systems from their Cartan/Gram matrices, then verify the axioms.
27. `prop-duality-exchanges-b-n-and-c-n-and-fixes-the-other-types` —
    **proposition** `[LA]` (up to isomorphism, including $F_4,G_2$ self-dual
    with long/short roots exchanged).

**Serre presentation and Lie-algebra classification**

28. `def-free-lie-algebra-on-a-vector-space` — **definition** `[LN]`; the Lie
    subalgebra generated by $V$ inside $T(V)_{\mathrm{Lie}}$, licensed by PBW.
29. `thm-universal-property-of-the-free-lie-algebra` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    every linear map $V\to\mathfrak g$ extends uniquely to a Lie homomorphism.
30. `def-lie-algebra-presented-by-generators-and-relations` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; quotient of the free Lie algebra by the ideal generated by the
    relations, with quotient well-definedness cited.
31. `def-serre-lie-algebra-of-a-finite-type-cartan-matrix` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; generators $e_i,f_i,h_i$ and relations
    $[h_i,h_j]=0$, $[h_i,e_j]=a_{ij}e_j$,
    $[h_i,f_j]=-a_{ij}f_j$, $[e_i,f_j]=\delta_{ij}h_i$, and
    $(\operatorname{ad}e_i)^{1-a_{ij}}e_j=
    (\operatorname{ad}f_i)^{1-a_{ij}}f_j=0$ for $i\ne j$.
32. `thm-serre-presentation-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. The algebra from a
    finite-type Cartan matrix is finite-dimensional semisimple with that root
    system, and every complex semisimple algebra has this presentation after
    choosing simple-root vectors. LANDMARK.
33. `thm-isomorphism-theorem-for-complex-semisimple-lie-algebras` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; isomorphic root systems imply isomorphic Lie algebras by the Serre
    presentation.
34. `thm-existence-theorem-for-complex-semisimple-lie-algebras` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; every reduced crystallographic root system occurs.
35. `thm-cartan-killing-classification-of-complex-simple-lie-algebras` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; connected diagrams in item 25 correspond exactly to
    finite-dimensional complex simple Lie algebras. LANDMARK.
36. `cor-complex-semisimple-lie-algebras-are-classified-by-finite-disjoint-unions-of-dynkin-diagrams` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
37. `prop-classical-types-correspond-to-sl-so-and-sp` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $A_n=\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1}$,
    $B_n=\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1}$,
    $C_n=\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}$,
    $D_n=\mathfrak{so}_{2n}$, with low-rank coincidences reconciled.
38. `prop-dimensions-of-the-exceptional-simple-lie-algebras` — **proposition**
    `[LL]`: $78,133,248,52,14$ for $E_6,E_7,E_8,F_4,G_2$, calculated as
    rank plus number of roots.
39. `rem-dynkin-diagrams-do-not-classify-global-lie-groups` — **remark**
    `[LN]`; they classify complex semisimple Lie algebras. Global connected
    groups require a lattice/central quotient, and real algebras require a
    real-form diagram.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-every-finite-reflection-invariant-set-of-vectors-is-a-crystallographic-root-system` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; integrality and reducedness are extra axioms.

- `fs-two-connected-lie-groups-with-the-same-dynkin-diagram-are-isomorphic` —

### B page — `root-system-and-dynkin-diagram-examples`

1. `ex-root-system-a-one` — **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-root-systems-a-two-b-two-and-g-two` — **example** `[LA]`; draw roots,

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, Ch. II §§5 “Abstract Root
  Systems,” 6 “Weyl Group,” 7 “Classification of Abstract Cartan Matrices,”
  8 “Classification of Nonreduced Abstract Root Systems,” 9 “Serre
  Relations,” 10 “Isomorphism Theorem,” and 11 “Existence Theorem.”** Primary
  full textbook route. Section 8's nonreduced systems are disposed to DG-34's
  restricted roots; they do not enter the reduced classification here.
- **Kirillov, *Introduction to Lie Groups and Lie Algebras*, root-system,
  Weyl-group, Dynkin-diagram, and classification chapters.** Independent
  lecture-text treatment with explicit classical systems.
- **Etingof, Lie algebra notes, sections on abstract root systems, Dynkin
  diagrams, Serre relations, and classification of semisimple Lie algebras.**
  Independent proof source for items 8–38.
- **Humphreys, *Introduction to Lie Algebras and Representation Theory*, Chs.
  9–14.** Standard cross-check for bases, Weyl groups, Cartan matrices, and
  classification, subject to legal source availability at build time.

### Proof strategy and traps

The rank-two calculation first limits products of Cartan integers to
$0,1,2,3$, fixing angles and root-length ratios. A regular functional gives a
positive system; repeated subtraction proves simple roots span with integral
one-sign coefficients. Chamber reflections then control the Weyl group.

For classification, positive definiteness forces the Dynkin graph to be a
tree with tightly bounded valence and edge multiplicity; an explicit
combinatorial enumeration produces the list without duplicate low ranks.
Serre's theorem supplies the converse: form the quotient of a free Lie algebra,
use root strings to prove finite-dimensionality and the triangular
decomposition, then identify its root system. Thus the existence half is not
an unexplained table.

**Well-definedness discharged:** coroot/inner-product normalization and
irreducible splitting (items 1–8), positivity/bases/lattices/chambers (9–18),
Cartan matrix row convention and diagram arrows (19–27), free quotient and
Serre ideal (28–34), and algebra versus global-group scope (35–39).
**Choice:** root systems are finite and all selections are finite; the pair is
ZF.
**Forward references:** DG-32 classifies finite-dimensional representations
by dominant integral weights; DG-33 adds compact-group lattices/root data;
DG-34 handles real forms and nonreduced restricted systems.

## DG-32. Highest-Weight Theory for Complex Semisimple Lie Algebras

**A page:** `highest-weight-theory-for-complex-semisimple-lie-algebras`
**B page:** `highest-weight-representation-examples`
**Requires:** DG-27, DG-29–DG-31. RL-2 and RL-7 are declared ownership
citations for the rehomed Verma-module and character-formula superstructure;
they are not load-bearing inputs to DG's finite-dimensional classification.

The pair fixes a Cartan subalgebra and a positive root system throughout.
Dominance is therefore relative to a stated base of simple roots, though the
classification is independent up to Weyl conjugacy. It covers the finite-
dimensional structure and classification boundary. RL-2 owns Verma modules and
simple quotients, while RL-7 owns formal characters and the Weyl/Kostant
formulas; DG cites those pages without making its direct finite-dimensional
classification depend on them.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Weights and triangular decomposition**

1. `def-weight-and-weight-space-of-a-lie-algebra-representation` —
   **definition** `[LN]`; for $\lambda\in\mathfrak h^*$,
   $V_\lambda=\{v:h\cdot v=\lambda(h)v\ \forall h\in\mathfrak h\}$.
2. `prop-finite-dimensional-representations-of-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra-decompose-into-weight-spaces` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; Cartan elements are commuting semisimple operators
   after complete reducibility.
3. `prop-root-vectors-shift-weight-spaces` — **proposition** `[LL]`:
   $\mathfrak g_\alpha V_\lambda\subseteq V_{\lambda+\alpha}$.
4. `def-positive-and-negative-nilpotent-subalgebras-and-borel-subalgebra` —
   **definition** `[LN]`:
   $\mathfrak n^\pm=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Phi^+}
   \mathfrak g_{\pm\alpha}$,
   $\mathfrak b=\mathfrak h\oplus\mathfrak n^+$.
5. `thm-triangular-decomposition-of-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`:
   $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak n^-\oplus\mathfrak h\oplus\mathfrak n^+$ and PBW
   gives the corresponding ordered factorization of $U(\mathfrak g)$.
6. `def-partial-order-on-weights` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   $\mu\le\lambda$ iff $\lambda-\mu$ is a nonnegative integral sum of simple
   roots.
7. `def-highest-weight-vector-and-highest-weight-module` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; $v\ne0$, $\mathfrak n^+v=0$, and $hv=\lambda(h)v$.
8. `lem-every-finite-dimensional-irreducible-representation-has-a-highest-weight-vector` —
   **lemma** `[LA]`; choose a maximal weight and use irreducibility.
9. `prop-a-finite-dimensional-irreducible-module-is-generated-by-any-highest-weight-vector` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`; all weights lie below its highest weight.
10. `prop-the-highest-weight-space-of-an-irreducible-module-is-one-dimensional` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.

**Dominant weights and finite-dimensional classification**

11. `def-integral-dominant-and-strictly-dominant-weights` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; $\lambda$ is dominant integral iff
    $\langle\lambda,\alpha_i^\vee\rangle\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$ for every simple
    root.
12. `prop-dominant-integral-weights-are-nonnegative-combinations-of-fundamental-weights` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
13. `lem-highest-weight-of-a-finite-dimensional-module-is-dominant-integral` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; restrict to every simple-root $\mathfrak{sl}_2$.
14. `lem-integrability-relations-for-a-dominant-highest-weight` — **lemma**
    `[LA]`:
    $f_i^{\langle\lambda,\alpha_i^\vee\rangle+1}v_\lambda=0$ in any finite-
    dimensional quotient.
15. `thm-finite-dimensionality-of-lambda-highest-weight-simple-modules-for-dominant-integral-lambda` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; construct the cyclic module with the highest-weight and
    simple-root integrability relations directly, then use the finite
    Weyl-stable convex hull of weights to prove finite-dimensionality and take
    its unique finite-dimensional simple constituent with the supplied highest
    line. No Verma-module theorem is used.
16. `thm-simple-highest-weight-modules-are-classified-by-their-highest-weight` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; two finite-dimensional simple highest-weight modules
    for the fixed positive system are isomorphic iff their highest weights
    agree. Prove this directly from the cyclic construction in item 15 and the
    unique highest-weight line; RL-2 owns the stronger Verma-module universal
    statement.
17. `thm-highest-weight-classification-of-finite-dimensional-irreducible-representations` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`. The finite-dimensional simple modules $V(\lambda)$
    with dominant integral $\lambda$ are exactly the irreducible
    $\mathfrak g$-modules, uniquely. LANDMARK.
18. `cor-every-finite-dimensional-representation-is-a-direct-sum-of-highest-weight-modules` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; cite Weyl complete reducibility.
19. `prop-highest-weight-of-the-dual-representation` — **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $-w_0\lambda$.
20. `prop-top-highest-weight-summand-in-a-tensor-product` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; for dominant integral weights \(\lambda,\mu\),
    \(v_\lambda\otimes v_\mu\) generates a unique summand
    \(V(\lambda+\mu)\) in \(V(\lambda)\otimes V(\mu)\), occurring with
    multiplicity one; every other irreducible summand has strictly lower
    highest weight.
21. `prop-the-adjoint-representation-has-highest-weight-the-highest-root` —
    **proposition** `[LL]` for simple $\mathfrak g$.

**Finite-dimensional boundary data**

22. `def-weyl-vector-rho` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\rho=\frac12\sum_{\alpha>0}\alpha=\sum_i\omega_i$.
23. `prop-weyl-orbit-of-the-highest-weight-gives-extremal-weights-with-multiplicity-one` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.
24. `rem-harish-chandra-isomorphism-and-category-o` — **remark** `[LN]`; RL-1
    through RL-7 own central characters, the Harish--Chandra isomorphism,
    linkage, category $\mathcal O$, BGG resolutions, Verma modules, and the
    Weyl/Kostant character formulas. DG's finite-dimensional classification
    cites that superstructure only for orientation and does not duplicate it.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-every-weight-vector-is-a-highest-weight-vector` — **false statement**
  `[LL]`; positive root spaces must annihilate it.
- `fs-every-verma-module-is-finite-dimensional` — **false statement** `[LL]`;

  **false statement** `[LL]`; RL-7 works in the localized formal group algebra

1. `ex-all-finite-dimensional-irreducible-sl-two-modules` — **example** `[LA]`;
   basis, weights, and raising/lowering coefficients.
2. `ex-verma-modules-for-sl-two` — **example** `[LA]`; a citation-bound RL-2

4. `ex-symmetric-powers-as-highest-weight-modules` — **example** `[LA]`.
5. `ex-exterior-powers-and-fundamental-weights-of-sl-n` — **example** `[LA]`.
6. `ex-the-adjoint-representation-and-the-highest-root` — **example** `[LL]`.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, Ch. V §§1 “Weights,” 2
  “Theorem of the Highest Weight,” 3 “Verma Modules,” 4 “Complete
  Reducibility,” and 6 “Weyl Character Formula.”** Primary textbook route.
  Named controls include Thms. 5.5 and 5.29. Section 5 “Harish–Chandra
  Isomorphism” is rehomed to RL for the central-character reason in item 24;
  §7 parabolic subalgebras is inline only as a B-page orientation remark.
- **Kirillov, *Introduction to Lie Groups and Lie Algebras*, Ch. IX
  §§9.1–9.7, especially Thms. 9.17 and 9.19.** Independent full lecture-text
  treatment of highest weights; its Weyl character/dimension formulas are
  routed to RL-7.
- **Etingof, Lie group/algebra notes, Lecture 24 §§24.1–24.3 and Lecture 25
  §§25.1 “Characters,” 25.3 “Weyl character formula,” and 25.4 “Proof of the
  Weyl character formula.”** Independent source routed to RL-7 for character
  formulas. §25.2 “Category O” is routed to RL as in item 24.
- **Conrad–Landesman, *Compact Lie Groups*, §§27–28, especially §28
  “Examples and Fundamental Representations,” Thms. 28.2–28.4.** Independent
  compact-form control; the lattice integration issue is disposed to DG-33.

### Proof strategy and traps

Restriction to every root $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ proves dominance is necessary.
For sufficiency, impose the simple-root nilpotence relations on a cyclic
highest-weight generator and use PBW ordering plus the finite Weyl-stable
weight region to obtain a finite-dimensional module directly; complete
reducibility and the one-dimensional highest line give the unique simple
module. This closes DG's classification without consuming RL's Verma-module
construction. RL-2 and RL-7 supply the cited infinite-dimensional and
character-formula superstructure.

**Well-definedness discharged:** chosen Cartan/positive system and weight
spaces (items 1–10), direct finite-dimensional uniqueness and integrability
(11–21), and the retained finite-dimensional boundary data (22–24).
**Choice:** finite root/weight and finite-dimensional decomposition arguments
are ZF.
**Forward reference:** DG-33 states exactly which dominant weights integrate
to a given compact connected group using its character lattice.

## DG-33. Compact Lie Groups, Maximal Tori, and Peter–Weyl Theory

**A page:** `compact-lie-groups-maximal-tori-and-peter-weyl-theory`
**B page:** `compact-lie-group-and-representation-examples`
**Requires:** DG-18, DG-21, DG-25–DG-32; measure page
`radon-measures-and-the-riesz-markov-kakutani-theorem`; functional-analysis
pages `hilbert-space-geometry-and-riesz-representation`, the orthonormal-basis
page, and `compact-self-adjoint-hilbert-schmidt-and-trace-class-operators`.

Those measure/functional pages are finished scaffolds but not yet authored in
the live plan. Haar and Peter–Weyl are therefore explicit build-time seams.
The finite-dimensional maximal-torus and root-data theory does not wait for
them except where averaging or $L^2(G)$ is expressly used.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Haar measure and unitary representations**

1. `def-left-right-and-bi-invariant-borel-measure-on-a-lie-group` —
   **definition** `[LN]`; invariance is stated on Borel sets and equivalently
   on $C(G)$ integrals for a finite Radon measure.
2. `thm-normalized-haar-measure-on-a-compact-lie-group` — **theorem** `[LA]`.
   Every compact Lie group has a unique regular Borel probability measure
   invariant under left translations; it is also right invariant and
   inversion invariant. LANDMARK.
3. `prop-integration-against-haar-is-invariant-under-translations-and-conjugation` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`.
4. `def-continuous-and-unitary-representation-of-a-compact-lie-group` —
   **definition** `[LN]`; finite-dimensional complex unless a Hilbert-space
   representation is printed.
5. `thm-every-finite-dimensional-continuous-representation-of-a-compact-lie-group-is-unitarizable` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; average an arbitrary Hermitian inner product over Haar.
6. `cor-complete-reducibility-for-compact-lie-groups` — **corollary** `[LA]`;
   invariant orthogonal complements split subrepresentations.
7. `def-matrix-coefficient-and-character-of-a-compact-group-representation` —
   **definition** `[LN]`.
8. `thm-schur-orthogonality-for-compact-lie-groups` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
   state the exact normalized matrix-coefficient formula for inequivalent and
   equivalent irreducibles.
9. `cor-irreducible-characters-are-orthonormal-class-functions` —
   **corollary** `[LL]`.

**Tori and maximal tori**

10. `def-torus-and-maximal-torus-in-a-compact-lie-group` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; a torus is a compact connected abelian Lie group.
11. `thm-structure-of-a-compact-connected-abelian-lie-group` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; $T\cong\mathfrak t/\Lambda\cong(S^1)^r$, where
    $\Lambda=\ker(\exp:\mathfrak t\to T)$ is a full lattice.
12. `thm-maximal-tori-exist-in-compact-lie-groups` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    maximality by dimension/closed connected abelian enlargement, not Zorn.
13. `thm-every-element-of-a-compact-connected-lie-group-lies-in-a-maximal-torus` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; take the closure of its cyclic subgroup and extend its
    identity component, treating the finite component explicitly.
14. `thm-conjugacy-of-maximal-tori` — **theorem** `[LA]`; use a bi-invariant
    metric and a minimizing geodesic/critical-point argument for the conjugation
    action. LANDMARK.
15. `cor-every-compact-connected-abelian-subgroup-is-contained-in-a-maximal-torus` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`.
16. `cor-rank-of-a-compact-connected-lie-group-is-well-defined` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; common dimension of maximal tori.
17. `def-weyl-group-of-a-compact-connected-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $W(G,T)=N_G(T)/T$.
18. `thm-compact-group-weyl-group-is-finite` — **theorem** `[LA]`; its
    faithful action on $T$ or $\mathfrak t$ has discrete compact kernel
    quotient.
19. `prop-conjugacy-classes-meet-a-fixed-maximal-torus-in-weyl-orbits` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.

**Roots, Weyl integration, and lattices**

20. `def-roots-of-a-compact-connected-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    nonzero weights of $T$ on $\mathfrak g_\mathbb C$ under the adjoint
    representation.
21. `thm-compact-group-roots-form-a-reduced-crystallographic-root-system-on-the-semisimple-part` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; the central torus contributes the orthogonal zero-
    root directions and is kept separate.
22. `thm-analytic-and-root-system-weyl-groups-agree` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    root $SU(2)$ subgroups produce reflections and chamber transitivity gives
    surjectivity.
23. `def-weyl-jacobian-on-a-maximal-torus` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    \[
      J(t)=\prod_{\alpha>0}|1-\alpha(t)^{-1}|^2.
    \]
    Because every root \(\alpha\) is an actual character of $T$, this is
    globally defined even when \(\alpha/2\notin X^*(T)\); it is
    $W$-invariant and independent of the positive system.
24. `thm-weyl-integration-formula` — **theorem** `[LA]`. For continuous class
    functions (and the general conjugation-average version), integrate over
    $G$ as $|W|^{-1}$ times the maximal-torus integral weighted by $J$.
    LANDMARK.
25. `def-character-and-cocharacter-lattices-of-a-torus` — **definition**
    `[LN]`:
    $X^*(T)=\operatorname{Hom}(T,S^1)$ and
    $X_*(T)=\operatorname{Hom}(S^1,T)$ with their perfect integer pairing.
26. `prop-differentiation-identifies-characters-with-the-integral-weight-lattice-of-t` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; normalize $2\pi i$ once.
27. `def-root-datum-of-a-compact-connected-lie-group` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $(X^*(T),\Phi,X_*(T),\Phi^\vee)$ with its perfect pairing.
28. `prop-root-and-weight-lattice-sandwich-for-a-compact-semisimple-group` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`: $Q\subseteq X^*(T)\subseteq P$; the simply
    connected form has $X^*(T)=P$ and the adjoint form has $X^*(T)=Q$.
29. `thm-compact-connected-semisimple-lie-groups-are-classified-up-to-isogeny-by-root-systems` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; “up to isogeny” is not deleted.
30. `thm-compact-connected-lie-groups-are-classified-by-root-data` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; isomorphism classes correspond to isomorphism classes
    of compact root data, including the central torus. LANDMARK.
31. `prop-central-quotients-correspond-to-intermediate-character-lattices` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; for a fixed semisimple Lie algebra, finite central
    quotients reverse the inclusion of allowed characters.

**Peter–Weyl and compact highest weights**

32. `def-left-and-right-regular-unitary-representations-on-l-two-of-a-compact-lie-group` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; Haar invariance makes them unitary.
33. `def-convolution-operator-associated-to-a-continuous-function-on-a-compact-group` —
    **definition** `[LN]`, with left/right convention fixed.
34. `lem-continuous-convolution-operators-are-hilbert-schmidt-and-compact` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; their continuous kernel lies in $L^2(G\times G)$.
35. `lem-compact-convolution-operators-decompose-into-finite-dimensional-invariant-subspaces` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; apply the authored compact-operator spectral theory to
    suitable positive self-adjoint convolutions.
36. `thm-peter-weyl-for-compact-lie-groups` — **theorem** `[LA]`. The matrix
    coefficients of finite-dimensional irreducible unitary representations
    form an orthogonal Hilbert basis of $L^2(G)$, with multiplicity equal to
    representation dimension. LANDMARK.
37. `cor-matrix-coefficients-are-uniformly-dense-in-continuous-functions-on-a-compact-lie-group` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; use the self-adjoint point-separating algebra and
    Stone–Weierstrass/approximate identities.
38. `cor-finite-dimensional-unitary-representations-separate-points-of-a-compact-lie-group` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`.
39. `cor-every-compact-lie-group-is-isomorphic-to-a-closed-matrix-lie-group` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; compactness plus finitely many separating
    representations yields one faithful finite-dimensional representation.
40. `thm-highest-weight-classification-for-a-compact-connected-lie-group` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`. Irreducible finite-dimensional complex representations
    correspond exactly to dominant weights in the **actual character lattice**
    $X^*(T)$, not all of $P$. LANDMARK.
41. `prop-differentiation-relates-compact-group-and-complexified-lie-algebra-highest-weights` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; a Lie-algebra module integrates iff its weights
    satisfy the group lattice condition.
42. `thm-weyl-character-formula-for-compact-connected-lie-groups` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; pull DG-32's formal formula to the simply connected
    cover of the derived group and evaluate it there. For
    \(\lambda\in X^*(T)\), prove independence of the chosen lift and descend
    the quotient to regular $t\in T$ before extending continuously across
    singular elements. Neither \(\rho\) nor each \(\alpha/2\) is assumed to
    be a character of the original non-simply-connected torus.
43. `cor-representation-ring-has-the-dominant-character-basis` — **corollary**
    `[LA]`; multiplication is tensor product and characters embed it in
    $\mathbb Z[X^*(T)]^W$.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-haar-measure-on-a-compact-group-is-only-left-invariant-not-right-invariant` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; uniqueness gives bi-invariance.
- `fs-every-element-of-a-disconnected-compact-lie-group-lies-in-the-identity-components-maximal-torus` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; connectedness is required.
- `fs-a-root-system-determines-a-compact-connected-semisimple-group-up-to-isomorphism` —

- `fs-every-dominant-weight-of-the-abstract-weight-lattice-integrates-to-every-compact-group-form` —

- `fs-peter-weyl-says-every-continuous-function-is-a-finite-sum-of-matrix-coefficients` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; finite sums are dense, not equal to all of
  $C(G)$.
- `fs-every-unitary-representation-of-a-compact-group-is-finite-dimensional` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; the regular representation is generally
  infinite-dimensional.

### B page — `compact-lie-group-and-representation-examples`

2. `ex-maximal-tori-and-weyl-groups-of-u-n-and-su-n` — **example** `[LA]`.
3. `ex-maximal-torus-and-weyl-group-of-so-three` — **example** `[LA]`.

5. `ex-character-lattices-of-su-two-and-so-three` — **example** `[LA]`;
   even weights survive the central quotient.
6. `ex-simply-connected-adjoint-and-intermediate-forms-of-a-semisimple-compact-group` —

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, Ch. IV §§1 “Examples of

  Independent comprehensive compact-group treatment.

### Proof strategy and traps

Construct Haar as a positive invariant functional on $C(G)$ and invoke
MT-20's Riesz–Markov–Kakutani representation; uniqueness plus inversion makes
it bi-invariant. Averaging gives invariant inner products. Maximal-torus
conjugacy uses compactness and the geometry of conjugation; root $SU(2)$
subgroups identify the analytic Weyl group.

Peter–Weyl uses continuous convolution kernels, authored Hilbert–Schmidt
compactness, and spectral decomposition to obtain finite-dimensional invariant
subspaces; an approximate identity proves density. Global classification
retains $X^*(T)$ between $Q$ and $P$, so no root-system theorem is upgraded
from isogeny to isomorphism.

**Well-definedness discharged:** Radon convention/normalization and averaging
(items 1–9), torus lattice/conjugacy/normalizer quotient (10–24), perfect
character–cocharacter pairing and central quotients (25–31), Hilbert
completion/convolution sides/multiplicities (32–39), and actual group lattice
in highest weights (40–43).
**Choice:** Haar inherits MT-20's $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$ sufficient boundary.
Peter–Weyl inherits the functional-analysis Hilbert projection/basis and
compact-spectral ledgers (including their stated choice costs). Finite-
dimensional root-data arguments add no choice.
**Unresolved seams:** MT-20 and FA-13/FA-14/FA-16 must be authored before Haar
and Peter–Weyl items; final stable page/id names must replace these scaffold
references.
