## DG-34. Real Forms and Real Semisimple Lie Algebras

**A page:** `real-forms-and-real-semisimple-lie-algebras`
**B page:** `real-form-and-cartan-decomposition-examples`
**Requires:** DG-25–DG-33 and planned covering machinery for global simply
connected group forms.

This pair prevents the complex Dynkin classification from being misreported
as a classification over $\mathbb R$. Ordinary complex roots remain reduced;
restricted roots of a real form can be nonreduced. Real Cartan subalgebras need
not be conjugate, so the classification records an involution and compact/
noncompact data through Vogan (and equivalently Satake) diagrams.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Complexification, conjugations, and compact forms**

1. `def-complexification-of-a-real-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $\mathfrak g_\mathbb C=\mathfrak g_0\otimes_\mathbb R\mathbb C$ with the
   complex-bilinear extended bracket.
2. `prop-complexification-has-a-canonical-conjugation-with-fixed-algebra-g-zero` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`.
3. `def-real-form-of-a-complex-lie-algebra` — **definition** `[LN]`; a real
   subalgebra $\mathfrak g_0$ for which
   $\mathfrak g_0\otimes_\mathbb R\mathbb C\cong\mathfrak g$, equivalently the fixed
   points of a conjugate-linear involutive automorphism.
4. `thm-real-forms-correspond-to-conjugate-linear-involutions` — **theorem**
   `[LA]`; the maps in both directions are inverse up to the stated notion of
   conjugacy.
5. `prop-complexification-preserves-semisimplicity` — **proposition** `[LA]`;
   a real finite-dimensional algebra is semisimple iff its complexification
   is, using nondegeneracy of the Killing form.
6. `def-compact-real-form-of-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; its Killing form is negative definite.
7. `thm-existence-of-a-compact-real-form` — **theorem** `[LA]`; choose a
   Chevalley basis and take the real span of the compact combinations of
   $h_i,e_\alpha,f_\alpha$. LANDMARK.
8. `thm-conjugacy-of-compact-real-forms` — **theorem** `[LA]`; any two compact
   real forms of a complex semisimple algebra are conjugate by an inner
   automorphism.
9. `def-split-real-form` — **definition** `[LN]`; it contains a Cartan
   subalgebra split over $\mathbb R$, equivalently has real rank equal to the
   complex rank.
10. `thm-existence-and-uniqueness-up-to-isomorphism-of-the-split-real-form` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; use a Chevalley basis with real structure constants.

**Cartan involutions and decompositions**

11. `def-cartan-involution-of-a-real-semisimple-lie-algebra` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; an involutive automorphism $\theta$ for which
    $B_\theta(X,Y)=-B(X,\theta Y)$ is positive definite.
12. `thm-existence-of-a-cartan-involution` — **theorem** `[LA]`; obtain it
    from a compact real form compatible with the given conjugation.
13. `thm-conjugacy-of-cartan-involutions` — **theorem** `[LA]` by an inner
    automorphism.
14. `def-cartan-decomposition-of-a-real-semisimple-lie-algebra` — **definition**
    `[LN]`: $\mathfrak g_0=\mathfrak k_0\oplus\mathfrak p_0$ as the
    $+1/-1$ eigenspaces of $\theta$.
15. `prop-bracket-relations-and-killing-signs-in-a-cartan-decomposition` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`:
    $[\mathfrak k,\mathfrak k]\subseteq\mathfrak k$,
    $[\mathfrak k,\mathfrak p]\subseteq\mathfrak p$,
    $[\mathfrak p,\mathfrak p]\subseteq\mathfrak k$; $B$ is negative on
    $\mathfrak k$ and positive on $\mathfrak p$.
16. `thm-global-cartan-decomposition-for-a-connected-finite-center-semisimple-lie-group` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`. Let $G$ be a connected real semisimple Lie group
    with finite center, \(\theta\) a global Cartan involution,
    $K=G^\theta$, and
    \(\mathfrak g_0=\mathfrak k_0\oplus\mathfrak p_0\). Then
    \((k,X)\mapsto k\exp X\) is a diffeomorphism
    $K\times\mathfrak p_0\to G$.
17. `cor-maximal-compact-subgroups-exist-and-are-conjugate-in-a-connected-finite-center-semisimple-lie-group` —
    **corollary** `[LA]` under precisely the setup of item 16.
18. `def-riemannian-symmetric-pair-of-noncompact-type` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    $(\mathfrak g_0,\theta)$ with semisimple $\mathfrak g_0$ and no compact
    ideal, yielding the homogeneous space $G/K$.
19. `prop-cartan-decomposition-gives-the-invariant-metric-and-curvature-of-g-mod-k` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; $B_\theta|_\mathfrak p$ is $K$-invariant and
    $R(X,Y)Z=-[[X,Y],Z]$ at the origin in the normalized symmetric metric,
    hence sectional curvature is nonpositive.
20. `thm-cartan-decomposition-identifies-p-with-the-noncompact-symmetric-space` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; under exactly item 16's compatible
    \((G,\theta,K,\mathfrak p_0)\) setup,
    $X\mapsto\exp(X)K$ is a diffeomorphism $\mathfrak p_0\to G/K$.

**Restricted roots and Iwasawa decomposition**

21. `def-maximal-split-abelian-subspace-and-real-rank` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    $\mathfrak a\subseteq\mathfrak p$ maximal abelian and
    $\operatorname{rank}_\mathbb R\mathfrak g_0=\dim\mathfrak a$.
22. `thm-maximal-abelian-subspaces-of-p-are-conjugate-by-k` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`.
23. `def-restricted-root-and-restricted-root-space` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    \(\mathfrak g_0^\lambda=
    \{X\in\mathfrak g_0:[H,X]=\lambda(H)X\ \forall H\in\mathfrak a\}\).
24. `thm-restricted-root-space-decomposition` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $\mathfrak g_0=\mathfrak g_0^0\oplus
    \bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Sigma}\mathfrak g_0^\lambda$, with
    $\mathfrak g_0^0=Z_{\mathfrak g_0}(\mathfrak a)$ and finite real
    multiplicities $m_\lambda=\dim_\mathbb R\mathfrak g_0^\lambda$.
25. `prop-restricted-root-systems-may-be-nonreduced` — **proposition** `[LA]`;
    $2\lambda$ can occur and the irreducible nonreduced family is $BC_r$.
26. `def-restricted-weyl-group` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $W(\mathfrak g_0,\mathfrak a)=N_K(\mathfrak a)/Z_K(\mathfrak a)$.
27. `thm-restricted-weyl-group-is-the-reflection-group-of-the-restricted-root-system` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`.
28. `def-positive-restricted-roots-and-nilpotent-n-algebra` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; $\mathfrak n=\bigoplus_{\lambda>0}\mathfrak g_0^\lambda$.
29. `thm-iwasawa-decomposition-on-the-lie-algebra-level` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $\mathfrak g_0=\mathfrak k_0\oplus\mathfrak a\oplus\mathfrak n$ as real
    vector spaces, with $\mathfrak a\oplus\mathfrak n$ solvable.
30. `thm-global-iwasawa-decomposition` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Under the
    exact compatible setup of item 16, let $A=\exp\mathfrak a$ and let $N$
    be the connected subgroup with Lie algebra \(\mathfrak n\). Then
    multiplication $K\times A\times N\to G$ is a diffeomorphism. LANDMARK.
31. `prop-uniqueness-and-change-of-positive-system-in-iwasawa-decomposition` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; $A,N$ depend on choices but alternatives are
    conjugate by the appropriate compact/Weyl data.

**Real Cartan subalgebras and classification**

32. `def-theta-stable-cartan-subalgebra-and-compact-split-parts` —
    **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\mathfrak h_0=(\mathfrak h_0\cap\mathfrak k_0)
    \oplus(\mathfrak h_0\cap\mathfrak p_0)$.
33. `thm-every-real-cartan-subalgebra-is-conjugate-to-a-theta-stable-one` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`.
34. `prop-real-cartan-subalgebras-need-not-be-conjugate` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; distinguish compact and split Cartans already in
    $\mathfrak{sl}_2(\mathbb R)$.
35. `def-cayley-transform-of-a-theta-stable-cartan-subalgebra` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; state the real/noncompact root conditions under which it changes
    compact dimension.
36. `thm-cayley-transforms-connect-theta-stable-cartans-in-the-classification` —
    **theorem** `[LA]` with the exact conjugacy/equivalence scope.
37. `def-vogan-diagram` — **definition** `[LN]`; a Dynkin diagram with an
    involution and painted fixed vertices encoding compact versus noncompact
    imaginary simple roots, modulo the standard equivalence moves.
38. `thm-vogan-diagram-of-a-real-semisimple-lie-algebra-is-well-defined-up-to-equivalence` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; changes of maximally compact Cartan/positive system
    induce precisely the allowed moves.
39. `thm-classification-of-real-forms-by-vogan-diagrams` — **theorem** `[LA]`.
    Equivalence classes of Vogan diagrams classify real forms of complex
    semisimple Lie algebras up to isomorphism. LANDMARK.
40. `def-satake-diagram` — **definition** `[LN]`; start with a
    \(\theta\)-stable maximally split Cartan and a compatible simple system.
    Black vertices are compact imaginary simple roots, equivalently roots
    restricting to zero on \(\mathfrak a\); arrows pair distinct white simple
    roots having the same nonzero restriction to \(\mathfrak a\).
41. `thm-vogan-and-satake-diagrams-give-equivalent-real-form-classifications` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; the conversion is stated through Cartan involution and
    restricted-root data, not as identical decorations.
42. `thm-complexification-dichotomy-for-a-real-simple-lie-algebra` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; its complexification is either complex simple, or a
    sum of two isomorphic simple ideals interchanged by conjugation (the case
    of a complex simple algebra viewed as real).
43. `thm-classification-of-real-semisimple-lie-algebras` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    decompose into real simple ideals and use the Vogan/Satake list, including
    compact, split, classical intermediate, and exceptional real forms.
44. `prop-classical-real-forms-of-the-classical-complex-lie-algebras` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; tabulate the $\mathfrak{sl}_n(\mathbb R)$,
    $\mathfrak{su}(p,q)$, $\mathfrak{su}^*(2n)$; orthogonal
    $\mathfrak{so}(p,q)$ and $\mathfrak{so}^*(2n)$; symplectic
    $\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}(\mathbb R)$ and $\mathfrak{sp}(p,q)$ families with
    ranges and low-rank coincidences checked against the diagram
    classification.
45. `rem-representation-theory-of-noncompact-real-reductive-groups` — **remark** `[LN]`;
    Harish–Chandra modules, $(\mathfrak g,K)$-modules, unitary duals,
    Plancherel, and Langlands classification require analysis beyond this
    finite-dimensional structure block.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-a-real-form-is-merely-the-same-complex-lie-algebra-with-scalars-forgotten` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; it is a fixed real algebra whose complexification
  recovers the complex algebra.
- `fs-all-real-forms-of-a-complex-semisimple-lie-algebra-are-isomorphic` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; compact and split forms already differ.
- `fs-all-cartan-subalgebras-of-a-real-semisimple-lie-algebra-are-conjugate` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`.
- `fs-restricted-root-systems-are-always-reduced` — **false statement** `[LL]`;
  $BC_r$ occurs.
- `fs-a-plain-dynkin-diagram-classifies-real-forms` — **false statement**
  `[LL]`; involution/painting data are required.
- `fs-global-cartan-and-iwasawa-decompositions-hold-for-every-nonlinear-cover-without-modified-k` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; global group hypotheses matter.

### B page — `real-form-and-cartan-decomposition-examples`

1. `ex-compact-and-split-real-forms-of-sl-two-c` — **example** `[LA]`:
   $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ and $\mathfrak{sl}_2(\mathbb R)$.
2. `ex-cartan-involution-and-k-plus-p-for-sl-n-r` — **example** `[LA]`:
   $\theta(X)=-X^T$.
3. `ex-polar-cartan-decomposition-of-sl-n-r` — **example** `[LA]`.
4. `ex-iwasawa-decomposition-of-sl-two-r` — **example** `[LA]`; $KAN$ written
   explicitly.
5. `ex-compact-and-split-cartan-subalgebras-of-sl-two-r` — **example** `[LA]`.
6. `ex-restricted-roots-of-sl-n-r` — **example** `[LA]`.
7. `ex-a-nonreduced-bc-root-system-from-a-real-form` — **example** `[LA]`.
8. `ex-vogan-diagrams-for-real-forms-of-sl-three-c` — **example** `[LA]`.
9. `ex-complex-simple-lie-algebra-viewed-as-a-real-simple-algebra` —
   **example** `[LA]`; its complexification splits into two conjugate ideals.
10. `cex-two-nonconjugate-real-cartan-subalgebras` — **counterexample** `[LL]`.
11. `cex-same-complexification-with-different-killing-form-signatures` —
    **counterexample** `[LL]`.
12. `ex-hyperbolic-space-as-so-zero-n-one-mod-so-n` — **example** `[LA]`:
    \(\mathbb H^n\cong SO_0(n,1)/SO(n)\); using the identity component avoids
    the generally disconnected full (SO(n,1)). It
    connects Cartan decomposition to earlier nonpositive geometry.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, Ch. VI §§1 “Existence of a
  Compact Real Form,” 2–3 “Cartan Decomposition” at Lie-algebra and group
  levels, 4–5 “Iwasawa Decomposition” and uniqueness, 6 “Cartan
  Subalgebras,” 7 “Cayley Transforms,” 8 “Vogan Diagrams,” 9
  “Complexification of a Simple Real Lie Algebra,” 10 “Classification of
  Simple Real Lie Algebras,” and 11 “Restricted Roots in the
  Classification.”** Primary complete textbook route; named controls are
  Thms. 6.11, 6.74, 6.88, and 6.96.
- **Etingof, Lie group/algebra notes, Lectures 39 §§39.1–39.4, 40
  §§40.1–40.3, 41 §41.1, and 43 §§43.1/43.6; Thms. 39.6, 40.1, 43.1, and
  43.20.** Independent compact/split form, Cartan decomposition, and real-form
  classification treatment.
- **Kirillov, Ch. III §§3.9 “Complex and real forms” and 3.10 examples.**
  Independent introductory convention check only; it does not back the
  classification.
- **Conrad–Landesman, §10 “Weyl's Unitary Trick.”** Independent compact-form
  input, not a substitute for real-form classification.

### Proof strategy and traps

Real forms are encoded by conjugate-linear involutions. A compact form gives a
positive form $-B(X,\theta Y)$ and hence a Cartan involution; spectral
decomposition yields $\mathfrak k\oplus\mathfrak p$. Maximal abelian
$\mathfrak a\subset\mathfrak p$ diagonalizes the restricted adjoint action,
and a positive restricted system yields $\mathfrak n$ and Iwasawa.

For classification, first conjugate a real Cartan to a $\theta$-stable one,
then use Cayley transforms to reach a maximally compact representative. The
involution on complex simple roots plus painting of fixed vertices is the
Vogan diagram; prove changes of choices give precisely its equivalences and
construct a real form back from each diagram. Restricted $BC_r$ is kept
separate from the reduced complex root system.

**Well-definedness discharged:** fixed-point/conjugation equivalence and
complexification (items 1–10), positivity/eigenspace/global-group hypotheses
(11–20), choice/conjugacy of $\mathfrak a$ and restricted multiplicities
(21–31), nonconjugate real Cartans and diagram equivalence (32–44).
**Choice:** finite-dimensional real/complex algebra and finite diagrams are
ZF. Global simply connected group forms inherit the covering-space seam.
**Scope boundary:** the entire finite-dimensional real structure/classification
is included; only analytic representation theory of noncompact groups is
deferred.

## DG-35. Symplectic Manifolds, Moser Stability, and Darboux–Weinstein Theory

**A page:** `symplectic-manifolds-moser-stability-and-darboux-weinstein-theory`
**B page:** `symplectic-foundations-and-normal-form-examples`
**Requires:** DG-2, DG-5, DG-7–DG-9, DG-11–DG-15, and DG-18; published
`thm-alternating-forms-have-a-symplectic-normal-form`.

The sign convention is §3.5: the tautological form is $\lambda$, the canonical
cotangent symplectic form is $\omega=-d\lambda$, and later
$\iota_{X_H}\omega=dH$. Sources using $\iota_{X_H}\omega=-dH$ are converted
globally, never formula by formula.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Symplectic linear algebra**

1. `def-symplectic-vector-space` — **definition** `[LN]`; a finite-dimensional
   real vector space with a nondegenerate alternating bilinear form.
2. `prop-symplectic-vector-spaces-have-even-dimension` — **proposition** `[LL]`;
   cite the published alternating-form normal form.
3. `def-symplectic-orthogonal-complement` — **definition** `[LN]`:
   $W^\omega=\{v:\omega(v,W)=0\}$.
4. `prop-symplectic-double-orthogonal-and-dimension-identities` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`:
   $(W^\omega)^\omega=W$ and
   $\dim W+\dim W^\omega=\dim V$.
5. `def-isotropic-coisotropic-symplectic-and-lagrangian-subspaces` —
   **definition** `[LN]`; respectively $W\subseteq W^\omega$,
   $W^\omega\subseteq W$, nondegenerate restriction, and $W=W^\omega$.
6. `thm-equivalent-characterizations-of-lagrangian-subspaces` — **theorem**
   `[LA]`; isotropic of half dimension, coisotropic of half dimension, or
   equality with its symplectic orthogonal.
7. `prop-symplectic-reduction-of-a-coisotropic-vector-subspace` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; $W/W^\omega$ has a well-defined nondegenerate
   induced form.
8. `prop-graphs-of-linear-maps-and-lagrangian-relations` — **proposition**
   `[LA]`; for a linear **isomorphism** $A:V\to W$ between equal-dimensional
   symplectic spaces, \(\operatorname{graph}(A)\) is Lagrangian in
   \((V,-\omega_V)\oplus(W,\omega_W)\) iff
   $A^*\omega_W=\omega_V$. A symplectic embedding into a larger space has
   merely isotropic graph, so no Lagrangian claim is made there. General
   canonical-relation theory is deferred.

**Symplectic manifolds and cotangent bundles**

9. `def-symplectic-form-and-symplectic-manifold` — **definition** `[LN]`; a
   closed nondegenerate two-form.
10. `thm-nondegeneracy-is-equivalent-to-a-nonvanishing-top-wedge` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; on a $2n$-manifold, $\omega$ is nondegenerate iff $\omega^n$ is
    nowhere zero.
11. `cor-symplectic-manifolds-have-a-canonical-orientation-and-volume-form` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; use $\omega^n/n!$.
12. `def-symplectomorphism-local-symplectomorphism-and-symplectic-embedding` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; pullback equality plus the appropriate
    diffeomorphism/embedding condition.
13. `prop-products-and-opposites-of-symplectic-manifolds` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; $M^-$ carries $-\omega$, and product carries the sum of pullbacks.
14. `def-isotropic-coisotropic-symplectic-and-lagrangian-submanifolds` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; each tangent space has the corresponding linear
    property.
15. `prop-lagrangian-submanifolds-have-half-dimension` — **proposition** `[LL]`.
16. `def-tautological-one-form-on-a-cotangent-bundle` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\lambda_{\alpha_q}(v)=\alpha_q(d\pi(v))$.
17. `lem-the-tautological-one-form-is-intrinsic-and-smooth` — **lemma** `[LA]`;
    local coordinates give $\lambda=p_i\,dq^i$ and the definition proves
    coordinate independence.
18. `thm-the-canonical-cotangent-two-form-is-symplectic` — **theorem** `[LA]`:
    $\omega_{\mathrm{can}}=-d\lambda=dq^i\wedge dp_i$ is closed and
    nondegenerate.
19. `prop-cotangent-lifts-are-symplectomorphisms` — **proposition** `[LA]`;
    for a diffeomorphism $f:Q\to Q$, the inverse-transpose lift preserves
    $\lambda$ and hence $\omega$.
20. `prop-graph-of-a-one-form-is-lagrangian-iff-the-one-form-is-closed` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; pull back $\lambda$ along the graph.

**Compatible structures**

21. `def-compatible-complex-structure-on-a-symplectic-vector-space` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; $J^2=-I$, $\omega(Ju,Jv)=\omega(u,v)$, and
    $g_J(u,v)=\omega(u,Jv)$ is positive definite.
22. `thm-compatible-complex-structures-exist-on-symplectic-vector-spaces` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; use a symplectic basis or polar decomposition.
23. `thm-every-symplectic-manifold-admits-a-compatible-almost-complex-structure` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; start with a Riemannian metric, take the skew-adjoint
    operator $A$ defined by $\omega(u,v)=g(Au,v)$, and set
    $J=(-A^2)^{-1/2}A$ smoothly.
24. `def-compatible-almost-kahler-metric` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $g(u,v)=\omega(u,Jv)$.
25. `rem-compatible-almost-complex-structures-and-kahler-geometry` — **remark** `[LN]`; compatibility does
    not make $J$ integrable. Complex/Kähler geometry and the Newlander–Nirenberg
    theorem lie outside this track.

**Moser's method and normal forms**

26. `lem-moser-pullback-differentiation-equation` — **lemma** `[LA]`; for a
    time-dependent flow $\varphi_t$,
    $\frac d{dt}\varphi_t^*\omega_t=\varphi_t^*(\dot\omega_t+
    d\iota_{X_t}\omega_t)$ when $d\omega_t=0$.
27. `thm-moser-stability-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. On compact $M$, a
    smooth family of symplectic forms with constant de Rham class is related
    by an isotopy $\varphi_t$ with $\varphi_t^*\omega_t=\omega_0$. LANDMARK.
28. `thm-compact-support-moser-stability-on-a-noncompact-manifold` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; require primitives $\sigma_t$ of $\dot\omega_t$ with
    supports uniformly contained in one compact set, ensuring the vector-field
    flow exists for the whole interval.
29. `thm-relative-moser-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`; if the forms agree
    along a submanifold and their first-order difference has a relative
    primitive, choose the isotopy fixed on that submanifold.
30. `lem-relative-poincare-primitive-near-a-submanifold` — **lemma** `[LA]`;
    use a tubular contraction and the de Rham homotopy operator, with vanishing
    on the submanifold proved.
31. `thm-darboux-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Every symplectic form has
    local coordinates with
    $\omega=\sum_i dq^i\wedge dp_i$. LANDMARK.
32. `cor-symplectic-manifolds-have-no-local-invariants-beyond-dimension` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`; explicitly local only.
33. `def-symplectic-normal-bundle-of-a-symplectic-submanifold` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; the symplectic orthogonal complement of $TS$ in $TM|_S$.
34. `thm-symplectic-neighborhood-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`; symplectic
    submanifolds with isomorphic symplectic normal bundles have
    symplectomorphic neighbourhood germs, under an isomorphism covering the
    supplied submanifold symplectomorphism.
35. `def-canonical-symplectic-model-near-the-zero-section-of-t-star-l` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; the zero section is Lagrangian.
36. `thm-weinstein-lagrangian-neighborhood-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. A
    neighbourhood of a Lagrangian $L$ is symplectomorphic, as a germ fixed on
    $L$, to a neighbourhood of the zero section in $(T^*L,-d\lambda)$.
    LANDMARK.
37. `prop-lagrangian-neighborhood-germ-is-not-canonical` — **proposition**
    `[LL]`; choices of tubular data/primitives change the symplectomorphism.
38. `thm-local-normal-form-near-a-coisotropic-submanifold` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    the characteristic distribution is integrable and the germ is determined
    by the presymplectic form under the stated bundle hypotheses. This supplies
    only the regular local model needed for reduction.
39. `prop-characteristic-distribution-of-a-coisotropic-submanifold-is-involutive` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; for
    $X,Y\in\Gamma((TC)^\omega)=\Gamma(\ker i^*\omega)$, the identity
    $d(i^*\omega)=0$ shows that \([X,Y]\) remains in
    \(\Gamma(\ker i^*\omega)\). The argument does not start with arbitrary
    tangent fields.

### `fs-` items (A page)

- `fs-every-nondegenerate-two-form-is-symplectic` — **false statement** `[LL]`;
  it must also be closed.
- `fs-symplectic-manifolds-can-have-odd-dimension` — **false statement** `[LL]`.
- `fs-every-half-dimensional-submanifold-is-lagrangian` — **false statement**
  `[LL]`; isotropy is also required.
- `fs-the-canonical-cotangent-symplectic-form-is-d-lambda-under-the-library-convention` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; it is $-d\lambda$.
- `fs-cohomologous-symplectic-forms-on-a-noncompact-manifold-are-always-isotopic` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`; flow completeness/support control is missing.
- `fs-darboux-theorem-makes-all-symplectic-manifolds-globally-symplectomorphic` —
  **false statement** `[LL]`.

### B page — `symplectic-foundations-and-normal-form-examples`

1. `ex-the-standard-symplectic-vector-space` — **example** `[LL]`.
2. `ex-isotropic-coisotropic-and-lagrangian-coordinate-subspaces` —
   **example** `[LL]`.
3. `ex-the-cotangent-bundle-of-a-circle-as-a-symplectic-cylinder` —
   **example** `[LA]`.
4. `ex-graphs-of-exact-and-closed-one-forms-as-lagrangians` — **example**
   `[LA]`.
5. `ex-product-and-opposite-symplectic-manifolds` — **example** `[LL]`.
6. `ex-a-compatible-complex-structure-on-standard-symplectic-space` —
   **example** `[LL]`.
7. `ex-moser-isotopy-for-area-forms-on-a-compact-surface` — **example** `[LA]`.
8. `ex-darboux-coordinates-for-a-nonconstant-area-form` — **example** `[LA]`.
9. `ex-the-zero-section-and-cotangent-fibres-as-lagrangians` — **example**
   `[LL]`.
10. `cex-a-nondegenerate-nonclosed-two-form-in-dimension-at-least-four` —
    **counterexample** `[LL]`.
11. `cex-cohomology-class-obstructs-a-global-symplectomorphism` —
    **counterexample** `[LA]`.
12. `cex-a-compatible-almost-complex-structure-that-is-not-integrable` —
    **counterexample** `[LA]`, source-cited and non-load-bearing.

### Sources and exact locators

- **Cannas da Silva, *Lectures on Symplectic Geometry*, Lectures 1–3;
  Lecture 6 §§6.1–6.3; Lecture 7 §§7.1–7.3; Lecture 8 §§8.1–8.3; and Lecture
  9 §§9.1–9.2.** Primary open monograph route. Thm. 1.1 is
  `already-published` as
  `thm-alternating-forms-have-a-symplectic-normal-form`. Named controls are
  Thms. 6.5–6.6, 7.2–7.4, 8.1 (Darboux), 8.4 (Weinstein Lagrangian
  neighborhood), 8.5, 8.6 (Coisotropic Embedding), and 9.1–9.3.
- **Meinrenken, *Symplectic Geometry* notes, §§2.1–2.4, 3.1–3.4, 4.3–4.6,
  and 5.1–5.3.** Independent full lecture-note treatment. Named controls are
  Props. 3.2 and 3.24; Thms. 4.28, 5.4 (Moser), 5.10 (Darboux), 5.12, 5.14
  (Lagrangian neighborhood), and 5.18.
- **Meinrenken §§2.5–2.10.** Compatible complex structures are included in
  items 21–25; polar-decomposition refinements are inline. The symplectic
  group, Lagrangian Grassmannian, and Maslov indices are deferred to
  symplectic topology because no later item depends on them.

### Proof strategy and traps

Linear symplectic operations are licensed by the published normal form and
explicit quotient checks. For $T^*Q$, the formula
$\lambda_{\alpha_q}(v)=\alpha_q(d\pi v)$ is the coordinate-free
well-definedness proof; coordinates only compute $-d\lambda$.

Moser chooses $\sigma_t$ with $\dot\omega_t=d\sigma_t$ and solves
$\iota_{X_t}\omega_t=-\sigma_t$. Compactness or uniform compact support gives
the whole time-one flow. Darboux shrinks until the straight-line family from
the constant form at a point stays nondegenerate. Weinstein uses a relative
primitive and relative Moser after matching first-order symplectic normal
data.

**Well-definedness discharged:** symplectic complements/quotients and graph
signs (items 1–8), top wedge/cotangent naturality/submanifold conditions
(9–20), smooth compatible $J$ (21–25), primitive/flow support and relative
fixing (26–30), and normal-bundle/germ independence (31–39).
**Choice:** linear algebra, local Moser, and compact-flow arguments are ZF.
The compatible global almost-complex construction and any partitioned tubular
data inherit DG-2's $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$ sufficient boundary; Moser itself
adds no choice.
**Forward references:** DG-36 builds Hamiltonian dynamics and action–angle
coordinates; DG-37 builds moment maps and reduction.

## DG-36. Hamiltonian Mechanics and Completely Integrable Systems

**A page:** `hamiltonian-mechanics-and-completely-integrable-systems`
**B page:** `hamiltonian-mechanics-and-integrable-system-examples`
**Requires:** DG-8–DG-9, DG-12, DG-15, DG-18–DG-20, and DG-35; measure page
`measure-preserving-transformations-and-poincare-recurrence` only for item 29.

With $\iota_{X_H}\omega=dH$, Hamilton's equations have their standard form
$\dot q^i=\partial H/\partial p_i$,
$\dot p_i=-\partial H/\partial q^i$. The resulting map
$H\mapsto X_H$ is an **antihomomorphism** for
$\{F,G\}=\omega(X_F,X_G)$; all Poisson and moment-map signs below follow from
that choice.

### A-page items in dependency order

**Symplectic and Hamiltonian vector fields**

1. `def-symplectic-vector-field` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   $\mathcal L_X\omega=0$.
2. `prop-a-vector-field-is-symplectic-iff-iota-x-omega-is-closed` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`; use Cartan and $d\omega=0$.
3. `def-hamiltonian-vector-field-and-hamiltonian-function` — **definition**
   `[LN]`; $\iota_{X_H}\omega=dH$.
4. `thm-hamiltonian-vector-fields-exist-uniquely-for-smooth-functions` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; invert the bundle map
   $\omega^\flat:TM\to T^*M$.
5. `prop-hamiltonian-vector-fields-are-symplectic-and-symplectic-fields-are-locally-hamiltonian` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; the latter is the local Poincaré lemma.
6. `prop-hamiltonians-for-a-fixed-vector-field-differ-by-a-locally-constant-function` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`; by constants on connected components, not
   necessarily one global constant.
7. `thm-symplectic-vector-fields-modulo-hamiltonian-vector-fields-are-first-de-rham-cohomology` —
   **theorem** `[LA]`; $X\mapsto[\iota_X\omega]$ is a well-defined vector-
   space isomorphism of the quotient.
8. `thm-hamiltonian-flows-preserve-the-symplectic-form` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
   local unless completeness is separately proved.
9. `prop-a-hamiltonian-is-conserved-along-its-own-flow` — **proposition**
   `[LL]`: $dH(X_H)=\omega(X_H,X_H)=0$.

**Poisson bracket**

10. `def-poisson-bracket-on-a-symplectic-manifold` — **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\{F,G\}=\omega(X_F,X_G)=X_G(F)=-X_F(G)$.
11. `prop-poisson-bracket-is-bilinear-skew-and-a-derivation-in-each-entry` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`.
12. `thm-hamiltonian-vector-field-map-is-a-lie-antihomomorphism` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`:
    $[X_F,X_G]=-X_{\{F,G\}}$ under the chosen convention.
13. `thm-poisson-bracket-satisfies-the-jacobi-identity` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    prove the identity pointwise from $d\omega=0$ (equivalently, combine
    Cartan's formula with the biderivation identity). The vector-field Jacobi
    identity alone determines the Jacobiator only up to a locally constant
    function, and vanishing of its differential is not misused to set that
    constant to zero.
14. `thm-smooth-functions-form-a-poisson-algebra` — **theorem** `[LL]`;
    commutative multiplication, Lie bracket, and biderivation compatibility.
15. `prop-observable-evolution-equation` — **proposition** `[LL]`:
    along the $H$-flow, $\frac d{dt}F=\{F,H\}$.
16. `def-first-integral-and-poisson-commuting-functions` — **definition**
    `[LN]`.
17. `prop-f-is-a-first-integral-of-h-iff-f-and-h-poisson-commute` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`.
18. `thm-hamiltonian-flows-commute-iff-their-hamiltonians-poisson-commute-up-to-locally-constant-bracket` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; on a connected manifold, commuting fields mean
    $\{F,G\}$ is constant, while zero bracket gives commuting local flows.

**Canonical coordinates and mechanics**

19. `thm-hamilton-equations-in-canonical-cotangent-coordinates` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; derive the coordinate formula from
    $\iota_{X_H}(dq^i\wedge dp_i)=dH$.
20. `prop-coordinate-formula-for-the-poisson-bracket` — **proposition** `[LL]`:
    $\{F,G\}=\sum_i(F_{q^i}G_{p_i}-F_{p_i}G_{q^i})$.
21. `prop-cotangent-lift-of-a-vector-field-is-hamiltonian` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; for $Y\in\mathfrak X(Q)$, its cotangent-lift flow has Hamiltonian
    $H_Y(q,p)=p(Y_q)$ with the lift convention written.
22. `def-time-dependent-hamiltonian-vector-field-and-flow` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; $H_t$ yields $X_{H_t}$ and a two-time symplectic evolution.
23. `prop-time-dependent-hamiltonian-evolution-is-symplectic` — **proposition**
    `[LA]`; differentiate pullback using DG-12.
24. `def-canonical-transformation` — **definition** `[LN]`; a
    symplectomorphism, with Hamiltonian isotopies as the exact-flow subclass.
25. `thm-liouville-volume-preservation` — **theorem** `[LA]`; symplectic flows
    preserve $\omega^n/n!$.
26. `cor-hamiltonian-flow-has-zero-divergence-with-respect-to-symplectic-volume` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`.
27. `def-liouville-vector-field-on-an-exact-symplectic-manifold` —
    **definition** `[LN]`; if $\omega=-d\lambda$, use
    $\iota_Z\omega=-\lambda$, so $\mathcal L_Z\omega=\omega$ under the
    library's sign.
28. `prop-canonical-liouville-vector-field-on-a-cotangent-bundle-is-radial-in-momenta` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`: $Z=p_i\partial_{p_i}$.
29. `cor-poincare-recurrence-for-finite-volume-hamiltonian-invariant-regions` —
    **corollary** `[LA]`; cite measure pair MT-22 and require a finite-measure
    invariant set. No recurrence theorem is re-proved.

**Lagrangian and Hamiltonian formulations**

30. `def-lagrangian-action-functional-on-curves` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    for $L:TQ\to\mathbb R$,
    $\mathcal S(\gamma)=\int L(\gamma,\dot\gamma)dt$ with fixed endpoints.
31. `thm-euler-lagrange-equations` — **theorem** `[LA]`; first variation gives
    $\frac d{dt}\bigl(\partial L/\partial\dot q^i\bigr)
    -\partial L/\partial q^i=0$.
32. `def-fibre-derivative-and-legendre-transform-of-a-lagrangian` —
    **definition** `[LN]`:
    $\mathbb FL(q,v)=\partial L/\partial v\in T_q^*Q$.
33. `def-regular-and-hyperregular-lagrangian` — **definition** `[LN]`; fibre
    Hessian nondegenerate versus global fibrewise Legendre diffeomorphism.
34. `def-energy-and-hamiltonian-of-a-hyperregular-lagrangian` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; $E_L=p(v)-L$ and $H=E_L\circ(\mathbb FL)^{-1}$.
35. `thm-equivalence-of-euler-lagrange-and-hamilton-equations-for-hyperregular-lagrangians` —
    **theorem** `[LA]`; apply the fibrewise inverse theorem and calculate the
    transformed variational equations.
36. `prop-natural-mechanical-lagrangian-gives-kinetic-plus-potential-hamiltonian` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; $L=\frac12g(v,v)-V$ gives
    $H=\frac12g^{-1}(p,p)+V$.

**Completely integrable systems and action–angle coordinates**

37. `def-completely-integrable-hamiltonian-system` — **definition** `[LN]`;
    on a $2n$-manifold, functions $F_1,\ldots,F_n$ have independent
    differentials on the regular locus and pairwise Poisson commute.
38. `prop-regular-common-level-sets-are-lagrangian-submanifolds` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; the Hamiltonian fields span their tangent spaces
    and are mutually symplectically orthogonal.
39. `prop-commuting-hamiltonian-vector-fields-integrate-to-a-local-r-n-action` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; completeness on a compact regular fibre globalizes
    the action there.
40. `lem-stabilizer-of-the-r-n-action-on-a-compact-connected-regular-fibre-is-a-full-lattice` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; the $n$-dimensional orbits are open in the connected
    regular fibre, so there is exactly one orbit. Its stabilizer \(\Gamma\) is
    discrete, and compactness of the identified quotient
    \(\mathbb R^n/\Gamma\) forces \(\Gamma\) to be a full-rank lattice.
41. `thm-compact-connected-regular-fibres-are-tori` — **theorem** `[LA]`.
42. `def-action-and-angle-coordinates` — **definition** `[LN]`; local
    coordinates $(I_1,\ldots,I_n,\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_n)$ with
    $\omega=\sum dI_i\wedge d\theta_i$ and angles periodic.
43. `thm-liouville-arnold-action-angle-theorem` — **theorem** `[LA]`. Near a
    compact connected regular fibre, under the stated completeness/properness
    needed to keep the commuting flows in the neighborhood, there is a
    Lagrangian torus fibration with action–angle coordinates. The integrals
    $F_i$, and any Hamiltonian constant on the fibres of this integrable
    system, depend only on the action variables; an arbitrary Hamiltonian on
    the neighbourhood need not. LANDMARK.
44. `cor-motion-of-a-completely-integrable-hamiltonian-is-linear-on-invariant-tori` —
    **corollary** `[LL]`: $\dot I=0$ and
    $\dot\theta=\partial H/\partial I$.
45. `prop-period-lattice-monodromy-obstructs-global-action-angle-coordinates` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; the local theorem does not assert one global
    coordinate system. A full integral-affine/monodromy theory is deferred.

  convexity, Legendre transform, and applications—is included in items 30–36

### Proof strategy and traps

Nondegeneracy solves $\iota_{X_H}\omega=dH$. Cartan proves preservation of
$\omega$; differentiating contractions gives
$[X_F,X_G]=-X_{\{F,G\}}$, which fixes Jacobi and evolution signs. Liouville
volume follows by wedging, not by a coordinate divergence slogan.

For Liouville–Arnold, commuting Hamiltonian fields span each regular fibre and
integrate to an \(\mathbb R^n\) action. Every orbit is open in the connected
fibre, hence there is one orbit; compactness of the resulting quotient makes
its discrete stabilizer a full period lattice. A local section and period-
normalized cycles yield actions; the relative Poincaré/Moser argument produces
conjugate angles. Properness/completeness and connectedness are never omitted,
and only the integrals or other fibre-constant Hamiltonians are asserted to be
functions of the actions.

**Well-definedness discharged:** Hamiltonian uniqueness/cohomology quotient
and signs (items 1–18), cotangent lift/time dependence/volume (19–29), fibre
Legendre inverse and energy (30–36), and regular level/lattice/action-angle
choices (37–45).
**Choice:** symplectic ODE, finite variational calculus, and torus-lattice
arguments add no choice. Poincaré recurrence inherits MT-22's ledger and is
not used elsewhere.
**Forward reference:** DG-37 packages families of Hamiltonians into moment
maps and performs free regular symplectic reduction.

## DG-37. Hamiltonian actions, moment maps, and symplectic reduction

### A page — `moment-maps-and-symplectic-reduction`

**Requires:** DG-26, DG-29, DG-33, DG-35, DG-36; published quotient topology
and compactness; the regular-value and quotient-manifold results of DG-4; the
homological algebra cited by DG-15 only for the optional equivariant-
cohomology outlook, not for reduction itself.

The sign convention is forced by §§3.3 and 3.5. For a left action,
\(\xi_M(p)=\left.\frac d{dt}\right|_0\exp(-t\xi)\cdot p\), so
\(\xi\mapsto\xi_M\) is a Lie homomorphism. A moment map satisfies
\(d\mu^\xi=-\iota_{\xi_M}\omega\), where
\(\mu^\xi(p)=\langle\mu(p),\xi\rangle\). Thus
\(X_{\mu^\xi}=-\xi_M\), and an equivariant moment map obeys
\(\{\mu^\xi,\mu^\eta\}=\mu^{[\xi,\eta]}\). The library's left coadjoint
action is \(g\cdot\alpha=\alpha\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\).

1. `def-symplectic-and-hamiltonian-lie-group-action` — **definition** `[LN]`;
   a symplectic action preserves \(\omega\), while a Hamiltonian action has
   an equivariant moment map with the displayed differential identity.
2. `def-moment-map-and-component-hamiltonian` — **definition** `[LN]`; records
   \(\mu^\xi\), equivariance, and the library's signs without relying on an
   implicit convention.
3. `prop-infinitesimal-generator-of-a-symplectic-action-is-symplectic` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; differentiate \(g^*\omega=\omega\).
4. `prop-moment-map-components-generate-the-negative-infinitesimal-action` —
   **proposition** `[LL]`; \(X_{\mu^\xi}=-\xi_M\).
5. `prop-equivariance-is-equivalent-to-the-moment-map-poisson-bracket-identity` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; on a connected group and manifold,
   \(\{\mu^\xi,\mu^\eta\}=\mu^{[\xi,\eta]}\), with the appropriate
   infinitesimal formulation stated for disconnected cases.
6. `lem-nonequivariance-defect-of-an-infinitesimal-moment-map-is-a-constant-lie-algebra-two-cocycle` —
   **lemma** `[LA]`; the defect is locally constant on \(M\), bilinear and
   alternating, and Jacobi gives the cocycle equation.
7. `prop-moment-maps-for-one-action-form-an-affine-space-over-coadjoint-fixed-covectors` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; for connected \(M\), two equivariant moment maps
   differ by an element of \((\mathfrak g^*)^G\).
8. `cor-semisimple-hamiltonian-actions-have-unique-equivariant-moment-map-when-one-exists` —
   **corollary** `[LL]`; \([\mathfrak g,\mathfrak g]=\mathfrak g\) annihilates
   the translation freedom.
9. `prop-whitehead-two-removes-the-infinitesimal-equivariance-obstruction-for-semisimple-actions` —
   **proposition** `[LA]`; DG-29 kills the finite-dimensional real cocycle,
   after which connected-group equivariance follows infinitesimally. The item
   states the connectedness hypothesis and does not claim that closed
   one-forms \(\iota_{\xi_M}\omega\) are exact.
10. `thm-noether-conservation-law-for-hamiltonian-actions` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; a \(G\)-invariant Hamiltonian Poisson-commutes with every
    \(\mu^\xi\), hence \(\mu\) is constant along its flow.
11. `prop-equivariant-symplectomorphisms-preserve-moment-maps-up-to-a-coadjoint-fixed-covector` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; for connected $M$, if \(\phi\) is equivariant
    and symplectic, then \(\mu\circ\phi-\mu\in(\mathfrak g^*)^G\). Literal
    preservation is asserted only after a normalization removes this affine
    ambiguity.

**Canonical examples**

12. `prop-cotangent-lift-is-hamiltonian-with-tautological-moment-map` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; for the lifted left action on \(T^*Q\),
    \(\langle\mu(q,p),\xi\rangle=-p(\xi_Q(q))\) under the library's
    fundamental-field convention.
13. `lem-tautological-cotangent-moment-map-is-equivariant` — **lemma** `[LA]`;
    naturality of the tautological one-form supplies equivariance without a
    coordinate choice.
14. `def-kirillov-kostant-souriau-form-on-a-coadjoint-orbit` — **definition**
    `[LN]`; \(\omega_\alpha(\xi_{\mathcal O},\eta_{\mathcal O})
    =\alpha([\xi,\eta])\).
15. `lem-kks-form-is-independent-of-lie-algebra-representatives` — **lemma**
    `[LA]`; changing \(\xi\) or \(\eta\) by an isotropy element changes no
    value.
16. `thm-coadjoint-orbits-are-symplectic-manifolds` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    nondegeneracy is the stabilizer quotient and closedness is Jacobi.
17. `prop-coadjoint-orbit-inclusion-is-an-equivariant-moment-map` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; inclusion \(\mathcal O\hookrightarrow\mathfrak
    g^*\) satisfies the chosen signs.
18. `prop-product-and-opposite-symplectic-moment-maps` — **proposition** `[LA]`;
    moment maps add on products and change sign with \(-\omega\).

**Regular reduction**

19. `lem-differential-of-the-moment-map-and-orbit-orthogonal-identity` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; \(\ker d\mu_p=(T_p(G\cdot p))^\omega\).
20. `prop-regularity-of-a-moment-map-is-equivalent-to-local-freeness` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; \(d\mu_p\) is onto iff the infinitesimal
    stabilizer \(\mathfrak g_p\) is zero.
21. `prop-moment-level-is-invariant-under-the-coadjoint-stabilizer` —
    **proposition** `[LL]`; \(G_\alpha\) acts on \(\mu^{-1}(\alpha)\).
22. `lem-characteristic-kernel-on-a-regular-moment-level` — **lemma** `[LA]`;
    \(\ker(\iota^*\omega)_p=T_p(G_\alpha\cdot p)\) on a regular level.
23. `lem-invariant-horizontal-form-on-a-free-proper-quotient-descends-uniquely` —
    **lemma** `[LA]`; uses DG-26's quotient principal bundle charts and proves
    independence of lifts.
24. `thm-marsden-weinstein-meyer-symplectic-reduction` — **theorem** `[LA]`;
    if \(\alpha\) is regular and the \(G_\alpha\)-action on
    \(\mu^{-1}(\alpha)\) is free and proper, the quotient
    \(M_\alpha=\mu^{-1}(\alpha)/G_\alpha\) has a unique symplectic form
    \(\omega_\alpha\) satisfying
    \(\pi^*\omega_\alpha=\iota^*\omega\). LANDMARK.
25. `cor-zero-level-symplectic-reduction-and-dimension-formula` — **corollary**
    `[LL]`; if \(0\) is regular and \(G\) acts freely and properly on the
    level, \(\dim M_0=\dim M-2\dim G\).
26. `prop-dimension-of-a-regular-nonzero-reduced-space` — **proposition**
    `[LL]`; \(\dim M_\alpha=\dim M-\dim G-\dim G_\alpha\) under item 24's
    hypotheses.
27. `prop-invariant-hamiltonians-descend-to-reduced-hamiltonians` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; invariant flow preserves the level and projects
    to the Hamiltonian flow of the descended function.
28. `prop-reduction-commutes-with-products` — **proposition** `[LA]`; at
    product values and under the stated product-action hypotheses.
29. `thm-reduction-in-stages-for-free-proper-regular-actions` — **theorem**
    `[LA]`; for a closed normal subgroup, under explicit regularity,
    freeness, properness, and residual-action hypotheses, two-stage reduction
    is canonically symplectomorphic to one-stage reduction.
30. `prop-shifting-trick-identifies-reduction-at-alpha-with-zero-reduction` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; use \(M\times\mathcal O_\alpha^{-}\) with diagonal
    moment map, carefully recording the stabilizer and quotient map.

**Singular boundary and compact consequences**

31. `prop-compact-group-symplectic-actions-admit-an-invariant-compatible-almost-complex-structure` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; average a background metric using DG-33's
    normalized Haar measure, then polar-decompose against \(\omega\).
32. `prop-compact-group-moment-map-can-be-averaged-to-an-equivariant-one-when-the-affine-obstruction-vanishes` —
    **proposition** `[LA]`; the exact hypotheses and normalization are stated,
    and averaging is not used to manufacture exact component one-forms.
33. `rem-nonregular-or-nonfree-symplectic-quotients-need-not-be-manifolds` —
    **remark** `[LN]`; stratified symplectic reduction and orbifolds are
    explicitly outside this page's theorem.
34. `rem-convexity-and-toric-classification-for-hamiltonian-torus-actions` —
    **remark** `[LN]`; Atiyah–Guillemin–Sternberg convexity, Delzant's theorem,
    and localization are valuable but not compressed into uncited corollaries.

### Proof strategy and traps

Differentiate equivariance before manipulating signs. The two identities
\(d\mu^\xi=-\iota_{\xi_M}\omega\) and
\(\xi_M=\frac d{dt}|_0\exp(-t\xi)\cdot p\) imply both
\(X_{\mu^\xi}=-\xi_M\) and the positive component bracket for an equivariant
map; no convention is imported silently from a source using \(\exp(t\xi)\).

For reduction, prove item 19 first. The regular-value theorem gives the level
submanifold, DG-26 gives the free proper quotient manifold, and item 22 says
exactly that the restricted two-form's radical is vertical. Invariance and
horizontality make it descend; nondegeneracy follows from the radical
calculation. This order prevents the common circular proof that calls the
quotient form symplectic before proving it is well defined.

**Well-definedness discharged:** component linearity and coadjoint action
(items 1–11), cotangent and orbit representative independence (12–18), tangent
lifts and descent through the quotient (19–30), and Haar normalization in the
compact averaging items (31–32).
**Choice:** finite-dimensional moment-map algebra and free proper reduction are
ZF once their input manifolds and quotients exist. Items 31–32 inherit DG-33's
compact-Haar construction and its choice charge; neither is a dependency of
the reduction theorem.
**Forward references:** singular reduction/orbifolds, moment-polytope
convexity, toric classification, localization, and equivariant cohomology are
deliberately withheld and recorded in §10.

---

## 6. Consolidated well-definedness and proof-obligation ledger

The local ledgers under every pair are binding. This consolidated pass names
the obligations most likely to disappear when the pages are built; “standard”
is not a discharge.

### 6.1 Smooth structures and local-to-global constructions

1. **Atlas closure.** `thm-each-smooth-atlas-is-contained-in-a-unique-maximal-smooth-atlas`
   proves compatibility of the union and uniqueness. It is a definable union,
   not a maximal object selected by Zorn.
2. **Chart independence.** `lem-chart-independence-of-c-r-smoothness`,
   `thm-coordinate-derivations-form-a-basis-of-the-tangent-space`, and
   `thm-curve-contact-classes-are-canonically-isomorphic-to-derivation-tangent-vectors`
   check transition maps and germ representatives before smooth maps,
   tangent vectors, or the differential are used.
3. **Boundary invariance.** `thm-smooth-invariance-of-manifold-boundary`
   uses the one-sided chain rule to show that the differentials of mutually
   inverse half-space transition maps are inverse. If a boundary point mapped
   to an interior point, the last coordinate of the local inverse would be a
   nonnegative smooth function with an interior zero, hence would have zero
   differential there, contradicting invertibility. Neither invariance of
   domain nor a picture of a half-ball is substituted for this argument.
4. **Smooth partitions.** The normalized denominator is pointwise positive,
   local finiteness makes its sum smooth, supports remain subordinate after
   normalization, and extension by zero is invoked only when support is
   closed inside the chart domain. DG-2 supplies a countable locally finite
   exhaustion before any infinite sum is differentiated; DG-13 item 15
   repeats the construction with Euclidean bumps restricted to half-space
   charts before any boundary-global field or integral uses it.
5. **Bundle gluing.** DG-3 and DG-5 verify the cocycle identities,
   Hausdorffness and second countability of the total space, independence of
   local representatives, smoothness of inverse transitions, and the
   universal properties of pullback, direct sum, dual, tensor-Hom, and
   quotient bundles.
6. **Rank and submanifolds.** Rank is invariant under chart changes; slice
   charts give a unique embedded smooth structure; regular-level tangent
   spaces are kernels; induced maps on a quotient or image are proved
   independent of representatives. An injective immersion is never silently
   given the subspace topology.
7. **Tubular data.** The normal bundle is defined as an orthogonal complement
   only after a metric is selected, the resulting tubular neighborhood is
   shown independent up to the claimed germ/isotopy, and a nearest-point map
   is asserted only on the constructed neighborhood.

### 6.2 Differential equations, fields, and foliations

8. **Picard operator.** DG-8 specifies its complete closed sup-norm ball,
   proves that the operator maps the ball to itself and is contractive, and
   derives a common time interval before differentiating in parameters.
9. **Maximal solutions.** Local solutions glue because uniqueness holds on
   overlaps; the union of all solution intervals is an interval; the maximal
   flow domain is open. No arbitrary choice of one solution through each
   point is made.
10. **Brackets and Lie derivatives.** The commutator of derivations is again a
    derivation and varies smoothly; the flow, commutator, and coordinate
    formulas agree; pullback versus pushforward signs are checked against the
    convention in §3.
11. **Frobenius.** A distribution is a constant-rank smooth subbundle, not
    merely pointwise subspaces. Flow preservation, independence of the local
    frame, compatibility of plaque charts, and the equivalence relation used
    to assemble maximal leaves are proved before the leaf topology is named.

### 6.3 Exterior calculus, integration, and de Rham comparison

12. **Exterior powers.** DG-11 constructs finite-dimensional exterior powers
    through alternating multilinear forms and duality, proves the normalized
    shuffle product independent of representatives, and does not cite the
    unauthored module tensor-product page. DG-27 separately waits for that
    page before forming arbitrary tensor algebras and enveloping quotients.
13. **Forms.** Tensor transitions obey the cocycle law; pullback is pointwise
    independent of extensions; the coordinate definition of \(d\) is invariant;
    \(d^2=0\), the graded Leibniz rule, contraction signs, and Cartan's formula
    are proved on a local frame and then globalized.
14. **Orientations.** The determinant-line and nowhere-zero-top-form
    descriptions are proved equivalent in every dimension; positive-basis,
    atlas, and frame-bundle descriptions are invoked only in positive
    dimension. In dimension zero, the two rays of \(\Lambda^0V=\mathbb R\)
    are retained explicitly, which supports signed boundary points. Boundary
    orientation is outward-normal-first, and changing a defining function or
    collar does not change the induced orientation.
15. **Integration.** A chart-supported integral is independent of its
   oriented coordinates by the published compact-support change-of-variables
   theorem. At a true half-space face, only the artificial edges have smooth
   zero extension; the face extension is bounded and Riemann integrable by
   an inline coordinate-hyperplane nullity estimate and the published
   Riemann-integrability criterion. Boundary transitions are extended locally
   to side-preserving Euclidean diffeomorphisms before change of variables is
   applied. A partitioned global integral is a finite sum on each compact
   support and is independent of the atlas and partition. Densities use
   \(|\det|\); signed top forms use \(\det\). Neither is substituted for the
   other.
16. **Stokes.** Artificial chart edges have the required support buffer;
    interior charts vanish by the fundamental theorem of calculus; the
    merely Riemann-integrable zero extension at the genuine boundary face
    produces exactly the outward-first term; locally finite partitions can
    be differentiated termwise on the relevant compact set.
17. **de Rham quotient.** Exact forms are closed because \(d^2=0\); pullback
    and wedge descend to cohomology; the homotopy operator preserves the
    advertised support conditions. The Mayer–Vietoris extension is written
    using a two-function smooth partition and checked on the overlap before
    the homological-algebra long exact sequence is cited.
18. **Singular localization.** Face identities give \(\partial^2=0\);
    barycentric subdivision and its prism homotopy are canonical; a finite
    chain becomes cover-small after finitely many subdivisions; extension by
    zero is defined on the singular-simplex basis and yields the short exact
    cochain sequence. This avoids the unsupported assertion that
    \(\mathbb R\) is injective as an abelian group.
19. **Smooth comparison.** A smooth simplex extends to a neighborhood of the
    closed standard simplex; relative-face smoothing respects already smooth
    faces; only the finitely many simplices in a chain are smoothed at once.
    Chain Stokes, naturality, and compatibility of the two connecting maps are
    separate lemmas. Hence the de Rham map is independent of both form and
    cochain representatives before the five-lemma induction.
20. **Degree.** Compact support makes pullback integrable for proper maps;
    normalization is independent of the chosen generator of top compact-
    support cohomology once orientations are fixed; regular-value signs are
    chart-independent; homotopy invariance is stated only for a proper
    homotopy where required.

### 6.4 Riemannian geometry and curvature

21. **Metric constructions.** Convex combinations of positive-definite forms
    remain positive definite; length is invariant under orientation-
    preserving reparametrization and unchanged under general monotone
    reparametrization in the stated sense; Riemannian distance is finite on a
    connected component and induces the manifold topology.
22. **Volume.** The Riemannian density is chart-independent; on an oriented
    manifold it agrees with the chosen positive volume form. Arbitrary
    measurable integration is not used until the planned measure page is
    cited.
23. **Connections.** Local connection matrices transform affinely, so the
    patched covariant derivative is independent of frame. Induced dual,
    tensor, Hom, determinant, and pullback connections respect their defining
    evaluation identities. Parallel transport is independent of curve
    coordinates and composes under concatenation.
24. **Levi–Civita and geodesics.** The Koszul formula is \(C^\infty\)-linear in
    the direction slot $X$ and satisfies the Leibniz rule in the
    differentiated slot $Y$; these identities make the defined object a
    smooth connection. Its uniqueness uses both torsion-freeness and metric
    compatibility. The geodesic spray is
    a global vector field on \(TM\); exponential-map domains, affine
    reparametrizations, normal neighborhoods, and radial coordinates are
    stated with their actual domains.
25. **Completeness.** Metric, geodesic, exponential, and compact-ball forms of
    Hopf–Rinow are proved equivalent only on connected finite-dimensional
    Riemannian manifolds. “Every pair has a minimizing geodesic” is not used
    before existence is proved.
26. **Curvature.** Tensoriality of \(R\), frame independence of traces, the
    symmetries and Bianchi identities, and the independence of sectional
    curvature from the chosen basis of a two-plane are explicit. Gauss,
    Weingarten, Codazzi, and Ricci equations use §3.2's signs throughout.
27. **Jacobi and cut data.** Variation fields and ODE solutions coincide;
    \(d\exp\) is identified through the correct rescaling; multiplicity of a
    conjugate point is kernel dimension; cut time may precede conjugacy; any
    statement “off the cut locus” names the open domain on which distance is
    smooth.
28. **Comparison.** Index-form comparison verifies endpoints and absence of
    conjugate points; Riccati inequalities are interpreted only before the
    first conjugate time; polar Jacobians have their correct limiting value;
    Bishop–Gromov ratios are defined at zero by a limit and use the measure
    track for almost-everywhere/Radon arguments. The covering seam is used
    only for the complete-local-isometry theorem, Cartan–Hadamard's covering
    conclusion, and cover-based corollaries. Toponogov instead uses hinge
    comparison, support functions, and finite subdivision and has no covering-
    space dependency.
29. **Gauss–Bonnet.** Turning angle conventions agree with boundary
    orientation; the local formula includes corners; a finite geodesic
    triangulation is constructed or cited with exact hypotheses; interior
    edge and vertex contributions cancel combinatorially; the final integral
    is independent of triangulation.

### 6.5 Lie theory

30. **Lie functor.** Left-invariant extensions identify \(T_eG\) with the
    invariant fields, so the bracket is independent of an extension. One-
    parameter subgroup uniqueness fixes the exponential map; naturality,
    \(d\exp_0=\mathrm{id}\), and the local inverse are proved without claiming
    global injectivity or surjectivity.
31. **Actions and quotients.** The \(\exp(-t\xi)\) fundamental field is a Lie
    homomorphism. Orbit maps factor through \(G/G_p\); closed-subgroup and
    free-proper quotient atlases are Hausdorff and second countable; descended
    maps and tensors are independent of representatives. “Immersed subgroup,”
    “embedded subgroup,” and “closed subgroup” are never interchanged.
32. **Enveloping algebra.** The two-sided ideal imposing
    \(x\otimes y-y\otimes x-[x,y]\) is specified; induced maps annihilate the
    ideal; the universal factorization is unique. Ordered PBW monomials depend
    on a chosen ordered basis, while
    \(\operatorname{gr}U(\mathfrak g)\cong S(\mathfrak g)\) is canonical.
33. **Radicals and cohomology.** Derived and lower-central series are ideals;
    sums and quotients used to define the radical and nilradical are checked;
    the Killing form is basis-independent and invariant. The Chevalley–
    Eilenberg differential squares to zero with the declared sign, and
    Whitehead lemmas are not inferred from one another.
34. **Integration.** A Lie-algebra morphism integrates first on a simply
    connected group; descent through a covering is characterized by its
    discrete central kernel. Ado, Lie II, and Lie III state their field,
    connectedness, and simply-connectedness hypotheses separately.
35. **Roots.** General Cartan subalgebras are nilpotent and self-normalizing;
    the maximal-toral equivalence is asserted only in the complex semisimple
    case. Root spaces, coroots, reflections, and Cartan integers are
    independent of root-vector scaling; a choice of positive roots is fixed
    before “highest” or “dominant” is used.
36. **Classification and weights.** Diagram isomorphism is separated from
    algebra isomorphism until Serre existence and uniqueness are proved.
    Low-rank coincidences are normalized. RL-2 owns Verma-module quotients and
    maximal proper submodules; DG classifies finite-dimensional irreducibles
    directly by dominant integral weights relative to the chosen root datum,
    not merely the root system of a global group.
37. **Compact and real forms.** Haar measure is normalized before averaging;
    Peter–Weyl closures are in the stated topology. The lattice
    \(Q\subseteq X^*(T)\subseteq P\) records central isogeny. A real form is the
    fixed set of a conjugate-linear involution, Cartan decomposition is
    independent up to the proved conjugacy, restricted roots may be
    nonreduced, and Vogan/Satake equivalence—not an ordinary Dynkin diagram—
    classifies the recorded real form.

### 6.6 Symplectic geometry and mechanics

38. **Symplectic linear algebra.** Musical maps and symplectic complements
    use nondegeneracy; Lagrangian, coisotropic and symplectic subspaces have
    the stated dimension identities. The canonical cotangent form is intrinsic
    and yields \(\omega=-d\lambda\) in every cotangent chart.
39. **Moser and normal forms.** The time-dependent field exists on the common
    compact domain, its flow remains there, and the cohomology hypothesis
    produces a smoothly varying primitive. Relative versions vanish on the
    fixed submanifold. Tubular identifications and normal-bundle forms are
    independent up to the exact germ claimed by Weinstein's theorem.
40. **Hamiltonians.** Nondegeneracy gives a unique \(X_H\); functions for one
    field differ by locally constant functions; the quotient by constants is
    taken componentwise. All Jacobi/evolution signs follow from §3.5.
    Legendre inversion is fibrewise and only for regular/hyperregular
    Lagrangians under the printed hypotheses.
41. **Action–angle data.** The common level is regular before it is called a
    submanifold; completeness globalizes the commuting flows; the stabilizer
    is a full lattice only on a compact connected fibre. The resulting angles
    are local and period-lattice monodromy is not erased.
42. **Moment maps.** Components are linear in \(\xi\), equivariance uses the
    declared coadjoint action, the infinitesimal defect is a constant cocycle
    only on connected components, the cotangent sign is checked directly,
    and the KKS form is independent of representatives modulo the stabilizer.
43. **Reduction.** The moment level is an embedded submanifold at a regular
    value; its characteristic kernel equals the stabilizer orbit; the
    restricted form is invariant and horizontal; hence it descends uniquely
    through the free proper quotient and is nondegenerate. Nonregular or
    nonfree quotients are never called smooth manifolds.

---

## 7. Choice-strength ledger

The ledger distinguishes a theorem's ordinary mathematical hypotheses from
the set-theoretic strength of constructing its inputs. “ZF” below is relative
to the library's already constructed real numbers and the named finite-
dimensional algebra/calculus inputs.

| Pair(s) | Strength planned | Reason and inheritance |
|---|---|---|
| DG-1, DG-3–DG-5, DG-11–DG-12 | ZF locally; \(\mathrm{AC}_\omega\) sufficient if a countable atlas must be extracted rather than supplied | Maximal atlases are definable unions. Finite-dimensional germs, quotients, duals, transitions, rank normal forms, and exterior algebra make no infinite selections. A countable chart refinement can require countably many chart choices. |
| DG-2; DG-13 item 15 and its global consumers | \(\mathrm{AC}_\omega\) is sufficient for the explicit manifold-specific constructions | A fixed countable coordinate-ball or half-ball basis is filtered canonically; countable choice selects containing cover members and chart assignments, after which shrinking, bump construction, and normalization are explicit. DG-13's local boundary calculus and supplied orientation data remain ZF. The published arbitrary-space paracompactness/partition theorem has the larger AC+DC upper bound only as a comparison and is not the route used here. |
| DG-6 | ZF for Euclidean Sard; \(\mathrm{AC}_\omega\) sufficient for globalization from second countability | The null-set induction is finite/countable. Global chart reduction uses a supplied countable atlas or selects one. |
| DG-7 | inherits DG-2; no additional choice in each finite projection step | Proper embedding, tubular neighborhoods, and smooth approximation use globally chosen locally finite smooth data. The finite-dimensional Sard projections add none. |
| DG-8 | ZF | Picard iteration is a specified sequence in a specified complete sup-norm space; the published constructive Banach fixed-point theorem is cited. |
| DG-9–DG-10 | ZF locally; DG-10 inherits DG-2's \(\mathrm{AC}_\omega\)-sufficient bound for the global countable-atlas step | Maximal solutions are unions of compatible local solutions, not one chosen solution per point. Local Frobenius composes finitely many canonical flows. Proving that every maximal leaf has a second-countable plaque atlas selects and countably organizes charts along a countable ambient basis, so the global leaf theorem does not claim a stronger ZF bound. Compactly extending a frame has the same inheritance. |
| DG-14–DG-15 | inherits DG-2 for global partitions; ZF for each chart, Stokes reduction, homotopy operator, and finite Mayer–Vietoris diagram | A compact support meets finitely many members of a supplied locally finite refinement. Generic cohomological exactness inherits HA's ledger. |
| DG-16 | inherits AT-2/AT-3/AT-6 for ordinary singular chains, subdivision/prism, and cohomology; its real two-open presentation is ZF, and \(\mathrm{AC}_\omega\) is sufficient for countable open-set globalization | AT owns the canonical finite-chain construction. The DG global comparison over a countable manifold basis inherits DG-2's countable organization, and relative smoothing inherits DG-7. No basis-wide smoothing and no injectivity/Zorn argument is used. |
| DG-17 | inherits DG-7 for smooth approximation and DG-2 for globalization; finite five-lemma steps are ZF | The comparison smooths one finite chain relative to its faces. It does not choose a smoothing of every singular simplex simultaneously. |
| DG-18–DG-19 | metric/connection formulas ZF once the objects are supplied; existence inherits DG-2 | Convex patching of local metrics and bundle connections uses a smooth partition. Parallel transport along one curve is constructive ODE theory. |
| DG-20 | ZF relative to the supplied metric under the selected compact-unit-sphere proof | The scaffold explicitly avoids the unused proof that selects one \(1/n\)-minimizer for each \(n\), which would require \(\mathrm{AC}_\omega\). Local geodesic and normal-neighborhood results are likewise ZF. |
| DG-21–DG-22 | ZF relative to the chosen metric/connection for curvature, Jacobi, index, and cut-time geometry; DG-22 items 36–37 inherit MT | Finite-dimensional tensor calculations and Jacobi ODEs make no infinite family of choices. Cut-locus nullity and polar integration specifically inherit MT's Radon/Fubini/null-set ledger. |
| DG-23 | inherits DG-20, the future covering-page ledger, and the measure track's Lebesgue/Radon ledger | Rauch and pointwise Riccati comparison are ZF. Bishop–Gromov and almost-everywhere polar integration use the measure machinery; lift arguments use covering theory. |
| DG-24 | inherits DG-2/DG-7; the finite cancellation argument is ZF | Producing the smooth/geodesic triangulation uses global smooth approximation. Once a finite triangulation is supplied, local Gauss–Bonnet and Euler counting add no choice. |
| DG-25–DG-32 | ZF for finite-dimensional local/abstract algebra; Lie II/III inherit the covering-page ledger | PBW uses a supplied ordered basis and finite words. Radical, Levi, root, Dynkin, and highest-weight arguments make only finite-dimensional choices inside a single object. No simultaneous choice over a family of Lie algebras is asserted. |
| DG-33 | inherits measure theory for Haar measure and functional analysis for Hilbert bases/compact operators | Haar averaging itself is an integral against the constructed normalized measure. Peter–Weyl may inherit the library's orthonormal-basis/Zorn strength; the future page must quote that ledger exactly. |
| DG-34 | local real-form structure is ZF; global group forms inherit covering theory; compact averaging inherits DG-33 | Cartan involution calculations are finite-dimensional. Conjugacy/globalization uses the printed group hypotheses and no stronger claim. |
| DG-35 | local symplectic algebra and Moser's equation are ZF; global compatible structures/tubular data inherit DG-2/DG-7 | A compact time interval gives one canonical flow once its vector field is fixed. |
| DG-36 | Hamiltonian algebra and finite-dimensional variational arguments are ZF; recurrence inherits the precise MT-22 ledger | Action–angle uses a finite basis of one period lattice. Poincaré recurrence is optional and no other item depends on it. |
| DG-37 | regular reduction is ZF once the free proper quotient exists; compact averaging inherits DG-33 | Descent is unique, not chosen. The regular reduction theorem does not use Haar averaging or singular stratification. |

Two global prohibitions follow. First, orientability of every connected
component does not by itself select orientations simultaneously for an
arbitrary family of components; orientation is supplied structure unless a
choice mechanism is printed. Second, no claim in the de Rham proof uses
“\(\mathbb R\) is injective” or extends a homomorphism from an arbitrary
subgroup: that common shortcut hides a Zorn/full-AC step and is unnecessary
for the cover-small cochain construction.

---

## 8. Canonical-coverage harvest

### 8.0 Corpus, access, and disposition syntax

The tables below are the source-anchored harvest; the shorter source blocks
under each pair are its local proof controls. Full texts were inspected, not
merely search snippets. A disposition has one of the required forms:
`included(item-id)`, `inline(item-id)`, `already-published(item-id)`,
`deferred(reason)`, or `out-of-scope(reason)`. A span such as “DG-11 items
1–9” is used only after at least one exact item id is printed in that row.
Problem headings are not mathematical-result headings and are not separately
harvested; a problem whose result is used is named explicitly.

**Full standard texts obtained:**

- John M. Lee, *Introduction to Smooth Manifolds*, 2nd ed., complete
  searchable text: <https://pierre.ag.gerard.web.ulb.be/textbooks/books/Introduction_To_Smooth_Manifolds.pdf>;
  author page and current corrections:
  <https://sites.math.washington.edu/~lee/Books/ISM/>. The complete first
  edition used for the early chapter cross-check is
  <https://edu.fjfi.cvut.cz/studijni-materialy/Ing/4.%20ro%C4%8Dn%C3%ADk/GMF2/John%20M.%20Lee%20%28auth.%29%20-%20Introduction%20to%20Smooth%20Manifolds-Springer%20New%20York%20%282003%29.pdf>.
- John M. Lee, *Riemannian Manifolds: An Introduction to Curvature* (GTM
  176, 1997), complete
  searchable text: <https://www.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/leeriemm.pdf>.
- Liviu Nicolaescu, *Lectures on the Geometry of Manifolds*, 3rd ed., complete
  author-hosted monograph: <https://www3.nd.edu/~lnicolae/Lectures.pdf>.
- Anthony Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction*, digital 2nd ed.,
  complete author-hosted monograph:
  <https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~aknapp/download/Beyond2-clickable.pdf>.

**Full lecture-note and focused-source corpus obtained:** Merry,
<https://www2.math.ethz.ch/will-merry/files/Merry%20-%20Differential%20Geometry%20%282021%29.pdf>;
Hitchin,
<https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/~joyce/Nairobi2019/Hitchin-DifferentiableManifolds.pdf>;
Robbin–Salamon differential geometry,
<https://people.math.ethz.ch/~salamon/PREPRINTS/diffgeo.pdf>, and differential
topology,

Eschenburg,

### 8.1 Lee, *Introduction to Smooth Manifolds*, 2nd ed.

**Range actually read:** Chs. 1–10, 12–19, 21–22, and Appendix D; contents
and the proofs surrounding every named control cited below. The early Chs.
1–4 were also checked in the complete first edition because several printed
theorem numbers differ.

- Ch. 1, “Topological Manifolds,” “Smooth Structures,” “Examples of Smooth
  Manifolds,” and “Manifolds with Boundary” →
  `included(def-topological-manifold-without-boundary)`,
  `included(def-smooth-structure-generated-by-an-atlas)`, DG-1 examples, and
  `included(def-smooth-chart-atlas-and-structure-on-a-manifold-with-boundary)`.
  The boundary-invariance named result →
  `included(thm-smooth-invariance-of-manifold-boundary)`.
- Ch. 2, “Smooth Functions and Smooth Maps” →
  `included(def-c-r-and-smooth-maps-between-smooth-manifolds)` and
  `included(lem-chart-independence-of-c-r-smoothness)`; “Partitions of Unity”
  → `included(thm-smooth-partitions-of-unity-exist-on-manifolds)` and, for
  half-space charts,
  `included(thm-smooth-partitions-of-unity-exist-on-manifolds-with-boundary)`.
  The named
  extension/bump results →
  `included(thm-smooth-urysohn-lemma-for-a-closed-set-in-an-open-set)` and
  DG-2 items 20–21.
- Ch. 3, “Tangent Vectors,” “The Differential of a Smooth Map,”
  “Computations in Coordinates,” “The Tangent Bundle,” “Velocity Vectors of
  Curves,” and “Alternative Definitions of the Tangent Space” →
  `included(def-derivation-at-a-point-and-tangent-space)`,
  `included(def-differential-of-a-smooth-map)`,
  `included(thm-the-tangent-bundle-has-a-canonical-smooth-2n-manifold-structure)`,
  and `included(thm-curve-contact-classes-are-canonically-isomorphic-to-derivation-tangent-vectors)`.
  “Categories and Functors” →
  `inline(prop-the-tangent-bundle-construction-is-functorial)`;
  `out-of-scope(generic category theory is not a differential-geometric
  result and no further generic categorical theorem is used here)`.
- Ch. 4, “Maps of Constant Rank,” “Embeddings,” and “Submersions” →
  `included(thm-constant-rank-theorem-for-manifolds)`,
  `included(def-smooth-embedding)`, and
  `included(cor-local-normal-form-for-submersions)`. The Rank Theorem,
  Global Rank Theorem, and local embedding/submersion results are included or
  absorbed by DG-4 items 8–14. “Smooth Covering Maps” →
  `deferred(covering-spaces-and-lifting owns the general smooth/topological
  covering and lifting theory)`. Only its later Lie-group specialization is
  `inline(def-covering-homomorphism-of-lie-groups)` on DG-26.
- Ch. 5, “Embedded Submanifolds,” “Immersed Submanifolds,” “Restricting Maps
  to Submanifolds,” “The Tangent Space to a Submanifold,” and “Submanifolds
  with Boundary” → `included(def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart)`,
  `included(def-immersed-submanifold)`,
  `included(prop-smoothness-into-an-embedded-submanifold-is-an-initial-property)`,
  `inline(prop-the-inclusion-of-an-embedded-submanifold-is-a-smooth-embedding)`
  together with the slice-chart calculation
  \(d\iota_p(T_pS)=\mathbb R^k\times\{0\}\), and
  `included(def-embedded-smooth-submanifold-with-boundary)`. The regular-level
  special case separately gives
  `included(prop-tangent-space-of-a-regular-level-set-is-the-kernel)`.
  The Regular Level Set/Preimage
  theorems → `included(thm-a-regular-level-set-is-an-embedded-submanifold)`
  and `included(thm-preimage-theorem-for-submanifolds-under-submersions)`.
- Ch. 6, “Sets of Measure Zero,” “Sard's Theorem,” “The Whitney Embedding
  Theorem,” “The Whitney Approximation Theorems,” and “Transversality” →
  `included(def-null-subset-of-a-smooth-manifold)`,
  `included(thm-morse-sard-for-smooth-manifolds)`,
  `included(thm-weak-whitney-proper-embedding-theorem)`,
  `included(thm-whitney-approximation-for-manifold-valued-maps)`,
  and `included(thm-parametric-transversality)`. The stronger
  \(2n\) Whitney theorem is
  `included(rem-strong-whitney-embedding-theorem)` as an exact source-stated,

  Whitney-trick machinery is
  `deferred(a differential-topology sequel is required for a full proof)`.
- Ch. 7, “Basic Definitions,” “Lie Group Homomorphisms,” “Lie Subgroups,” and

  `included(thm-differential-of-a-lie-group-homomorphism-is-a-lie-algebra-homomorphism)`,
