  `included(def-smooth-left-action-of-a-lie-group)`.
- Ch. 8, “Vector Fields on Manifolds,” “Vector Fields and Smooth Maps,” “Lie
  Brackets,” and “The Lie Algebra of a Lie Group” →
  `included(def-smooth-vector-field-as-a-tangent-bundle-section)`, `included(def-f-related-vector-fields)`,
  `included(def-lie-bracket-of-smooth-vector-fields)`, and
  `included(def-lie-bracket-on-the-tangent-space-of-a-lie-group)`.
- Ch. 9, “Integral Curves,” “Flows,” “Flowouts,” “Flows and Flowouts on
  Manifolds with Boundary,” “Lie Derivatives,” “Commuting Vector Fields,” and
  “Time-Dependent Vector Fields” →
  `included(def-integral-curve-of-a-vector-field)`,
  `included(thm-fundamental-theorem-on-flows)`, DG-13's collar proof,
  `included(def-lie-derivative-of-a-tensor-field)`,
  `included(thm-two-vector-fields-commute-if-and-only-if-their-local-flows-commute)`, and
  `included(thm-time-dependent-vector-fields-have-local-smooth-evolution-operators)`.
  “First-Order Partial Differential Equations” →
  `out-of-scope(the PDE track owns the local first-order PDE and
  characteristics application; the Pfaffian theorem does not absorb this
  heading)`.
- Ch. 10, “Vector Bundles,” “Local and Global Sections of Vector Bundles,” “Bundle
  Homomorphisms,” “Subbundles,” and “Fiber Bundles” →
  `included(def-smooth-vector-bundle-rank-fibre-and-trivial-bundle)`,
  `included(def-smooth-section-local-section-and-support)`,
  `included(def-vector-bundle-map-over-a-smooth-base-map)`,
  `included(def-vector-subbundle)`, and
  `included(def-smooth-fibre-bundle-and-local-trivialization)`. General obstruction/classifying-space
  theory → `deferred(it belongs to algebraic topology and characteristic
  classes, neither needed here)`.
- Ch. 12, “Multilinear Algebra,” “Symmetric and Alternating Tensors,” and
  “Tensors and Tensor Fields on Manifolds” →
  `included(def-type-r-s-tensor-on-a-finite-dimensional-vector-space)`,
  `included(def-alternating-k-covectors)`, and
  `included(def-smooth-tensor-field)`.
- Ch. 13, “Riemannian Manifolds,” “The Riemannian Distance Function,” and
  “The Tangent–Cotangent Isomorphism” →
  `included(def-riemannian-metric-and-riemannian-manifold)`,
  `included(def-riemannian-distance-on-a-connected-manifold)`, and
  `included(thm-the-musical-maps-are-smooth-inverse-bundle-isomorphisms)`.
  “Pseudo-Riemannian Metrics” →
  `out-of-scope(the positive-definite theory is the commissioned remit and
  later proofs use norms and distance)`.
- Ch. 14, “The Algebra of Alternating Tensors,” “Differential Forms on
  Manifolds,” and “Exterior Derivatives” →
  `included(def-wedge-product-of-alternating-covectors)`,
  `included(def-smooth-differential-k-form)`, and
  `included(thm-existence-and-uniqueness-of-the-exterior-derivative)`.
  Properties of wedge, pullback, coordinate \(d\), invariant \(d\), and
  Cartan's magic formula → DG-11/DG-12, including
  `included(thm-cartans-magic-formula)`.
- Ch. 15, “Orientations of Vector Spaces,” “Orientations of Manifolds,” “The
  Riemannian Volume Form,” and “Orientations and Covering Maps” →
  `included(def-orientation-of-a-finite-dimensional-real-vector-space)`,
  `included(def-oriented-smooth-manifold-and-oriented-chart)`,
  `included(def-riemannian-volume-form-on-an-oriented-manifold)`, and
  `inline(prop-pointwise-orientation-sign-of-a-local-diffeomorphism)`
  for the local behavior of local diffeomorphisms. General orientation-cover
  construction and classification are
  `deferred(the unauthored covering-spaces-and-lifting page must first supply
  the covering and lifting theory)`.
- Ch. 16, “The Geometry of Volume Measurement,” “Integration of Differential
  Forms,” “Stokes's Theorem,” “Integration on Riemannian Manifolds,” and
  “Densities” →
  `included(def-integral-of-an-oriented-chart-supported-top-form)`,
  `included(thm-general-stokes-theorem)`,
  `included(def-riemannian-volume-density)`, and
  `included(def-density-bundle-and-smooth-density)`. “Manifolds with Corners”
  and the named corners Stokes theorem →
  `deferred(the scaffold proves only the piecewise-smooth two-dimensional
  corner formula needed for Gauss–Bonnet; general corners require their own
  atlas and boundary-face theory)`. Green, divergence, and surface Stokes
  named corollaries → DG-14 examples, including
  `included(thm-divergence-theorem-relative-to-a-volume-form)`.
- Ch. 17, “The de Rham Cohomology Groups,” “Homotopy Invariance,” “The
  Mayer–Vietoris Theorem,” “Degree Theory,” and “Proof of the Mayer–Vietoris
  Theorem” → `included(def-de-rham-cohomology)`,
  `included(cor-smoothly-homotopic-maps-induce-the-same-de-rham-map)`,
  `included(thm-mayer-vietoris-sequence-in-de-rham-cohomology)`,
  and
  `included(def-degree-of-a-proper-smooth-map-by-compact-support-cohomology)`
  on DG-17.
  Lee's Zigzag Lemma →
  `deferred(HA-4 owns the general long-exact-sequence construction and is
  scaffolded but unauthored)`; the Five Lemma →
  `already-published(thm-five-lemma-for-modules)`.
- Ch. 18, “Singular Homology,” “Singular Cohomology,” “Smooth Singular
  Homology,” and “The de Rham Theorem” →
  `included(def-real-singular-chain-complex)`,
  `included(def-real-singular-cochain-complex)`,
  `included(def-smooth-singular-chain-and-cochain-complexes)`,
  and `included(thm-de-rham-theorem)`.
  Lee's injective-\(\mathbb R\) Mayer–Vietoris shortcut and sketched
  subdivision argument are not proof sources; Hatcher/Park supply the
  replacement in §8.3.
- Ch. 19, “Distributions and Involutivity,” “The Frobenius Theorem,”
  “Foliations,” and “Lie Subalgebras and Lie Subgroups” →
  `included(def-smooth-distribution-on-a-manifold)`,
  `included(thm-frobenius-local-coordinate-theorem)`,
  `included(def-regular-foliation-atlas)`, and
  `included(thm-lie-subgroup-lie-subalgebra-correspondence)`.
  “Overdetermined Systems of Partial Differential Equations” →
  `inline(thm-pfaffian-frobenius-criterion)` for the integrability criterion;
  analytic PDE beyond it is `out-of-scope(the PDE track owns it)`.
- Ch. 21, “Quotients of Manifolds by Group Actions,” “Covering Manifolds,”
  “Homogeneous Spaces,” and “Applications to Lie Theory” →
  `included(thm-free-proper-action-quotient-manifold)`, DG-26's
  smooth-covering bridge,
  `included(thm-quotient-manifold-by-a-closed-lie-subgroup)`, and
  Lie II/III items in DG-29. General covering classification remains
  `deferred(the planned topology page owns it)`.
- Ch. 22, “Symplectic Tensors,” “Symplectic Structures on Manifolds,” “The
  Darboux Theorem,” and “Hamiltonian Vector Fields” → DG-35/DG-36, including
  `already-published(thm-alternating-forms-have-a-symplectic-normal-form)`,
  `included(def-symplectic-form-and-symplectic-manifold)`, `included(thm-darboux-theorem)`, and
  `included(def-hamiltonian-vector-field-and-hamiltonian-function)`.
- Appendix D, “Existence, Uniqueness, and Smoothness” and named Theorems D.1
  Fundamental Theorem for Autonomous ODEs, D.2 ODE Comparison, D.3
  Existence, D.4 Uniqueness, D.5 Smoothness, and D.6 Fundamental Theorem for
  Nonautonomous ODEs → DG-8, respectively
  `included(thm-fundamental-theorem-for-autonomous-smooth-odes)`
  and its decomposed Picard, comparison, uniqueness, parameter-dependence,
  and nonautonomous items.

### 8.2 Independent smooth-manifold lecture-note harvest

**Merry, *Differential Geometry*. Range read:** Lectures 1–9, 14–28, and 37,
with complete contents and focused proofs in 15, 25–28.

- Lectures 1 “Smooth manifolds,” 2 “Tangent Spaces,” 3 “Partitions of Unity,”
  4 “The Derivative,” 5 “The Tangent Bundle,” 6 “Submanifolds,” and 7
  “The Whitney Theorems” → DG-1–DG-7, with exact controls
  `included(def-smooth-manifold)`,
  `included(def-derivation-at-a-point-and-tangent-space)`,
  `included(thm-smooth-partitions-of-unity-exist-on-manifolds)`,
  `included(thm-constant-rank-theorem-for-manifolds)`, and
  `included(thm-weak-whitney-proper-embedding-theorem)`.
- Lectures 8 “Vector Fields” and 9 “Flows” → DG-9, including
  `included(thm-fundamental-theorem-on-flows)`; Euclidean existence is backed
  separately by DG-8 rather than assumed.
- Lectures 14 “Distributions and Integrability” and 15 “Foliations and the
  Frobenius Theorem” →
  `included(thm-frobenius-local-coordinate-theorem)` and
  `included(thm-existence-and-uniqueness-of-maximal-connected-integral-manifolds)`.
- Lectures 16 “Bundles,” 17 “The Fibre Bundle Construction Theorem,” and 18
  “Associated Bundles” → `included(def-smooth-fibre-bundle-and-local-trivialization)`,
  `included(thm-vector-bundle-construction-from-a-smooth-cocycle)`, and DG-26 associated-
  bundle items. General classifying spaces →
  `deferred(characteristic-class/algebraic-topology machinery is absent)`.
- Lectures 19 “Tensor and Exterior Algebras,” 20 “Sections of Vector
  Bundles,” 21 “Tensor Fields,” 22 “The Lie Derivative Revisited,” and 23
  “The Exterior Differential” → DG-11/DG-12, including
  `included(def-wedge-product-of-alternating-covectors)` and
  `included(thm-cartans-magic-formula)`.
- Lecture 24 “Orientations and Manifolds With Boundary” → DG-13;
  `included(prop-boundary-orientation-is-independent-of-the-outward-vector-field)`.
- Lecture 25 “Smooth Singular Cubes” →
  `deferred(the selected singular-simplex route avoids importing a full

- Lecture 27 “The Poincaré Lemma and the de Rham Theorem,” named homotopy operator,

- Lecture 28 “Connections” and Lecture 37 “Riemannian Vector Bundles” →
  DG-19, including `included(def-connection-on-a-smooth-vector-bundle)` and
  the induced bundle-connection items; later holonomy/characteristic-form
  theory is `deferred(the advanced connections/Chern–Weil block denied in §2)`.

**Hitchin, *Differentiable Manifolds*. Range read:** §§2–7 and Appendix
§§10.1–10.5.

- §2 “Manifolds” (coordinate charts, compatibility, manifolds, examples) →

  `included(def-smoothly-compatible-charts)`.
- §3 “Tangent and cotangent vectors” (smooth partitions, submanifolds,

**Nicolaescu, *Lectures on the Geometry of Manifolds*, 3rd ed. Range read:**

**Robbin–Salamon, *Introduction to Differential Topology*. Range read:** Ch.
1 §§1.1–1.3; Ch. 5 §§5.1–5.3; Ch. 6 §§6.1–6.5; Ch. 7 §7.1; Appendix A
§§A.1–A.3.

- §1.1 “Smooth Manifolds and Smooth Maps,” all five subheadings through

- §5.1 “Exterior Algebra” (alternating forms, exterior product and pullback,

  all three subheadings → DG-12/DG-14; named Stokes →
  `included(thm-general-stokes-theorem)`.
- §5.3 “Lie Derivative”: “Cartan's Formula” →
  `included(thm-cartans-magic-formula)`; “Integration and Exactness” →
  `integration-and-stokes-examples`
  and DG-17 top compact-support items.
- §6.1 “Poincare Lemma,” including homotopy operator/invariance → DG-15.
  §6.2 “Mayer–Vietoris Sequence”: “Long Exact Sequences” →
  `deferred(HA-4 owns the general long-exact-sequence machinery but is
  scaffolded and unauthored; DG-15 consumes it conditionally)`; “Finite Good
  Covers” → `inline(thm-de-rham-theorem)`;
  “Kunneth Formula” → `deferred(the algebraic-topology product theorem is a
  separate page)`. §6.3 compactly supported forms → DG-17; §6.4 Poincaré
  duality → `deferred(a full duality/Thom theory is absent)`; all §6.5 Čech
  headings → `out-of-scope(the singular route is selected and no sheaf/Čech
  machinery exists)`.
- §7.1 “Vector Bundles,” all five subheadings → DG-5, with orientation to
  DG-13. Appendix A §§A.1 “Paracompactness,” A.2 “Partitions of Unity,” and
  A.3 “Embedding a Manifold into Euclidean Space” → DG-1, DG-2, DG-7.

**Gualtieri and Lang focused controls.** Gualtieri, *Topology I*, Parts 6
“Transversality,” 8 “Sard's theorem,” and 11 “Partitions of unity, Whitney
embedding and approximation” → DG-6/DG-7, including
`included(thm-parametric-transversality)` and the weak Whitney
embedding proof. Lang, *Differential Geometry I*, §§9.1 “Null sets” and 9.2
“Sard's theorem” →
`included(thm-morse-sard-for-euclidean-maps)`.
Gualtieri's displayed finite-regularity inequality differs typographically
from Lang/Lee; Lang's exact \(r>\max(m-n,0)\) statement governs. Gualtieri
parts outside those ranges are `out-of-scope(not inspected and not cited)`;
Lang's later geometry is `out-of-scope(the focused source was used only to
settle the sharp Sard regularity convention)`.

### 8.3 Singular and de Rham comparison sources

**Hatcher, *Algebraic Topology*. Range read:** §2.1 from “Singular Homology”
through “Exact Sequences and Excision” (pp. 108–132); the “Mayer–Vietoris
Sequences” portion of §2.2 (pp. 149–150); §3.1 “Cohomology of Spaces” and its
Mayer–Vietoris sequence (pp. 197–204).

- “Singular Homology” →
  `included(def-continuous-singular-simplex-and-real-singular-chain-group)`,
  with boundary and boundary-squared-zero `already-planned(AT-2)`.
- “Homotopy Invariance,” named Thm. 2.10 and Prop. 2.12 →
  `already-planned(AT-2: def-prism-operator-for-a-homotopy,
  thm-singular-chain-homotopy-formula)`; DG retains only the real-coefficient
  consequence and smooth comparison.
- “Exact Sequences and Excision,” specifically barycentric subdivision,
  cover-small chains, and named Prop. 2.21 → `already-planned(AT-3)`; DG-16
  items 7–11 specialise the two-open real-cochain interface.
  General relative homology and excision →
  `already-planned(AT-3 owns the full relative/excision theory)`;
  “The Equivalence of Simplicial and Singular Homology” →
  `out-of-scope(no simplicial-homology construction is needed for de Rham)`.
- §2.2 “Mayer–Vietoris Sequences” and its short exact chain sequence →
  `included(thm-short-exact-two-open-cover-small-singular-chain-sequence)`
  and `included(thm-mayer-vietoris-sequence-in-real-singular-cohomology)`.
- §3.1 “Cohomology of Spaces” and “Mayer–Vietoris Sequences” →
  `included(def-real-singular-cochain-complex)` and DG-16 items 8–11, citing
  AT-6's cohomology functoriality and Mayer--Vietoris interfaces.
  “The Universal Coefficient Theorem” →
  `deferred(it is unnecessary because real singular cohomology is defined
  directly from cochains and would add Ext/injectivity machinery)`.

**Arvind Nair, *Topology II*. Range read:** §6.13 “Singular cohomology and
sheaf cohomology” (pp. 83–85; TOC lists p. 84).

- The recalled small-chain statement in the proof of Thm. 6.13.3 →
  `already-planned(AT-3: thm-cover-small-inclusion-is-a-chain-homotopy-equivalence)`.
- Lemmas 6.13.1–6.13.2, the sheaf-comparison conclusion of Thm. 6.13.3,
  Remark 6.13.4, and Cor. 6.13.5 →
  `out-of-scope(the selected proof does not construct sheaves or sheaf
  cohomology)`.

**Park, “Proof of de Rham's Theorem.” Range read:** the complete paper,
§§1–4, headed “Introduction,” “Smooth Singular Homology,” “De Rham Map,” and
“Example: deRham Cohomology of \(S^n\).”

- §1 “Introduction” → `inline(thm-de-rham-theorem)` as proof architecture.
- §2 “Smooth Singular Homology,” named Lem. 2.1 boundary-face gluing, Thm.
  2.2 Whitney approximation, and Thm. 2.4 smooth-versus-singular homology →
  `included(lem-relative-smoothing-of-a-continuous-simplex-along-its-faces)`
  and `included(thm-smooth-singular-chains-compute-singular-homology)`.
- §3 “De Rham Map,” named Thm. 3.1 chain Stokes, Prop. 3.2 naturality and
  connector compatibility, and Thm. 3.3 de Rham →
  `included(thm-stokes-theorem-for-smooth-singular-chains)`,
  `included(lem-the-de-rham-map-commutes-with-mayer-vietoris-connectors)`,
  and `included(thm-de-rham-theorem)`. Park is a focused proof guide, not the
  sole primary.
- §4 sphere example → `inline(thm-de-rham-cohomology-of-spheres)` on DG-15.

**van der Vorst, *De Rham Cohomology*. Range read:** §§6–10.

- §6 “Differential forms,” headings on contractions, exterior derivative,
  and top-form integration → DG-11, DG-12, DG-14, including
  `included(thm-cartans-magic-formula)`.
- §7 “Chains and cohomologies,” “chains,” “cochains,” and “useful lemmas” →
  AT-2/AT-6 for ordinary singular objects and DG-16 for their real/smooth
  comparison; the generic complex/quotient algebra is
  `deferred(HA-1 owns general complexes and cohomology but is scaffolded and
  unauthored)`.
- §8 “deRham cohomology,” headings definition, homotopy invariance, and
  Mayer–Vietoris → DG-15. §9 computations, including named sphere, punctured
  Euclidean-space, and top-cohomology results →
  `de-rham-cohomology-computations` and DG-17, including
  `included(thm-integration-is-an-isomorphism-on-top-compactly-supported-de-rham-cohomology)`.
- §10 “deRham theorem,” headings “singular homology,” “singular cohomology,”
  “smooth simplices,” “de Rham homomorphism,” and “de Rham theorem” →
  DG-16/DG-17. Named Whitney approximation, chain Stokes, naturality, convex
  case, disjoint-union/union lemmas, Bredon open-set principle, and theorem →
  respectively DG-16 item 17,
  `included(lem-de-rham-and-singular-cohomology-respect-countable-disjoint-unions)`,
  DG-16 item 22, and DG-17 items 5, 9–15, including
  `included(thm-de-rham-theorem)`. Small expository slips
  mean this source is a cross-check; Lee/Hatcher/Park govern the exact
  statement.

The selected route therefore specialises AT's definition
\(H^*(X;\mathbb R)=H^*(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb R}(C_*(X;\mathbb R),
\mathbb R))\), consumes AT-3's cover-small chains, and proves the
comparison. It does not define cohomology as
\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_*(X),\mathbb R)\), does not invoke injectivity of
\(\mathbb R\), and does not select one global smoothing for every simplex.

### 8.4 Riemannian and comparison-geometry harvest

**Lee, *Riemannian Manifolds: An Introduction to Curvature* (1997). Range read:** Chs.
3–11, with all chapter headings and the named results listed here.

- Ch. 3 “Definitions and Examples of Riemannian Metrics”: “Riemannian
  Metrics,” “Elementary Constructions Associated with Riemannian Metrics,”
  and “The Model Spaces of Riemannian Geometry” → DG-18 and
  `riemannian-metric-and-volume-examples`, including
  `included(thm-every-smooth-manifold-admits-a-riemannian-metric)` and model
  Euclidean, sphere, hyperbolic, product and submanifold metrics.
- Ch. 4 “Connections”: “Connections,” “Vector Fields Along Curves,” and
  “Geodesics” → DG-19/DG-20, including named Thm. 4.11 Parallel Translation,
  Thm. 4.12 Existence and Uniqueness for Linear ODEs, and Thm. 4.10
  Existence and Uniqueness of Geodesics:
  `included(thm-existence-and-uniqueness-of-parallel-sections)` and
  `included(thm-existence-uniqueness-and-smooth-dependence-of-geodesics)`.
- Ch. 5 “Riemannian Geodesics”: “The Riemannian Connection,” “The
  Exponential Map,” and “Normal Neighborhoods and Normal Coordinates” →
  `included(thm-fundamental-theorem-of-riemannian-geometry)`,
  `included(def-domain-and-exponential-map-of-a-connection)`, and
  `included(thm-existence-of-normal-neighborhoods)`. Named Thm. 5.4
  Fundamental Lemma of Riemannian Geometry is the first of these.
- Ch. 6 “Lengths and Distances on Riemannian Manifolds,” “Geodesics and
  Minimizing Curves,” and “Completeness” → DG-18/DG-20. Named Prop. 6.5
  First Variation, Thm. 6.8 Gauss Lemma, Thm. 6.13 Hopf–Rinow, and Cors.
  6.14–6.16 → `included(thm-first-variation-formula-for-energy)`,
  `included(thm-gauss-lemma)`, `included(thm-hopf-rinow)`, and its compact-
  ball/minimizer/completeness corollaries.
- Ch. 7 “Curvature”: “Local Invariants,” “Flat Manifolds,” “Symmetries of

  `included(thm-algebraic-symmetries-of-the-riemann-tensor)`,

  `included(def-ricci-curvature)`, and `included(def-scalar-curvature)`.

  `included(thm-weingarten-equation-and-adjointness-of-the-shape-operator)`.
- Ch. 9 “The Gauss–Bonnet Theorem”: “Some Plane Geometry,” “The

  Problem 9-5's finite geodesic-triangulation result is promoted and proved

- Ch. 10 “Jacobi Fields”: “The Jacobi Equation,” “Computations of Jacobi

- Ch. 11 “Curvature and Topology”: “Some Comparison Theorems,” “Manifolds of

- Chs. 11–14: §§11.1 “Riemann curvature endomorphism,” 11.2 “Flat

- Chs. 21–23: §§21.1 second variation, 21.2 index form; §§22.1 proof

- Chs. 34–36, the convexity and soul-theorem machinery →

- Ch. 40, Ricci-flat structure →

- Chs. 41–45, rigidity and Cheeger–Colding–Naber limit-space headings →

### 8.5 Lie groups and Lie-algebra structure harvest

## 9. Amendments owed to other scaffolds

This section began as requests for the orchestrator or owning future build
lane. Entries marked **Applied** are now reconciliation receipts; unmarked
entries remain requests.

### 9.1 Published multivariable calculus

**Applied 2026-08-14.** RA-36 now scaffolds the open invertible-derivative
locus, local-inverse derivative, parameterised implicit function theorem, and
local/global counterexample remark requested below.

The authored page `inverse-and-implicit-function-theorems` has exactly five
items: two definitions, the Newton-contraction lemma,
`thm-euclidean-inverse-function-theorem`, and
`thm-euclidean-implicit-function-theorem`. Those two theorems are sufficient
for DG-4's coordinate proofs, so no load-bearing gap is hidden. Its next
enrichment cycle nevertheless owes the useful inexpensive consequences that
the differential-geometry sources repeatedly use: openness of the invertible-
derivative locus, the derivative formula for a local inverse as a separately
searchable corollary, a parameterized implicit-function formulation, and
examples/false statements distinguishing local from global invertibility.
The Euclidean constant-rank theorem is **not** owed there: DG-4 derives and
owns it in the manifold rank-theorem block.

### 9.2 Homological algebra

1. **Owner-authorised and applied 2026-08-14.** Repoint this track's placement anchor from the empty planned companion
   `spectral-sequences-examples` to the finished scaffold's final companion
   `grothendieck-spectral-sequences-and-computations-examples` when HA-17 is
   spliced.
2. Give DG-15–DG-17 the stable ids from HA-1
   `chain-complexes-and-homology`, HA-2
   `chain-homotopy-and-the-homotopy-category`, and HA-4
   `long-exact-sequences-in-homology`. The required payload is cochain
   complexes/cohomology, induced cochain maps, short-exact-complex connecting
   maps, natural long exact sequences, and the five lemma. Differential
   geometry supplies the de Rham and singular complexes and verifies every
   geometric differential.
3. Preserve HA's generality: DG-16's cover-small singular subcomplex,
   subdivision/prism homotopy, and simplex-basis extension-by-zero are
   geometric input, not requests for HA to mint singular theory.
4. DG-29 supplies the Chevalley–Eilenberg differential and proves its square
   is zero; HA supplies only the already-general cohomology/LES machinery.
   Lie-algebra cohomology must remain on this track, as the HA scaffold's own
   scope denial requires.

### 9.3 Topology

The live page `covering-spaces-and-lifting` has an empty `items` array. Its
future build owes stable definitions and the existence/uniqueness of path and
homotopy lifts, deck transformations, universal covers under the printed
local path-connectedness/semilocal simple-connectedness hypotheses, and the
classification needed by DG-20, DG-23, DG-26, DG-29, and DG-34. Until then,
every covering conclusion in those pairs is a forward reference, not a
published fact. The existing published manifold-useful topology—Hausdorff and
second-countable spaces, local compactness, paracompactness, locally finite
refinements, partitions of unity, metrization, compactness, quotient topology,
and homotopy—is consumed exactly as listed in §1 and must not be re-minted.

The topology partition page should also retain its own stated choice ledger
when DG-2 converts a locally finite topological partition into a smooth one;
the splice must not relabel that construction ZF merely because the smoothing
steps themselves are explicit.

**Applied 2026-08-14.** The topology scaffolds now expose the covering,
lifting, deck, universal-cover and classification ids with the printed
hypotheses, and preserve the partition-of-unity choice ledger through
smoothing.

### 9.4 Abstract and linear algebra

The planned `tensor-products-of-modules` page has no authored items. Before
DG-27 is built, its owning abstract-algebra cycle must provide stable ids for
the tensor algebra, quotient-by-relations universal property, functoriality,
and tensoring algebra maps. PBW and the universal enveloping algebra cite that
construction. DG-11 does **not** wait for it: in finite dimension it defines
alternating covectors directly as subspaces of published multilinear forms,
constructs wedge by the explicit alternation formula, and proves the needed
universal property locally.

**Applied 2026-08-14.** MOD-3 now exposes the requested tensor-algebra,
functoriality, quotient-relations and tensoring-algebra-map ids.

**R-5 applied 2026-08-14.** DG-30 cites X-2's stable finite-dimensional
Jordan--Chevalley theorem and no longer carries a local operator fallback.
Clifford algebras and spin representations are not requested from
abstract algebra by this scaffold; the only spin example is the already
reachable covering $SU(2)\to SO(3)$.

### 9.5 Measure theory

1. **R-9 applied 2026-08-14.** MT-11 owns the unconditional
   $C^1$-diffeomorphism change-of-variables theorem for the **Lebesgue**
   integral. DG cites it and does not mint it. DG-14's Stokes proof does not
   need it; DG-18's arbitrary
   measurable density/Radon-volume extension does.
2. MT-20's Radon/Riesz–Markov–Kakutani page is consumed by DG-18's Radon
   Riemannian volume statement and DG-33's Haar-measure construction. DG-33,
   not measure theory, supplies the topological-group invariance argument.
3. MT-22's Poincaré-recurrence page is cited only by DG-36's recurrence
   corollary. It is a leaf and no Hamiltonian construction depends on it.
4. **Applied at scaffold level 2026-08-14.** The splice must preserve the distinction between the published
   Riemann/Jordan integral used for compactly supported smooth top forms and
   the future Lebesgue integral used for $L^1$ densities. “Integration on a
   manifold” is not one untyped imported operation.

### 9.6 Functional analysis, PDE, and future geometric sequels

DG-33's Peter–Weyl block needs the future functional-analysis pages supplying
Hilbert-space orthogonal projection and orthonormal-basis machinery, compact
self-adjoint/Hilbert–Schmidt spectral theory, and density/closure arguments.
The compact Lie group, Haar measure, convolution kernel, and finite-rank
isotypic constructions remain here; functional analysis must not duplicate
compact Lie theory.

The PDE track owns elliptic regularity and the Euclidean theory of harmonic
functions. A future Hodge page should cite DG-12/DG-15/DG-17 for $d$, de
Rham cohomology, and the de Rham theorem; DG-18/DG-19 for the metric, volume,
and connection; and then add the codifferential, Hodge star analytic theory,
ellipticity, harmonic representatives, and Hodge decomposition. Nothing in
this scaffold asserts those analytic theorems in advance.

Future dedicated sequels, rather than amendments to current tracks, are the
proper homes for Chern–Weil/characteristic classes, complex and Kähler
manifolds, Morse theory, nonfree/stratified symplectic reduction, and
toric/equivariant symplectic geometry.

---

## 10. Seam reconciliation and unresolved forward references

### 10.1 Seams consumed

- **Published topology:** the pages and ids in §1 supply quotient topology,
  compactness, countability, paracompactness/partitions, metrization, and
  homotopy. DG-1–DG-2 prove only the smooth refinements.
- **Published calculus:** total derivatives, chain rule, higher smoothness,
  Euclidean inverse/implicit theorems, Riemann/Jordan integration, Fubini,
  compact-support change of variables, line integrals, and the Euclidean
  star-shaped Poincaré lemma are cited, not reconstructed.
- **Homological algebra:** the completed HA scaffold is consumed for general
  cochain-complex, cohomology, homotopy, LES, and five-lemma machinery;
  stable item ids await its future build.
- **Measure theory:** the finished MT scaffold is consumed for Lebesgue/Radon
  density integration, Riesz–Markov–Kakutani, and Poincaré recurrence, with
  the exact boundaries in §9.5.
- **Algebra and analysis:** published finite-dimensional multilinear algebra,
  groups, modules, quotients, completeness, Banach fixed point, and linear ODE
  prerequisites are consumed. The planned tensor and functional-analysis
  pages remain explicit forward seams.

### 10.2 Unresolved forward references

| Consumer | Unresolved page or stable-id seam | Consequence until authored |
|---|---|---|
| track placement | HA-17 companion `grothendieck-spectral-sequences-and-computations-examples` | orchestrator must replace the older empty `spectral-sequences-examples` anchor |
| DG-15–DG-17 | HA-1, HA-2, HA-4 stable cochain/cohomology/LES ids | de Rham algebraic arguments cannot be built before HA is authored |
| DG-20, DG-23, DG-26, DG-29, DG-34 | `covering-spaces-and-lifting` | covering local isometries, Cartan–Hadamard's covering conclusion, universal covering Lie groups, and Lie II/III global forms remain conditional |
| DG-27 onward | `tensor-products-of-modules` | tensor algebra, universal enveloping algebra, PBW, and representation-theory consumers wait for the abstract-algebra tensor construction |
| DG-18, DG-22, DG-23, DG-33 | MT-11/MT-20 stable ids | measurable/Radon Riemannian volume, cut-locus nullity and polar integration, Bishop–Gromov measure statements, and Haar integration wait for the measure build |
| DG-36 | MT-22 stable recurrence ids | only the Poincaré-recurrence corollary waits; Hamiltonian mechanics does not |
| DG-33 | future Hilbert geometry, orthonormal-basis, and compact-operator pages | Peter–Weyl and its representation-density corollaries wait; finite-dimensional compact Lie structure does not |
| future Hodge sequel | PDE/functional-analysis elliptic machinery | Hodge decomposition and analytic Laplace–Beltrami theory are deliberately absent |

These are splice/build dependencies, not blockers to the present prose
scaffold. DG-8 closes the previously missing ODE seam locally. DG-16 closes
the exact singular-cochain and smoothing seam needed for de Rham without
assuming a nonexistent algebraic-topology track. DG-30 closes the finite-
dimensional Jordan–Chevalley seam by citing X-2 under R-5; no local fallback
remains.

### 10.3 Source-stated, non-load-bearing boundaries

The strong Whitney (2n) embedding statement is retained as an honestly cited
`[AN]` theorem statement; the proved weak proper (2n+1) theorem is the one
available to dependencies. Myers–Steenrod is named but not used because its
regularity proof is not built. Noncompact real-group global Cartan/Iwasawa
statements print their connectedness, linearity, and finite-center hypotheses;
they are not silently extended to arbitrary Lie groups. Peter–Weyl is
load-bearing only after its measure/functional-analysis seams resolve.

### 10.4 Scope denials carried forward

The deliberate denials of §2 remain unresolved by design: Hodge/elliptic
theory; pseudo-, Finsler, and sub-Riemannian geometry; geometric flows and
minimal-surface theory; higher Chern–Gauss–Bonnet and Chern–Weil theory;
noncompact reductive representation theory; infinite-dimensional and
Kac–Moody/super/quantum Lie theory; general Poisson/contact/complex/Kähler
geometry; and Floer, gauge, toric, equivariant-cohomological, singular-
reduction, and stratified-symplectic theories. The canonical harvest adds the
specific denied prerequisites: surface classification belongs to topology;
Maslov theory needs the topology of the Lagrangian Grassmannian; constrained
mechanics needs a singular Legendre/constraint theory; Myers–Steenrod needs a
regularity argument; higher global Riemannian finiteness/sphere theorems need
Morse/index and geometric-analysis machinery; and orbifold reduction needs a
new quotient category. None is needed by an included theorem.

### 10.5 Blockers

**None.** The source texts and full lecture-note sets required for the
commission were reachable. Every unresolved entry above is a future authored-
page/stable-id dependency already owned by another track or an explicitly
denied sequel; no permission or external-authority request was made.
